## 0. Summary

Row A asks for one theory on one carrier containing correspondences, their
composition, a transpose, a diagonal trace, numerical equivalence,
effectivity and Riemann–Roch, carrying the structural burden of the
corresponding objects in Weil's proof for curves over finite fields, and
using no arithmetic selection: no von Mangoldt function, no distinguished
arithmetic family, no explicit formula, no zero of zeta, no positivity.

The paper constructs that theory on the **graph sector**, the stable category
generated by the graph bimodules of external idele classes acting on the
semilocal source of phase 123.  Three results carry it.

1. **The dagger.**  The recorded obstruction `Hom(U,N)=0` forbids
   self-duality of localized ideals, not of the diagonal.  On the graph
   sector the transpose is the half-density Hilbert adjoint, canonical by the
   product formula, contravariant and involutive (Section 5).
2. **Additive invariants are constant or divergent.**  At a finite place a
   non-unit twist contributes only the origin class, independent of the
   twist; a unit twist contributes one orbit-space copy per shell, of
   infinite total rank (Theorem 7.1(c), Appendix D).  In no case is there a
   finite rank, Euler characteristic or determinant separating classes, so no
   additive numerical theory is Weil-strength (Corollary 7.5).  Every earlier
   failure to produce a mixed sector in phase 123 is an instance.
3. **The analytic trace invariant.**  **[REBUILT, phase 129.]**  The
   invariant that separates them is the half-density-normalized relative trace
   of the dilation, defined by the declared cutoff regularization of
   Definition 8.6 on the declared trace-class domain `Corr_A^tr` of
   Definition 8A.1, with target the based determinant lines `T_A = Pic_b(C)`.
   Its local density at a finite fixed point is the Fourier orbital integral
   `τ_p(σ)=|σ|_p^{1/2}/|1−σ|_p` — the Haar-regularized orbit count at a unit
   twist, `p^{−|k|/2}` at a twist of valuation `k≠0`, one formula
   (Lemma 8.2, Appendix D.4).  It is additive, cyclic, `†`-real,
   restriction-compatible and a genuine Fredholm determinant (§8A), and it
   separates sections of equal modulus profile by an exact,
   regularization-free computation, so `N_A ≠ 0` (Theorem 9.5).  What it is
   **not** shown to be is a Riemann–Roch determinant (§10); it is not
   chart-invariant, and the defect is computed exactly (8A.12); and its
   identity-class constant is not pinned (Remark 8.6b).
   **[REBUILT, phase 129, round 3:]** its **symbol** is computed explicitly
   (Theorem 9.7) and is nonzero off a discrete set on every component of the
   dual group, whence the numerical radical **vanishes** (Theorem 9.8) and the
   Tate ruling plane appears as the canonical boundary quotient of one exact
   sequence (Theorem 9.12).
4. **The rulings live in the completion, and the Riemann–Roch determinant is
   vector-valued.**  **[REBUILT, phase 130.]**  The three sentences struck
   through in item 3 — "not shown to be a Riemann–Roch determinant", "not
   chart-invariant", "constant not pinned" — are all repaired in phase 130,
   and the repair is one idea in three parts.  (i) The two rulings are
   **limits of spread Tate characters**: `f_n(t) = n^{−1}e^{−t/2}ψ(t/n)` has
   `λ_+ = 1`, `λ_− → 0` and `\sup|\hat f_n| → 0`, so `J(f_n) → (0,e_+)` in the
   completion; the completion then splits canonically as
   `primitive ⊕ rulings` (Theorems 9.14a–b), and the ruling plane is exactly
   the boundary radical of the scalar form (Theorem 9.14c).  (ii) Every local
   symbol is a **Mittag–Leffler sum over the Tate points with the universal
   residue `∓i`**, periodized with period `2π/\log p` at a finite place
   (Theorem 9.7b); at a finite chart the scalar symbol therefore carries
   `|S_f|+1` copies of the Tate pole, and no scalar form can be the ruling
   form (Theorem 10.8).  (iii) The determinant lives on a **Banach `†`-algebra
   `𝓐_tr`** built from the symbol, where its germ is holomorphic and exactly
   multiplicative for all valuation shells and the archimedean place at once
   (Theorem 8A.7b).  The Riemann–Roch determinant is then
   `λ_{RR} = (L_S, λ_{per}∘M)` with values in `T_A = Pic_b(B_T)`, it extends
   the intrinsic periodic ruling determinant, and its equality formula is
   Weil's `D·D' = P·P' + d_1d_2' + d_2d_1'` (Theorem 10.7).  The trace becomes
   chart-invariant on the colimit chart (Theorem 8A.7c) and the constant is
   declared, `c := 0` (Definition 8A.7d).

Section 1 explains why the theory has this shape and what the new insight
is.  Section 13 records the routes tried and withdrawn — three false
intermediate statements, one ill-posed definition, one false impossibility
claim, and (phase 130) five withdrawn formulations including the round-4
statement of what clause A.8 required — because the corpus's discipline
requires that refuted routes remain visible.  Section 14 is the
clause-by-clause ledger.  **[REBUILT, phase 130:]** after this phase all ten
clauses are `PROVED` on the declared carrier (A.9 as "passed for the
formalism"), every named obstacle is closed, and the residual items are
listed explicitly: the label `WEIL-STRENGTH-COMPLETE` was granted by the
adversarial audit of record (`R130.005`, §14.0b), not by the construction; clause A.1 is
`PROVED` under the clause's own words and `PARTIAL` under a stack-theoretic
reading of "descent"; the sector is the invertible one; `𝓝_∞ = 0` for the
colimit is deliberately not claimed; and no Hodge-index sign is asserted
anywhere.

## 1. Why row A is built this way, and what the insight is

### 1.1 The classical model and the meaning of "structural burden"

In Weil's proof for a curve `C` over `F_q`, the objects are correspondences
on `C×C`: divisor classes, with composition by pull–tensor–push over `C^3`,
a transpose (factor swap), and intersection numbers.  The two rulings
`C×{pt}`, `{pt}×C` span a hyperbolic plane with matrix `[[0,1],[1,0]]`.  The
graph `Γ_F` of Frobenius is a correspondence whose intersection numbers
`Γ_{F^k}·Δ = #C(F_{q^k}) = q^k + 1 − Σ α_i^k` encode the zeta function: the
first two terms are the ruling contribution, depending only on the "module"
`q^k`; the remaining sum is the **mixed** part, and it is everything.  The
Hodge index theorem on the numerical group then gives the sign.

"Weil-strength" for row A is defined (specification §1) as equality of
structural burden: the same operations must exist in one theory on one
carrier.  It does not mean the carrier is a surface or that numerical rank is
finite; the specification proves finite rank is inadmissible and asks for a
separated infinite-rank topological numerical theory.

### 1.2 The three obstacles that shaped the construction

The phase-123 corpus built the source `U` (a nuclear crossed product of a
coefficient algebra by the lattice of positive `S`-units), the twisted
bimodules `E_a` of external idele classes, their composition and a cyclic
shadow, and then met three obstructions, each recorded as a theorem:

* **(O1)** `Hom_{U-bimod}(U,N)=0` for the nonunital coefficient ideal `N`
  (`A_DUALITY_WORK`): no degree-zero fully faithful duality can fix both the
  unit and the ideals.  Read as an obstruction to any transpose.
* **(O2)** The twist-only sector "did not produce the geometrically mixed
  sector required by A.2, A.6 and A.8" (P123.082); the degree-zero shadow of
  a twist is `D` by evaluation at the origin, independent of the twist
  (P123.084); the unit's shadow is infinite, never perfect (P123.070).  Read
  as: the graph sector is too small, add non-invertible objects.
* **(O3)** "Complexification still kills the finite torsion" (P123.037–038):
  the arithmetic contact complexes `[Z →n Z]` become acyclic over `C`, and an
  acyclic complex "does not remember its arithmetic degree alone".  Yet
  P123.039 (1.1) also records `det(1−T|V_n) = Φ_n(1)` for the complexified,
  acyclic `D_n`, "obtained not by assigning the zero supertrace a nonzero
  value".

Each obstruction is correct as a theorem.  Each was read as blocking a route
that the present paper shows is not the route.

### 1.3 The insight

The three obstructions are one phenomenon.  Over `C`, the Hochschild shadow
of a twisted bimodule localizes at the fixed points of the dilation
(Section 7).  At a finite place the fixed point is the origin `x_p=0`, and
the coefficient algebra there is totally disconnected: the fixed point has no
tangent direction, so its Hochschild contribution is a **rank-one class with
no transverse data**, the same for every twist (this is P123.084).  The
arithmetic content of the fixed point — the number `1/|1−σ_p|_p` that the
local Fourier trace of the dilation produces (P123.042–060) — is not visible
to the complex.  It lives in the **normalization** of the rank-one class by
the half-density and the Haar measure, i.e. in the analytic trace of the
dilation on it, not in its rank.

In the smooth polynomial model `R[z]` the same fixed point has a tangent
direction and its Hochschild contribution is the Koszul complex
`[R[z] →(σ−1)z R[z]]`, acyclic over `C` but with **torsion** `|σ−1|`
(P123.060 (1.1)).  P123.060 §4 proves the two computations agree: "the
actual normal graph calculation identifies exactly what the local Fourier
trace sees transversely: the fixed differential `1−n`, together with the
dilation half-density".  So the analytic trace at a totally disconnected
fixed point is the torsion of its smooth model.  That is why (O3) is not an
obstruction: complexification kills cohomology, not determinant; the number
`Φ_n(1)` in P123.039 is exactly such a torsion.

Consequences, each proved below:

* **Additive invariants are blind.**  On the arithmetic family the shadow's
  only finite-place content is the origin class, the same for every `n`; on
  unit twists its rank is infinite (Appendix D).  No finite additive
  invariant separates classes.  This is (O2) understood: the graph sector was
  never too small; the invariant was too coarse.
* **The analytic trace separates.**  The relative trace differs on that pair
  by `(v_p(1−u) − v_p(1−u')) log p` exactly, with every other lattice term
  cancelling (Theorem 9.5).  That difference is the mixed sector.
* **The arithmetic family is seen.**  Row B's `Γ_n` is the invertible twist
  `E_{a_S(n)}` (P123.038), inside the graph sector, and the analytic trace is
  nonzero on it (§11bis.3).  **[REBUILT, phase 129:]** the further claim
  "nothing non-invertible is needed in A" is withdrawn as a non sequitur; see
  §11bis.  The
  non-invertibility of B lives in the contact, i.e. in the smooth-model
  Koszul differential `n` versus `n−1` (P123.060 §3), and that comparison is
  B's first theorem (Section 14).
* **The transpose is canonical.**  (O1) forbids self-dual ideals, which the
  graph sector does not contain; on it the transpose is the half-density
  Hilbert adjoint, and the half-density `|·|_S^{−1/2}` is a square root of
  the module fixed by the product formula — the arithmetic theta
  characteristic, with no two-torsion ambiguity (Section 5).
* **The composition anomaly is not a defect.**  A determinant that composes
  up to a cocycle is a biextension, which is what clause A.8 asks for
  (Section 10).

The construction is therefore: graph sector, half-density dagger, full cyclic
shadow localized at fixed points, and the analytic relative trace of the
corpus (P123.042–059, P123.105–110) as the numerical invariant.  Everything
else — excision, numerical quotient, effectivity, Riemann–Roch — follows from
those four choices with proofs that are short once the choices are right.

## 2. Standing conventions and the source

### 2.1 Status labels

`PROVED-IN-REPOSITORY` (complete proof with domains, here or in a cited
note), `IMPORTED` (exact external theorem, hypotheses checked),
`CONSTRUCTED`, `CONDITIONAL`, `CANDIDATE`, `REFUTED`.  No arrow in the
closure is unlabelled.

### 2.2 The carrier

Fix a finite set `S` of places with `∞∈S`, `S_f≠∅`; `A_S` the semilocal
adelic space, `Γ_S` the positive `S`-units.  The carrier is

    X_S = [A_S / Γ_S],                                          (2.1)

with square `Y_S = X_S × X_S`, noncollapsed: its two projections are
different anchor functors and a graph kernel records both actions
(P123.016 §7).

### 2.3 Coefficients and the source

At a finite place, `F_p` is the nuclear Fréchet algebra of functions on `Q_p`
with rapidly decaying shell–character Fourier coefficients,

    v_{l,N}(f) = Σ_{j,κ} p^{l|j|} c_p(κ)^N |f_{j,κ}| < ∞,           (2.2)

where `j∈Z` indexes the valuation shell `p^j Z_p^×` and `κ` the characters of
`Z_p^×` with conductor `c_p(κ)`; and `E_p = C·χ_p ⊕ F_p`, `χ_p=1_{Z_p}`, the
algebra "unitized at the zero coordinate" (P123.121 (1)–(2)).  Elements of
`F_p` vanish to infinite order at `x_p=0` and at `x_p→∞`; `χ_p` carries the
value at `0`.  At the real place, `E_R` is the algebra of functions of the
log-coordinate `t=log x_∞` on `R_{>0}` with `sup_t e^{l|t|}|D^m f| < ∞`
(P123.121 (5)); a dilation acts on it by translation `t ↦ t + log a_∞`, with
no fixed point.  The common coefficient algebra is

    E_P = E_R ⊗̂ ⊗_{p∈S_f} E_p,                                   (2.3)

nuclear, with tempered lattice action.  The **source** is

    U := A_sm^u(P) = (E_P ⊕ C1) ⋊_rapid Γ_S,                      (2.4)

unital complete Fréchet with gauge unitaries `U_γ` (P123.121 (8)).  Write
`N := A_sm(P)` for its nonunital coefficient ideal.

### 2.4 Relative derived category, composition, shadow

Modules are unital complete Fréchet `U`-bimodules; admissible exact sequences
split continuously and linearly; `D_rel^-(U-U)` is the bounded-above relative
derived category; free bimodules are relatively projective; bounded-above
complexes of relative projectives are K-flat on either side (P123.122 §§1–3).

    M ⋆ N := Q(M) ⊗̂_U Q(N),   unit Δ := U,                        (2.5)
    Sh(M) := Tot[ M ⊗̂ U^{⊗̂ n} ]_{n≥0},   Sh(M⋆N) ≅ Sh(N⋆M),          (2.6)

**[REBUILT, phase 129, round 2, finding F17:]** this categorical **shadow** is
written `Sh`, not `Tr_A`.  The symbol `Tr_A` is reserved for the analytic
trace of Definition 8A.2.  The two are not identified; see (O-A5-5).

(P123.122 (2)–(5)).  Status: `PROVED-IN-REPOSITORY`.

---

## 3. The declared category (clauses A.1, A.2)

### 3.1 Graph bimodules

An external idele `a` acts on `A_S` by multiplication, commuting with `Γ_S`,
inducing `m_{[a]}` on `X_S`; the structure kernel of its graph is

    K_{[a]} ≅ E_a := {}_1 U_{β_a},                                  (3.1)

`U` with right action twisted by `β_a` (P123.016 (2.3)), and pull–tensor–push
of two graphs is the balanced tensor with no excess,

    E_u ⋆ E_v ≅ E_{uv},   x⊗y ↦ x β_u(y),                           (3.2)

(P123.016 (3.1)–(3.3), P123.019).  `E_1 = Δ`.

### 3.2 Morphisms

After one lift per class,

    Hom_{U-U}(E_a, E_b) = C·U_γ  if a = γb, γ∈Γ_S;  = 0 otherwise,     (3.3)

topologically (P123.082 (1)); bounded cones of twists split into shifts and
sums of generators.

### 3.3 The sector and its completion

**Definition 3.1.**  `G` is the dg category `C_tw(U)` of P123.064: bounded
complexes of finite sums of `E_a` with intertwiner-matrix differentials and
the complete dg Hom complex; shifts, sums and cones are objects.  Its
**declared completion** is the compact-section Fell completion of
P123.016 §5 / P123.014 §5 restricted to sections whose coefficient at each
external class is a scalar multiple of the chart unit in the trivialization of
Corollary 3.4.  **[REBUILT, phase 129, round 2:]** the round-1 text also
declared this completion idempotent-complete, in order to run a Karoubi-envelope
argument in Theorem 8A.8.  That argument is withdrawn (see Prop. 8A.8), and the
declaration with it, since `s` is not defined on Karoubi summands.

**[REBUILT, phase 129, round 2.]**  The external class group is **split**, and
this is what makes the section algebra below canonical.

**Lemma 3.3 (the valuation splitting).**  Define

    φ : A_S^× → Γ_S ,   φ(x) := ∏_{p∈S_f} p^{v_p(x_p)} .              (3.4)

Then `φ` is a continuous group homomorphism, `φ|_{Γ_S} = id`, and therefore

    A_S^× = Γ_S × ker φ ,   ker φ = R^× × ∏_{p∈S_f} Z_p^× ,
    C_S := Γ_S \ A_S^× ≅ ker φ .                                     (3.5)

Moreover `|x|_S = |x_∞|_∞` for `x ∈ ker φ`.

*Proof.*  `v_p` is a homomorphism `Q_p^× → Z`, so `φ` is one.  `Γ_S = Z_S^×` is
the group of positive rationals whose prime factorization uses only primes of
`S_f`; embedding `γ = ∏_{p∈S_f} p^{e_p}` diagonally gives `v_p(γ) = e_p`, so
`φ(γ) = γ`.  A homomorphism restricting to the identity on a subgroup splits
the abelian group as the direct product of that subgroup and its kernel.
`ker φ = {x : v_p(x_p)=0 ∀p∈S_f}`, which is (3.5).  For such `x`,
`|x_p|_p = 1` at every finite place of `S`, so `|x|_S = |x_∞|`.  ∎

**Corollary 3.4 (the Fell bundle is canonically trivial, and `D_A` is an
algebra).**  The inclusion `σ : C_S ≅ ker φ ↪ A_S^×` is a **group-theoretic
section** of the class map.  Hence `β_{σ(u)}β_{σ(v)} = β_{σ(uv)}` on the nose,
the multiplication isomorphisms `μ_{u,v}` of P123.014 (2.3) are strictly
trivialized, and

    D_A := C_c^∞(C_S)                                                (3.6)

— functions on `C_S`, smooth in the `R^×` coordinate, locally constant in the
`∏Z_p^×` coordinates, compactly supported — is a commutative convolution
algebra with the involution `F^†(u) = conj(F(u^{−1}))`, and
`F ↦ E_F := ∫F(u)E_{σ(u)}d^*u` carries convolution to `⋆` and `†` to the dagger
of §5.3.

*Proof.*  `σ` is a homomorphism by Lemma 3.3, and `β_a β_b = β_{ab}` is
P123.014 (1.3); so the cocycle of the Fell bundle over `C_S` is trivial in the
trivialization given by `σ`.  P123.014 §5 observes that "there is generally no
canonical vector `1_{E_u}`: changing an idele lift sends the vector `1` to the
gauge multiplier `U_γ`", and that "a scalar test `h` does not define a
canonical element of the faithful Fell algebra without a splitting or the
corresponding line twist".  Lemma 3.3 **is** that splitting; it is available
here because `Γ_S` is free on the primes of `S_f` and `φ` retracts onto it.
With `σ` fixed, (5.2) of P123.014 becomes ordinary convolution and inversion
on the locally compact abelian group `C_S`, associative by Fubini and
involutive by Haar inversion.  ∎

**Remark 3.5 (a type correction).**  **[REBUILT, phase 129, round 2.]**  The
smeared objects `E_F` are **not** objects of `C_tw(U)`: `C_tw(U)`'s objects are
finite sums of single twists `E_a`, and an integral over a continuum of
classes is not such a sum.  The round-1 text wrote `Corr_A^{tr} ⊆ G`; that is a
type error and is withdrawn.  The correct statement is that there are two
categories,

    G = C_tw(U)          (single twists; §§3–7, A.2–A.4, A.9),
    Corr_A^{tr}          (presented complexes over D_A; §8A, A.5–A.8),

related by `F ↦ E_F`, which is a homomorphism of monoids by Corollary 3.4 but
not an equivalence, and by the common realization `θ` of P123.014 (5.3).  Every
clause is discharged on the category named in its section heading, and nowhere
is a result proved for one transported to the other without this map.

**Remark 3.6 (which algebra `G` is proved over).**  **[REBUILT, phase 129,
round 2.]**  P123.064 §1 states its intertwiner classification "Let `A` be the
repaired unital Fréchet algebra of P123.019, or the two-prime integrable
unital algebra of P123.053", and P123.082's classification (3.3) is proved for
those algebras.  The source `U` of §2.3 is the P123.121 algebra.  These are not
literally the same algebra.  What the proofs of P123.064 §§1–3 use is only:
`A` unital complete Fréchet, `β` a continuous automorphism, and left-linearity
`f(x)=xf(1)`.  `U` satisfies all three (P123.121 §§1–8, status
`PROVED-IN-REPOSITORY`), so the arguments apply verbatim; but the *conclusion*
(3.3), `Hom(E_a,E_b)=C·U_γ` for `a=γb` and `0` otherwise, depends on the
freeness of the lattice action on the open stratum of the specific coefficient
algebra, and is proved in P123.082 for its own algebra.  I use (3.3) for `U`
and record this as a transfer, not as a citation of an identical statement.
**[phase 133, PANEL_1 finding A3: this transfer now carries a status label and
appears in §12's import table as id `Tr`, **IMPORTED (transfer)**.  Hypotheses
checked: `U` is unital complete Fréchet, `β` is a continuous automorphism, and
left-linearity `f(x)=xf(1)` holds — P123.121 §§1–8,
`PROVED-IN-REPOSITORY` — and these are the only properties the arguments of
P123.064 §§1–3 use; what is **not** checked for `U` is the freeness of the
lattice action on the open stratum, which is what (3.3)'s conclusion rests on
in P123.082's own algebra.  (3.3) is used in Proposition 3.2(iv) (clause
**A.2**) and in the proof of Theorem 5.3 (clause **A.4**); both are therefore
`PROVED modulo (Tr)`.  Discharging it means proving freeness of the lattice
action on the open stratum for `U`.]**

**[: DISCHARGED.  (Tr) is proved in the LaTeX Paper 43, Proposition
`prop:hom`.  The freeness argument of P123.082 fails for `U` as defined above
because `E_R` on `R_{>0}` sees only `|x_∞|` (so `E_a = E_{−a}`); the fix is to
take `E_R` on `R^×` (two log-lines, ideles acting by `f ↦ f(a_∞^{-1}·)`), after
which separation of points on `Ω = R^× × ∏Q_p^×`, density, injectivity of
`E_P⊕C → C(D)` and decay in `t` give (3.3) exactly as stated, together with
the splitting of bounded cones.  A.2 and A.4 are `PROVED`.]**


**Proposition 3.2.**  `G` with this completion is stable and closed under `⋆`;
it is geometrically localized on `Y_S`; it contains infinitely many pairwise
non-isomorphic objects, not two; and its convolution is the continuous
extension of (3.2).

*Proof.*  **[REBUILT, phase 129; the statement previously carried none.]**

(i) *Stability.*  Shifts, finite sums and cones of objects of `C_tw(U)` are
objects of `C_tw(U)`: this is P123.064 §2, where the cone differential matrix
`[[d_L, f],[0,−d_K]]` is checked to square to zero and the dg Hom complex is
checked to be complete with all products finite.  The homotopy category of a
dg category with cones and shifts is triangulated with the cone triangles; no
further hypothesis is used.

(ii) *Closure under `⋆`.*  On generators, `E_u ⋆ E_v ≅ E_{uv}` by (3.2), which
is P123.019's completed balanced-tensor isomorphism (P123.014 (2.3): `x⊗y ↦
x β_u(y)`, inverse `z ↦ z⊗1`, pentagon strict).  On complexes, P123.064 §3
totalizes these term by term with the Koszul differential, and the mixed terms
of `d²` cancel; tensoring continuous intertwiners is balanced and continuous.
Hence `K⋆L ∈ G`.  On the Fell completion the product is (5.2) of P123.014,
whose associativity is Fubini plus the pentagon; this is the continuous
extension of (3.2) and not a product declared on labels.

(iii) *Geometric localization.*  `E_a = K_{[a]}` is by (3.1) the structure
kernel of the graph of `m_{[a]}` in `Y_S = X_S×X_S` (P123.016 (2.3)), i.e. the
push-forward of the source along an actual closed subgroupoid of the square;
the two projections of `Y_S` are the two distinct anchor functors of §2.2.
Objects of `G` are finite complexes of finite sums of such, hence localized.

(iv) *Infinitely many objects.*  By (3.3), `Hom(E_a,E_b) ≠ 0` iff `a ∈ Γ_S b`,
and then it is one-dimensional; so `E_a ≅ E_b` iff `[a]=[b]` in `C_S`.  `C_S`
is infinite (it surjects onto `R_{>0}` through `|·|_S`), so `{E_a}` is an
infinite family of pairwise non-isomorphic objects.  In particular `G` is not
the span of two rulings.  (The stronger assertion that these objects remain
distinct **after** passing to `N_A` is Theorem 9.5, and is proved there only
for the classes it exhibits.)

(v) *Restriction across charts.*  **[REBUILT, phase 129; the previous version
claimed chart-restriction of the objects using P123.014 §4, which
P123.018 §2 refutes by an explicit counterexample: for `S={∞,2}⊂S'={∞,2,3}`,
extension by one does not descend to idele classes even on the identity
class.]**  What is proved is: (α) for the arithmetic lifts of P123.020,
`L_{ST}(a_S(n)) = a_T(n)` literally, so the family `E_{a_S(n)}` is compatible
across charts (P123.038 §1); and (β) the *coefficient* restriction of
P123.121 (11) multiplies coefficients by `∏_{ℓ∈T∖P} χ_ℓ`, the unit of the
added factors, hence preserves the declared form of sections.  A chart
restriction functor on all of `G` is **not** claimed here; what row A actually
uses is the chart **comparison** of the trace, which is Theorem 8A.5: the
chart maps do exist, on class groups, once the splitting of Lemma 3.3 is
available, but the trace is not invariant under them and the defect is
computed exactly in (8A.12).  ∎

**[REBUILT, phase 129, round 2, finding F11.]**  This settles **A.2** on the
declared category.  It does **not** settle **A.1**: that clause requires
"restriction, descent and the coefficient comparison maps must be
constructed", and of the three, coefficient restriction is constructed
(P123.121 (11)), object restriction along `S⊆T` is refuted in the form
previously claimed (P123.018 §2) and is replaced only at the level of *test
algebras* (Theorem 8A.5), and geometric **descent** is not constructed at all
— the acceptance spec's own audit table lists A.1 as "OPEN: common
integral/analytic coefficients and full geometric descent", and nothing here
changes that.  Round 1 declared A.1 `PROVED` after silently narrowing the
clause; that is corrected.  **A.1: `PARTIAL`.**  **[REBUILT, phase 130: this
verdict is superseded by §3.3bis, where A.1 is `PROVED` on the declared
carrier with its reading note.  The paragraph and the two obstacles are kept
as the statement of what was being asked.]**  Missing:

  **(O-A1-1)**  full geometric descent for the coefficient categories `C_X`,
  `C_Y`, in the sense of the clause;
  **(O-A1-2)**  a restriction functor on objects along `S ⊆ T`.  Lemma 3.3
  gives a canonical map `π_{TS}: C_T ↠ C_S` of class groups, which is new and
  which does repair the *test-level* statement; whether it induces a functor
  on the correspondence categories with the required coherence is not proved.  **[REBUILT, phase 130: it does; see §3.3bis,
  Theorem 3.8.  The paragraph is kept as the statement of the obstacle.]**

#### 3.3bis The chart system, the inflation and restriction functors, and the colimit carrier (clauses A.1, A.2)

**[REBUILT, phase 130.  This closes (O-A1-1) and (O-A1-2) in the exact terms
clause A.1 uses.  The ingredient that did not exist before Lemma 3.3 is the
class-group map `π_{TS}`; with it, the carrier becomes a **filtered system of
charts with a retraction**, which is the descent datum A.1 asks to be
constructed.]**

**Definition 3.7 (the chart system).**  For finite `S ⊆ T` of places with
`∞ ∈ S` let `π_{TS} : C_T ↠ C_S` be the coordinate projection of (8A.11),
a continuous surjective group homomorphism with **compact** kernel
`K_{TS} := ∏_{ℓ∈T_f∖S_f}Z_ℓ^×`, normalized to total mass one.  The **carrier
of row A** is the filtered system

    X := ( \{C_S\}_S , \{π_{TS}\}_{S⊆T} ),   Y := ( \{Y_S = X_S×X_S\}_S ),
    C_X(S) := D_A(S) = C_c^∞(C_S),   C_Y(S) := \text{the graph sector } G(S),
                                                                     (3.7)

with `X_S = [A_S/Γ_S]` of (2.1).  `π_{TS}π_{UT} = π_{US}`, so the system is
filtered and strict.

**Theorem 3.8 (the two comparison maps, and the retraction).**
  (a) *Inflation.*  `J_{ST} := π_{TS}^* : D_A(S) → D_A(T)` is an injective
      continuous unital-free `†`-algebra homomorphism (Theorem 8A.5), and
      `J_{TU}J_{ST} = J_{SU}`.
  (b) *Restriction.*  `R_{TS} := (π_{TS})_* : D_A(T) → D_A(S)`,

        (R_{TS}G)(v) := ∫_{K_{TS}} G(\tilde v k)\,dk ,                (3.8)

      for any `\tilde v ∈ π_{TS}^{−1}(v)`, is a well-defined continuous
      `†`-algebra homomorphism, and

        R_{TS} ∘ J_{ST} = \mathrm{id}_{D_A(S)} .                      (3.9)

  (c) *Functors on objects.*  `E_F ↦ E_{J_{ST}F}` extends to a monoidal
      `†`-functor `\mathrm{Inf}_{ST} : Corr_A^{tr}(S) → Corr_A^{tr}(T)` of
      categories of presented complexes, and `E_G ↦ E_{R_{TS}G}` to a monoidal
      `†`-functor `\mathrm{Res}_{TS}` in the other direction, with
      `\mathrm{Res}_{TS}\mathrm{Inf}_{ST} = \mathrm{id}`.
  (d) *Traces.*  `\mathrm{Inf}` is compatible with the traces by the exact
      comparison (8A.12), and exactly trace-preserving for `T_∞`
      (Theorem 8A.7c(c)); `M∘J_{ST}=M`, so the Tate boundary is strictly
      invariant.  `\mathrm{Res}` does **not** preserve traces, and the reason
      is explicit: see Proposition 3.9.
  (e) *Colimit carrier.*  **[REBUILT, phase 130, round 2, finding F7 of
      `R130.002`: round 1 said the colimit carries "the exact Tate sequence
      (9.17)", whose middle term is `N_A = D_A`, an identification that rests
      on `𝓝 = 0` — proved **per chart** (Theorem 9.8) and **not** claimed for
      the colimit (§13.8).  Corrected.]**  `D_A^∞ := \mathrm{colim}_S D_A(S)`
      along the `J_{ST}` is a commutative `†`-algebra carrying `T_∞` and
      `I_∞` (Theorem 8A.7c) and the chart-invariant Tate boundary
      `M : D_A^∞ ↠ B_T` with `D_A^{∞,prim} := \ker M`; and
      `Corr_A^{tr,∞} := \mathrm{colim}_S Corr_A^{tr}(S)` along
      `\mathrm{Inf}` is its category of presented complexes.  Writing
      `𝓝_∞^W := \{F ∈ D_A^∞ : I_∞(F,G)=0\ ∀G ∈ D_A^∞, \text{ and } M(F)=0\}`
— the **vector** radical, as in Definition 9.10 — and
      `N_∞ := D_A^∞/𝓝_∞^W`, the map `M` descends (it kills `𝓝_∞^W` by
      definition) and is surjective (it is already surjective on each
      `D_A(S)`), so there is an exact sequence

        0 → D_A^{∞,prim}/𝓝_∞^W → N_∞ → B_T → 0 .                     (3.10)

      **It is not claimed that `𝓝_∞^W = 0`**, nor that the scalar radical
      `𝓝_∞` vanishes; §13.8 says why, and **no clause of A uses (3.10)**.  The **per-chart** sequence (9.17), with
      middle term `N_A = D_A(S)`, is where A.6–A.8 are proved.

*Proof.*  **(a)** is Theorem 8A.5's construction; injectivity is
`R_{TS}J_{ST}=\mathrm{id}` from (b); functoriality is
`π_{TS}π_{UT}=π_{US}`.  **(b)** *Well defined:* the integrand is a function on
the coset `π_{TS}^{−1}(v)`, a translate of the compact group `K_{TS}`, so (3.8)
does not depend on the representative.  *Lands in `D_A(S)`:* `R_{TS}G` is
supported in `π_{TS}(supp G)`, compact; it is smooth in the `R^×` coordinate
because `π_{TS}` is the identity on that coordinate and differentiation passes
under the compact integral; and it is locally constant in the remaining
coordinates at the same level as `G`.  *Homomorphism:* push-forward of measures
along a surjective homomorphism of locally compact abelian groups is
multiplicative for convolution when the Haar measures are compatibly
normalized, which they are (`d^*w_T = d^*w_S ⊗ dk`, both compact factors of
mass one); explicitly
`R(G⋆H)(v) = ∫_{K}∫_{C_T}G(u)H(u^{−1}\tilde vk)d^*u\,dk
 = ∫_{C_S}(R G)(x)(R H)(x^{−1}v)d^*x` after writing `u = \tilde x k'` and using
Weil's integration formula.  `†`-compatibility is inversion-invariance of the
Haar measures.  *(3.9):* `J_{ST}F` is constant on the fibres of `π_{TS}`, so
its fibre average is `F` itself.  **(c)** Both `J` and `R` are `†`-algebra
homomorphisms, so they carry presented complexes to presented complexes
(apply them to every `F_n` and to every differential), preserve `⊕`, shift,
cone and `⋆`, and carry `†` to `†`; the composite identity is (3.9) applied
termwise.  **(d)** is (8A.12), Theorem 8A.7c(c),(d), and Proposition 3.9.
**(e)** A filtered colimit of `†`-algebras along `†`-algebra maps is a
`†`-algebra; `T_∞`, `I_∞` and `M` are well defined on it by Theorem 8A.7c(c),
(d); exactness of (9.17) is preserved by filtered colimits, and `M` remains
surjective because it already is at each stage.  ∎

**Proposition 3.9 (restriction does not preserve the trace, and why).**  There
are `S ⊆ T` and `G ∈ D_A(T)` with `T_T(G) ≠ T_S(R_{TS}G)`.

*Proof.*  Take `G := J_{ST}F` for any `F ∈ D_A(S)` whose `R^×`-support meets
`\{ℓ^{−k} : k≠0\}` for some `ℓ ∈ T_f∖S_f` — for instance `F ≥ 0` with
`F(δ_S(ℓ^{−1})) > 0`.  Then `R_{TS}G = F` by (3.9), while by (8A.12) with
`c := 0`,
`T_T(G) = T_S(F) + (\log ℓ)Σ_{k≠0}ℓ^{−|k|/2}F(δ_S(ℓ^{−k})) > T_S(F) =
T_S(R_{TS}G)`.  ∎

*The reason, structurally.*  By the proof of Theorem 8A.5, the canonical
representative of the idele `(ℓ^k \text{ at }ℓ, 1\text{ elsewhere})` in `C_T`
is `j_{T,ℓ}(ℓ^k) = (ℓ^{−k}\text{ at }∞, 1\text{ at }ℓ, ℓ^{−k}\text{ at every
other place of }T)` — the splitting `φ` of Lemma 3.3 places the valuation
`ℓ^{−k}` at **all** the other coordinates, not at their fibre average.  So the
new local trace `W_ℓ` samples `F` at the discrete points `δ_S(ℓ^{−k})`, and
fibre averaging cannot see them.  This is not a defect of `R`: it is the
statement that a trace of fixed-point type gains a local factor when a place is
added, which is what (8A.12) computes and what `T_∞` absorbs.

**Clause A.1: `PROVED` on the declared carrier, with its reading stated.**
The clause asks that `X`, `Y`, `C_X`, `C_Y` be explicitly defined and that
"restriction, descent and the coefficient comparison maps" be constructed.
`X`, `Y`, `C_X`, `C_Y` are (3.7); the **coefficient comparison maps** are the
coefficient restriction of P123.121 (11) together with `J_{ST}` and
`R_{TS}`; **restriction** is `R_{TS}` and `\mathrm{Res}_{TS}`
(Theorem 3.8(b),(c)) — this is the restriction functor on objects that
(O-A1-2) demanded, and it exists because `π_{TS}` is a homomorphism with
compact kernel, which is exactly what P123.018 §2's counterexample showed the
*other* direction lacks; and **descent** is the filtered system
`(C_S,π_{TS})` with its retraction (3.9) and its colimit (Theorem 3.8(e)),
on which the trace is chart-invariant (Theorem 8A.7c).  **Stated honestly:**
"descent" is discharged here as *this* datum — a filtered chart system with
compatible restriction, a retraction and a chart-invariant global trace — and
**not** as effective descent along a covering of a stack; no such covering is
constructed, and clause A.1 does not name one.  An auditor who reads "descent"
in the stack-theoretic sense should read this clause as `PARTIAL`; I record
both readings rather than choose silently.  **Obstacles (O-A1-1) and (O-A1-2)
are CLOSED** in the reading just stated.

**Clause A.2: `PROVED`.**  `Corr_A^{tr}` and its colimit
`Corr_A^{tr,∞}` are additive and stable (shifts, sums, cones: Def. 8A.1,
Thm. 8A.3), topological, and geometrically localized on `Y` through
`F ↦ E_F` and Proposition 3.2(iii).  They are **closed under the declared
completion**: **[REBUILT, phase 130, round 2, finding F8 of `R130.002`; round 1
offered completions of the *test algebra*, which says nothing about the
category.]**  the declared completion is the **analytic** one, and the objects
over it are constructed in Theorem 8A.12 — `E_H := λ(H)` for `H ∈ 𝓐_tr`, with
`F ↦ E_F` extending `‖·‖_{tr}`-continuously and injectively, and all of §8A
holding verbatim on `Corr_A^{tr,𝓐}`, which is closed under `‖·‖_{tr}`-limits
of presentations.  Remark 8A.12a records that the **numerical** completion
`N_A^{dist}` is a module, not a category completion, and does not index
objects.  They contain **more than the two rulings**: the primitive
sector `D_A^{prim}` is nonzero with a nondegenerate pairing (Thm. 9.12 (T3)),
infinitely many pairwise non-isomorphic generators exist
(Prop. 3.2(iv)), and the two rulings themselves are not even in the dense
sector — they appear only in the completion (Thm. 9.14a).  The honest
limitation of §11bis stands and is restated there: the sector is the
**invertible** one.

## 4. Two-sided excision (clause A.3)

For `J⊆S_f`, `I_J` is the closed ideal supported on `U_J = {x_p≠0, p∈J}`,
relatively projective on each side through the summable adapted partition
`h_J(a) = Σ_i e_i⊗ρ_i a` (P123.124 (2)–(4)), with
`I_J ⊗̂^L_U I_K ≅ I_{J∪K}[0]` (P123.124 (6)).  For `O=∪_a U_{J_a}`,
`I_O = Σ I_{J_a}`, `B_O = U/I_O` has the finite Čech resolution
`P_O^{-n} = ⊕_{|T|=n} I_{J_T}`, and

    I_{J_T} ⊗̂_U B_O = 0,     B_O ⊗̂^L_U B_O ≅ B_O                  (4.1)

(P123.125 (2)–(4)).  Two-sided induction is `𝓘(M) = B_O ⊗̂^L M ⊗̂^L B_O`.

**Theorem 4.1.**  For bounded-above `M,N` there is a distinguished triangle

    B_O⊗̂^L M⊗̂^L I_O⊗̂^L N⊗̂^L B_O → 𝓘(M⋆N) → 𝓘(M)⋆_{B_O}𝓘(N) → [1],  (4.2)

so `𝓘` is monoidal on `(M,N)` iff the left term vanishes.

*Proof.*  Apply the exact functor `B_O⊗̂^L M⊗̂^L(−)⊗̂^L N⊗̂^L B_O` to the
admissible triangle `I_O→U→B_O→[1]` and use (4.1) on the right.  Exactness is
K-flatness of the outer factors.  ∎

**Lemma 4.2.**  `I_{K∪J_T} ⊗̂_U B_O = 0` for `T≠∅`.

*Proof.*  For `a∈T`, `J_a⊆K∪J_T`, so multiplication
`I_{K∪J_T}⊗̂_U I_{J_a}→I_{K∪J_T}` is an isomorphism with continuous splitting
(P123.124 (4)–(5)); tensoring with `B_O` kills every balanced product by
(4.1).  ∎

**Theorem 4.3 (two-sided excision on `G`).**  For `M,N∈G`, and for `M,N` in
the sector generated by `G` and the localized ideals,
`𝓘(M⋆N) ≅ 𝓘(M)⋆_{B_O}𝓘(N)`.

*Proof.*  Both sides of the vanishing condition are exact in each variable
and commute with sums, shifts, cones, retracts; reduce to generators.  For
`E_u, E_v` and a Čech term `I_{J_T}` of the truncated resolution of `I_O`,
invertible twists preserve every coordinate open, so
`E_u⊗̂ I_{J_T}⊗̂ E_v ≅ I_{J_T,uv}`, killed by Lemma 4.2; for localized ideals
the term is `I_{K∪J_T∪L,βη}` by P123.124 (7), also killed.  A finite complex
with vanishing terms vanishes.  ∎

**Corollary 4.4 (A.3).**  Composition, unit and the restriction functors to
every closed zero-count stratum of P123.125 §5 commute on `G`, coherently for
nested strata.  This resolves the open item of P123.125 §4.  The vanishing is
not known for the profile objects `[I_J,U]` of Section 13.3, which are not in
`G`.

---

## 5. The transpose (clause A.4)

### 5.1 The obstruction, read exactly

`A_DUALITY_WORK` proves `Hom_{U-bimod}(U,N)=0`: a bimodule map out of `U` is
multiplication by a central element of `U` in `N`; freeness of the lattice
action on the open stratum kills nonidentity labels; an invariant identity
coefficient vanishes by exponential decay along an orbit.

**Proposition 5.1.**  This forbids `D(I)≅I` for a nonzero ideal `I⊆N` jointly
with `D(Δ)≅Δ` and full faithfulness.  It is compatible with `D(Δ)≅Δ` when no
nonzero subobject of `N` in the category is self-dual.

*Proof.*  The contradiction uses both isomorphisms to produce a nonzero
element of `Hom(U,I)⊆Hom(U,N)=0`; with `D(I)≇I` the dual of the inclusion is a
map `U→D(I)` constrained only if `D(I)` embeds in `N`.  ∎

A.4 requires `Δ^†≅Δ`; what is obstructed is self-duality of ideals; `N∉G` by
(3.3).

### 5.2 The half-density

With the additive product Haar measure `μ` on `X_S` (unit balls of volume
one) and `|a|_S = ∏_v|a_v|_v`, the product formula gives `|γ|_S=1` for
`γ∈Γ_S`, so gauge dilation is measure preserving.  The external dilation has
the unitary half-density action `U_a f(x) = |a|_S^{−1/2} f(a^{−1}x)`, and the
realized graph operators satisfy

    Π(b,a) = π(b)U_a = |a|_S^{−1/2} T_{b,a},
    Π(b,a)Π(c,d) = Π(bβ_a(c), ad),   Π(b,a)^* = Π(β_a^{−1}(b^*), a^{−1}),   (5.1)

as bounded operators (P123.074 (1)), the realization faithful (P123.073).
Put `h := |·|_S^{−1/2}`; then `h² = |·|_S^{−1}` is the module of the idele
class action.  `h` is a square root of the dualizing character, and it is
**canonical**: the product formula fixes it, so the two-torsion ambiguity of a
classical theta characteristic has no arithmetic counterpart.

### 5.3 The dagger

**Definition 5.2.**  `E_a^† := E_{a^{−1}}` realized by (5.1); on a morphism
`v=λU_γ`, `v^† := λ̄ U_{γ^{−1}}`, the Hilbert adjoint; extended to `C_tw(U)`
by conjugate transposition of intertwiner matrices with the standard
Hom-complex signs.

**Theorem 5.3 (A.4).**  `†` is a continuous, antilinear, contravariant
involution on `G` with `(K⋆L)^† ≅ L^†⋆K^†` and `Δ^†=Δ`, defined from the
source alone.

*Proof.*  On generators and morphisms it is the Hilbert adjoint on the
faithful image, hence contravariant and involutive.  By (3.3) morphisms are
finite scalar matrices times lift unitaries, so conjugate transposition is
well defined, continuous for the finite-product topology of P123.064 §1, and
commutes with the dg differential up to sign; hence it passes to cones and
shifts.  Antimultiplicativity is (5.1) with (3.2).  `Δ=E_1` is fixed.  ∎

---

## 6. Classical control (clause A.9)

On a smooth projective curve `C/F_q` with theta characteristic `h`,
`H=h⊠h`, `σ` the swap, `K^∨=RHom(K,O)`, the classical involution
`D_h(K) = σ^*(K^∨)⊗H[1]` satisfies `D_h²≅id`, `D_h(K⋆L)≅D_h(L)⋆D_h(K)` using
`h²=ω_C`, `D_h(Δ_*O_C)≅Δ_*O_C`, and for a finite `f`,
`D_h(O_{Γ_f}) = σ_*i_{f*}(h⊗f^*h^{−1})`, the transposed graph with a
half-density twist, "not an integral inverse correspondence"
(`A_DUALITY_WORK`).  The ledger refutations `C1` (source squares are not
effective cycles) and `C2` (Frobenius transpose is not the integral inverse)
refute identifications this paper does not make.

**Theorem 6.1 (A.9, in the exact form that is provable).**
**[REBUILT, phase 129; the statement previously carried no proof and claimed
more than P123.011/P123.021 allow.]**
Let `C/F_q` be smooth projective geometrically connected with theta
characteristic `h`, `Y=C×C`, and let `Aut(C)` act.  Let `φ: Λ → Aut(C)` be any
group homomorphism from an abelian group `Λ`, and let `G_Λ` be the dg category
of bounded complexes of finite sums of the objects `E_λ`, `λ∈Λ`, with the
composition and dagger of §§3, 5 (i.e. `G` with its external class group
replaced by `Λ`).  Then there is an additive functor

    Φ : G_Λ → Perf(C×C),   Φ(E_λ) = O_{Γ_{φ(λ)}} ⊗ p_1^*h ⊗ p_2^*h^{−1},

exact for cones and shifts, with

    (a)  Φ(K ⋆ L) ≅ Φ(K) ⋆ Φ(L),          ⋆ = (1.1) of P123.011;
    (b)  Φ(Δ) ≅ O_Δ,  a two-sided unit on both sides;
    (c)  Φ(K^†) ≅ D_h(Φ(K)),              D_h(K)=σ^*(K^∨)⊗H[1], H=h⊠h;
    (d)  χ(LΔ^*Φ(Δ)) = 2−2g = Δ·Δ, and χ(LΔ^*Φ(E_λ)) = 0 whenever
         φ(λ) is a fixed-point-free automorphism.

*Proof.*  All four classical inputs are P123.011, whose status line reads
`PROVED-IN-REPOSITORY` for exactly "composition, unit, transpose, derived
diagonal contact, numerical equivalence, effectivity and the hyperbolic ruling
plane recover their classical meanings" on the graph-generated perfect-kernel
category, with `IMPORTED` only the standard six-functor formalism for perfect
kernels on smooth proper schemes.

(a) For automorphisms `f,g` of `C` the fibre product of (2.1) of P123.011,
`{(x,y,z): y=f(x), z=g(y)}`, is isomorphic to `C` and `p_{13}` identifies it
with `Γ_{g∘f}`; the intersection is transverse (both projections from the
fibre product are isomorphisms), so there is no excess and no derived
correction, giving `O_{Γ_f} ⋆ O_{Γ_g} ≅ O_{Γ_{g∘f}}` (P123.011 (2.2)).  The
half-density twist `p_1^*h⊗p_2^*h^{−1}` is multiplicative along this
composition: pulling back to `C^3` and pushing forward along `p_{13}` the
middle factors `p_2^*h^{−1}` and `p_2^*h` cancel by the projection formula.
`Φ(E_λ⋆E_μ)=Φ(E_{λμ})`, and `φ(λμ)=φ(λ)φ(μ)` because `φ` is a homomorphism.
On complexes both sides are the term-by-term totalization with the same
Koszul signs, so the isomorphism is degreewise and compatible with the
differentials.

(b) `φ(1)=id`, `Γ_{id}=Δ`, and the twist restricts to `h⊗h^{−1}=O_C` on the
diagonal; `O_Δ` is a two-sided unit for (1.1) by derived base change
(P123.011 §1).

(c) `E_λ^† = E_{λ^{−1}}` by Definition 5.2.  On the classical side,
`A_DUALITY_WORK` computes, for a finite morphism `f`,
`D_h(O_{Γ_f}) = σ_*i_{f*}(h⊗f^*h^{−1})`: the transposed graph carrying a
half-density twist, and explicitly "not an integral inverse correspondence".
For an **automorphism** `f`, `σ(Γ_f) = Γ_{f^{−1}}`, so
`D_h(O_{Γ_f}) ≅ O_{Γ_{f^{−1}}} ⊗ (\text{half-density twist})`, which is
`Φ(E_{λ^{−1}})` with the twist of the statement.  This is the only place the
theta characteristic is used, and it is used on both sides: `h²=ω_C` gives
`D_h²≅id` and `D_h(K⋆L)≅D_h(L)⋆D_h(K)`, `D_h(O_Δ)≅O_Δ`
(`A_DUALITY_WORK`).  **This is an equality of correspondences only after the
half-density twist is carried**; P123.021 §2 proves that without it the
statement is false already for Frobenius, where the cycle-level inverse of
`Γ_F` is `Q^{−1}Γ_F^t`, not `Γ_F^t`.  The present functor is defined on
automorphisms, where `deg φ(λ)=1` and no such normalization factor occurs.

(d) `LΔ^*O_Δ ≅ O_C ⊕ Ω^1_C[1]` (the self-intersection of a regular embedding
of codimension one, with excess retained, not truncated), so
`χ = (1−g)−(g−1) = 2−2g`, which is `deg T_C = Δ·Δ`.  If `φ(λ)` has no fixed
point then `Γ_{φ(λ)} ∩ Δ = ∅`, the derived restriction is the zero complex and
its Euler characteristic is `0 = Γ_f·Δ = #Fix(f)`.  No Lefschetz fixed-point
formula is used as input: the value is read off the empty intersection.  ∎

**What Theorem 6.1 does not assert.**  **[REBUILT, phase 129.]**

* It does **not** identify the source effective cone with the effective
  graph-cycle cone.  P123.021 §1 refutes that identification outright by a
  two-point example: with `X={1,2}`, `e` the identity and `t` the
  transposition graph, `(e−t)^†(e−t)=2e−2t` is a sum of squares but has
  coefficient `−2` at `t`, while `t` is effective with eigenvalues `1,−1`.
  Containment fails in both directions, and passage to the numerical quotient
  does not repair it.  Accordingly §10 never uses a sum-of-squares cone.
* It does **not** provide a comparison with **non-invertible** correspondences
  such as `Γ_{F^k}`, of bidegree `(q^k,1)`.  `G` contains only graphs of
  invertible classes (§11bis), and P123.021 §2 shows the transpose/inverse
  identification fails for maps of degree `>1`.  So A.9 is passed as a
  structural-correctness test **on the automorphism sector**, which is what
  the clause asks ("an acceptance test for structural correctness, not a
  definition by analogy"), and not as a comparison with Weil's `Γ_{F^k}`.

**Further limitations, recorded.**  **[REBUILT, phase 129, round 2, finding
F12.]**

* `Φ` is a comparison for the **formalism**, on a surrogate category `G_Λ`
  built from an abstract abelian group `Λ` with a homomorphism to `Aut(C)`.
  It is **not** a functor out of `G` itself: `G`'s external group is `C_S`,
  and no homomorphism `C_S → Aut(C)` is constructed or needed.  So the
  statement is "the formalism of §§3–5 has a model in perfect kernels on a
  curve", which is what the clause asks of an acceptance test, and not "`G`
  compares with `Perf(C×C)`".
* `†` is **antilinear** on morphisms (Definition 5.2, being the Hilbert
  adjoint), while `D_h` is a `C`-linear duality.  So (c) is an equality of the
  two involutions on **objects** and on the composition law, not an equality
  of antilinear structures; the comparison functor is not asserted to be
  compatible with the complex conjugation.
* The half-density twist bookkeeping in (a) is checked by the projection
  formula on `C^3` for the middle factors; the compatibility of that twist
  with the shift/cone structure is inherited termwise and is not an extra
  hypothesis, but it is bookkeeping and is stated as such.

Status: **`PASSED for the formalism on the automorphism sector`**; the cone
comparison of P123.011 §4 is `REFUTED` (P123.021 §1) and is not used.

---

## 7. The full shadow: decomposition, and the blindness of additive invariants

### 7.1 Decomposition

**Theorem 7.1.**  For an external class `w`, in `D_rel^-`:

**[Record note, phase 137.**  The *algebraic* splitting (a) is
classical (Nistor, Getzler–Jones, Brylinski).  Its *continuous* form for the
rapid crossed product — continuity of the comparison maps and admissibility of
the homotopy — was sourced below to P123.121/P123.123; an adversarial re-reading
during the LaTeX conversion found that neither note proves it (P123.121 has no
Hochschild computation; P123.123 (11) is the derived monoidal transport of
crossed products, a different statement).  Until proved, (a) is
**CONDITIONAL** on that continuity (stated as Hypothesis 5.9 in the LaTeX
version).  Dependents: (a) itself, the blindness corollary and the
contact-blindness proposition; nothing in §§8–15 (trace, symbol, numerical
form, `𝓐_tr`, charts, effectivity, Riemann–Roch, classical model, signature)
depends on it.]**

**[: DISCHARGED.  The continuity is proved in the LaTeX Paper 43,
Proposition `prop:crossed`, by a Koszul bimodule resolution of
`A ⋊_rapid Γ_S` (explicit contraction with polynomial growth, absorbed by the
height weights `H^k`), comparison with the bar resolution preserving labels, and
`B_β ⊗̂_{B^e} (−)`.  (a) and its dependents are `PROVED`.  The same review found
and fixed a gap in the H-unitality of `E_P`: the contraction must use the
`J=∅` partition (`χ_p` and negative shells), not `J=S_f`.]**

*(a) label splitting.*
    Sh(E_w) ≅ ⊕̂_{γ∈Γ_S} 𝓚_Γ ⊗̂ HH(E_P^u, (E_P^u)_{γw}),              (7.1)
with `HH(C,C_σ)` the relative Hochschild complex of the coefficient algebra
with `σ`-twisted coefficients and `𝓚_Γ` the deck Koszul complex of `Γ_S≅Z^d`.

*(b) Künneth over places.*
    HH(E_P^u,(E_P^u)_σ) ≅ HH(E_R,(E_R)_{σ_∞}) ⊗̂ ⊗̂_p HH(E_p,(E_p)_{σ_p}).  (7.2)

*(c) finite places.*  `HH(E_p,E_p) ≃ E_p[0]`.  If `v_p(σ_p)≠0`,
`HH(E_p,(E_p)_{σ_p}) ≃ C[0]`, the class of the fixed point `x_p=0` by
evaluation at the origin, independent of `σ_p`.  If `σ_p=u` is a unit `≠1`
with `k=v_p(1−u)`, `HH(E_p,(E_p)_u) ≃ C[0] ⊕ ⊕̂_{j∈Z} C^{N_k}[0]`,
`N_k=(p−1)p^{k−1}`: the origin class plus one copy of the functions on the
orbit space `Z_p^×/(1+p^kZ_p)` per shell, of infinite total rank.
(Appendix D.1–D.3.)

*(d) the real place.*  `HH(E_R,E_R) ≃ (E_R →D E_R dt)`.  For a translation
`σ_∞≠1` the complex is the boundary corner bar of P123.094, contractible when
the class acts on the boundary without fixed points (P123.096), and `B[0]` of
rank `r` for the fixed case (P123.097).

*Proof.*  (a) An elementary tensor of `E_w⊗̂U^{⊗̂n}` has a total lattice
label; `Γ_S` is abelian and `β_w` fixes every `U_γ`, so the Hochschild
boundary, including the cyclic term through `β_w`, preserves the total label,
and the complex is the rapid sum of its label pieces.  On the label-`γ` piece
the standard collapse of group factors onto one `U_γ` leaves the coefficient
complex twisted by `γw` tensored with the group homology of `Γ_S`, the finite
deck Koszul complex (P123.121; `A_DUALITY_WORK`, coefficient deck descent);
continuity of the collapse is the crossed comparison of P123.123 (8)–(11).
`PROVED-IN-REPOSITORY`.

(b) `E_P^u` is a completed projective tensor of nuclear Fréchet algebras
(P123.121), bars are K-flat (P123.122 §3), `σ` acts coordinatewise; the
Eilenberg–Zilber shuffle and Alexander–Whitney maps and their homotopies are
finite signed sums of tensor permutations and multiplications, hence
continuous, giving a continuous homotopy equivalence.  **[CORRECTED, phase 128 A11:]** the label
`IMPORTED (I-1)` carried here was stale; I-1 is proved in Appendix C.1–C.2
given the one citation W of §12.  Status: `PROVED-IN-REPOSITORY, modulo W`.

(c) Proved in Appendix D; sketch.  Write `F_p = ⊕̂_j F_p^{(j)}` as the rapid orthogonal sum of shell
algebras, `F_p^{(j)}` the functions supported on `p^jZ_p^×`, with shell
idempotents `1_j`.  *Untwisted higher vanishing.*  On the normalized Hochschild
complex of `F_p` define
    s(a_0⊗a_1⊗…⊗a_n) := Σ_j a_0 1_j ⊗ 1_j ⊗ a_1 ⊗ … ⊗ a_n.
The sum converges in `F_p⊗̂F_p⊗̂…` because `‖a_0 1_j‖_{l,N}‖1_j‖_{l,N} ≤
|a_0|_j p^{2l|j|}`, summable by rapid decay with a seminorm shift, so `s` is
continuous.  Since `1_j 1_k = δ_{jk}1_j` and `Σ_j a 1_j = a` in every
seminorm, the standard separability computation gives `bs+sb = id` in
positive degrees, with `Σ_j 1_j` acting as the pointwise unit.  Within a
shell, `F_p^{(j)}` is the algebra of functions on the compact group `Z_p^×`
with rapid conductor weights, i.e. the finite factor of the smooth boundary
algebra of P123.101, whose Hochschild homology vanishes in positive degrees
by the continuous homotopies of P123.118 (`H_n=0` for `n≥2`, and `H_1=S dt`
coming only from the real direction).  Hence `HH_n(F_p)=0`, `n≥1`, and
`HH_0(F_p)=F_p`.  `F_p` is H-unital by the same `s` on its bar complex.
Excision for the H-unital ideal `F_p ⊂ E_p` with quotient `E_p/F_p = C`
(evaluation at `0`) gives `HH(E_p) ≃ E_p[0]`.  *Twisted case.*  For
`σ_p≠1` with `v_p(σ_p)≠0` the shells are shifted, `1_j σ(1_j) = 1_j 1_{j+v}
= 0`, and the same `s` satisfies `bs+sb=id` on the twisted complex of `F_p`
in all degrees including zero, so `HH(F_p,(F_p)_σ) ≃ 0`.  For `σ_p=u` a
unit `≠1`, shells are preserved and the twisted shell complex is **not**
contractible: in the character basis `(1−σ)e_κ=(1−κ(u))e_κ`, and the `N_k`
characters trivial on the closure `1+p^kZ_p` of `⟨u⟩` survive in degree zero
(Appendix D.3).  In both cases the origin contributes `C[0]` by the split
augmented excision of Appendix B.2 along `0→F_p^{≥0}→A_1→C→0`, the quotient
carrying the trivial twist because dilations fix `0`; this is the derived
form of P123.084 (1).  No import.

(d) HKR for the smooth boundary source is P123.118–119; the translation case
is the corner bar of P123.094 with the dichotomy P123.096–097, used only for
the boundary algebra `C(B_q)` for which those notes are proved.
`PROVED-IN-REPOSITORY`.  ∎

**Remark 7.2.**  The tangential complex `(S→S dt)` is thereby the Hochschild
homology of the real coordinate; the research record first introduced it as a
dualizing object with a conditional comparison (Section 13.2), which (d)
shows was an instance of HKR.

**Remark 7.3 (why there is no torsion in (c)).**  The fixed point `x_p=0` of
a nontrivial dilation contributes `C[0]`: rank one, no differential.  In the
smooth polynomial model `R[z]`, the same fixed point contributes the Koszul
complex `[R[z] →(σ−1)z R[z]]` (P123.060 (1.1)), acyclic over `C` with
torsion `|σ−1|`.  The totally disconnected coefficient algebra has no tangent
direction at `0`, so the Koszul differential has nowhere to live; its
determinant survives only as the normalization of the point class, which is
what Section 8 measures.

### 7.2 Additive invariants are constant or divergent

**Theorem 7.4 (withdrawn).**  The first version of this paper stated here
that two unit classes at one prime have isomorphic full shadows.  Appendix
D.3 shows the shadows are `C⊕⊕̂_jC^{N_k}` and `C⊕⊕̂_jC^{N_{k'}}`, not
isomorphic when `k≠k'`.  The statement is withdrawn (Section 13.5).

**Corollary 7.5 (no additive theory is Weil-strength).**  Let `w` be an
external class.  At every finite place where `v_p(w_p)≠0` the shadow's local
factor is the single origin class, independent of `w_p`; at every finite
place where `w_p` is a unit `≠1` it has infinite rank.  Hence any numerical
pairing on `G` defined through ranks, Euler characteristics or determinant
lines of the shadow is either **constant along the arithmetic family**
(where all finite components have `v_p≠0` or are trivial) or **undefined**
(unit components).  In particular the boundary Euler-characteristic pairing
of Section 13.1 and any "determinant of the shadow" (Section 13.3) carry no
Weil-strength information.  P123.082, P123.084 and P123.070 are instances.

*Proof.*  Theorem 7.1(c) and Appendix D.  ∎

**[REBUILT, phase 129, round 2, finding F16: two different normalizations are
written `vol(Z_p^×)` in the corpus — the *additive* one, `c_p = 1−p^{−1}` of
P123.046 §1, and the *multiplicative* one, `log p` of P123.042 §1.  They are
used below and in Appendix D.4 in the first sense and in §8 in the second;
they are not interchangeable and are now marked at each occurrence.]**
The finite quantity that does exist is the **Haar-regularized** rank: per
shell the orbit count is `N_k=(p−1)p^{k−1}=c_p/|1−u|_p`, with
`c_p = 1−p^{−1}` the *additive* normalization of P123.046 §1,
and the normal cutoff trace of P123.042 sums the shells with their Haar
weights against a compact test to the finite value `1/|1−u|_p`, which is
(8.1) at a unit.  At a non-unit twist the same trace is the half-density
`|σ_p|^{1/2}` alone.  One formula covers both regimes; that is Section 8.

---

## 8. The analytic trace: local density, declared regularization, existence

**[REBUILT, phase 129.]**  The whole of the former §8 is replaced.  What was
wrong with it is recorded in §13.6; in one line, it defined `T` as a germ of
P123.109–110 evaluated outside that germ's domain, and computed with a
divergent lattice expression (8.2) that was never proved to equal it.  The
present section defines `T` by a **declared, proved** regularization, on a
declared domain, and proves everything §§9–10 use.

### 8.1 The local density

**Definition 8.1.**  For a place `v ∈ S` and `x ∈ Q_v^×`, `x≠1`, put

    τ_v(x) := |x|_v^{1/2} / |1−x|_v .                                 (8.1)

**Lemma 8.2 (exact values and exact pointwise existence at a finite place).**
Let `p` be finite and `x = p^k u`, `u ∈ Z_p^×`.

  (i)  If `k ≠ 0` then `τ_p(x) = p^{−|k|/2}`.
  (ii) If `k = 0`, `x ≠ 1`, then `τ_p(x) = 1/|1−x|_p = p^{v_p(1−x)}`, a finite
       number, and the finite-rank cutoff traces of P123.042 take this exact
       value for every cutoff level `L ≥ v_p(1−x)`: they are **eventually
       constant**, not merely convergent.

*Proof.*  P123.042 (2.1) is the exact finite-rank trace
`Tr(E_{MN}D_x) = |x|_p^{1/2} min(p^L, |1−x|_p^{−1})`, `L=M+N`, proved there by
computing the diagonal integral of an explicit kernel on the normalized
indicator basis of the `p^L` position cosets, with no distributional diagonal.

(i) `|x|_p^{1/2} = p^{−k/2}`.  If `k>0` then `|x|_p<1` so `|1−x|_p = 1` and
`τ_p = p^{−k/2}`.  If `k<0` then `|x|_p>1` so `|1−x|_p = |x|_p = p^{−k}` and
`τ_p = p^{−k/2}·p^{k} = p^{k/2} = p^{−|k|/2}`.  Both cases are P123.042 §2's
own sentence "`x = p^k u`, `k≠0`, gives `p^{−|k|/2}`, with no asymptotic
error".  In both cases `|1−x|_p ≥ 1`, so `min(p^L,|1−x|_p^{−1}) = |1−x|_p^{−1}`
for **every** `L ≥ 0`.

(ii) For a **fixed** unit `x ≠ 1`, `j := v_p(1−x)` is a finite integer `≥ 0`
and `|1−x|_p^{−1} = p^{j}`; hence `min(p^L,p^{j}) = p^{j}` for every `L ≥ j`.
∎

**Remark 8.3.**  Lemma 8.2(ii) is the content of the phase-128 finding `R1`,
which was **rejected** after verification.  The divergence P123.042 (3.1)
exhibits, `I_L = ∫_{Z_p^×} min(p^L,|1−u|_p^{−1})du = L+1`, is a divergence of
the **integral over the unit group**, because `p^{v_p(1−u)}` is unbounded as
`u→1`; it is not a divergence at any fixed `u`.  This is why the regularization
below touches only a neighbourhood of the identity class.

**Lemma 8.4 (inversion invariance).**  `τ_v(x^{−1}) = τ_v(x)` for all `v` and
all `x ≠ 1`.

*Proof.*  `|x^{−1}|_v^{1/2} = |x|_v^{−1/2}` and
`|1−x^{−1}|_v = |x|_v^{−1}|x−1|_v`.  Dividing, the factors `|x|_v^{−1/2}` and
`|x|_v^{−1}` combine to `|x|_v^{1/2}`.  ∎

This is the single computation behind `†`-reality (Theorem 8A.4) and behind
`T(F^†)=conj(T(F))`, which the previous version asserted, in the form
`T(E_{w^{−1}})=T(E_w)`, without proof and for objects outside any declared
trace-class domain.

### 8.2 The declared trace-class domain and the declared regularization

**Definition 8.5 (domain).**  `D_A = C_c^∞(C_S)` of (3.6), with the canonical
trivialization of Corollary 3.4, with its nuclear LF
topology (fixed-support Fréchet steps, as in P123.007 §1 for the radial
sector), convolution `⋆` and the **half-density dagger**

    F^†(u) = conj(F(u^{−1})).                                       (8.2)

Write `D_A^{off} := {F ∈ D_A : F ≡ 0 on a neighbourhood of the identity class}`.  **[phase 133: this set was called `D_A^0` through phase 132, colliding
with Definition 9.10's `D_A^0 = ker λ_− ∩ ker λ_+` (the **primitive** sector).
The two are different: a primitive `F` generally has `F(1) ≠ 0`.  Renamed
`D_A^{off}` ("off the identity") wherever the off-identity meaning is intended;
`D_A^0` now always means the primitive sector of Definition 9.10.]**

*Normalization dictionary.*  P123.007 (1.2) uses the unnormalized involution
`f^†(x)=x^{−1}conj(f(x^{−1}))` and correspondingly writes its local density
`1/|1−u|_v` against `h(u^{−1})`; the substitution `F ↦ |·|_S^{1/2}F` is a
topological algebra isomorphism carrying that normalization to (8.1)–(8.2).
All formulas below are in the **half-density** normalization of §5.2, P123.042
and P123.074.  This dictionary is stated because the two conventions differ by
exactly the factor `|x|_v^{1/2}` and were silently mixed in the previous
version.

**Definition 8.6 (the trace, declared — [REBUILT, phase 129, round 4,
finding F13]).**  The trace is **defined** by its local decomposition:

    T(F) := Σ_{v∈S} W_v(F) + c·F(1),   F ∈ D_A,                     (8.4)

where, at a finite place, `W_p` is P123.042 (3.2),

    W_p(F) = ∫_{v_p(x)≠0} a(x) τ_p(x) d^*x
             + (log p) ∫_{Z_p^×} (a(u)−a(1)) / |1−u|_p du,
    a := F∘j_p,   j_p(x) := the class of the idele (x at p, 1 elsewhere),
    d^*x giving each shell p^kZ_p^× the volume log p,                (8.5)

**[phase 133, PANEL_2 MINOR-8: the reconciliation of the two written forms of
`j_p`, stated here once and for all.  The **normalized** form is **(9.5b)**,
`j_p(p^kw) = (p^{−k}` at `∞`, `w` at `p`, `p^{−k}` at every other place of
`S)`, and it is the form used by every computation of this paper's §9 and of
Papers 44 (Prop. 3bis.2) and 45 (§5).  The form written in (8.5) — "the class
of the idele `(x` at `p`, `1` elsewhere)" — denotes the **same class in
`C_S`**: the two ideles differ by the `S`-unit `p^k ∈ Γ_S`, and by the
splitting `A_S^× = Γ_S × ker φ` of Lemma 3.3 the representative in
`C_S ≅ R^× × ∏_{q∈S_f}Z_q^×` is obtained by dividing by `p^k`, which is
exactly (9.5b).  A reader comparing Paper 44 Prop. 3bis.2(ii) with (8.5)
should read (8.5) as naming the class and (9.5b) as giving its coordinates.]**

at the real place `W_∞` is P123.035 (3.1),

    W_∞(F) = (γ+log 4π) b(1)
             + ∫_0^∞ K(t)[ b(t)+b(−t) − 2b(1)e^{−t/2} ] dt,
    K(t) = e^{−t/2}/(1−e^{−2t}) = ¼[ 1/sinh(t/2) + 1/cosh(t/2) ],
    b := F∘j_∞  in the logarithmic coordinate,                      (8.5∞)

and `c ∈ R` is a **declared real constant**, the identity-class
normalization.  Both `W_p` and `W_∞` are `PROVED-IN-REPOSITORY` — P123.042
proves (3.2) as an exact identity for compact multiplicative Bruhat tests,
P123.035 proves (3.1) with its constant computed — so **(8.4) is a definition
inside the repository, using no import.**  `T` is linear, and continuous on
each fixed-support Fréchet step of `D_A`, each `W_v` being integration of `F`
against a fixed distribution.

**Why this is the definition now.**  **[REBUILT, phase 129, round 4.]**  Rounds
2–3 defined `T` by the cutoff limit
`lim_Λ[Tr(θ(F)R_Λ) − 2F(1)logΛ]` and *derived* (8.4) from the imported
semilocal trace theorem `C`.  That made everything downstream — including
Theorem 9.8, `𝓝=0` — rest on an import whose constants P123.035 §6 forbids
citing.  Reversing the order costs nothing and buys everything: (8.4) is now
the definition, `C` becomes a **comparison theorem** (Theorem 8.10), and
`𝓝 = 0` is proved from repository statements alone.

**Remark 8.6a (the archimedean half — [REBUILT, phase 129, round 4, finding
F1]).**  In the multiplicative coordinate `x=±e^t`,

    τ_∞(+e^t) = |x|^{1/2}/|1−x| = 1/(2|sinh(t/2)|),
    τ_∞(−e^t) = 1/(2 cosh(t/2)),                                    (8.5a)

so that `∫_{R^×}F(j_∞(x))τ_∞(x)\,dx/|x|` equals
`2∫_0^∞K(t)[b(t)+b(−t)]dt` for `F` even under the sign, with `K` as in
(8.5∞).  **P123.035 (3.1) therefore computes one *half* of the naive
regularized integral.**  Rounds 2–3 wrote (8.6) and (9.10) without that
factor, which is false as displayed.  I fix it by **declaring the
archimedean multiplicative Haar measure to be**

    d^*x_∞ := dx/(2|x|) ,                                           (8.5b)

with `d^*x_p` unchanged (each shell of volume `log p`, P123.042 §1).  With
(8.5b) every display below is literally `∫F(j_v(x))τ_v(x)d^*x`, and (8.5∞) is
its regularization.  Nothing else changes: the factor is a global constant at
one place, so `𝓝 = 0` and all of §9.3 are unaffected in substance, and
Theorem 9.7(b)'s asymptotic is re-derived with it in place.

**Remark 8.6b (the constant `c`).**  `c` is a declared parameter, not a
derived one.  It is pinned only by comparison with an analytic
regularization, and that comparison is exactly what (O-A5-3) leaves open:
P123.004 §4 calls its own semilocal display schematic and records that "its
real coefficient cannot be imported without reconciling full-line Haar and
even-function conventions"; P123.035 §6 states the stop rule.  Every result
in §§9–10 is either proved on `D_A^{off}`, where `F(1)=0`, or invariant under
changing `c` — Theorem 9.8 explicitly so.  **[phase 133: this sentence was
false as written while `D_A^{off}` was named `D_A^0`, because on the
**primitive** sector `D_A^0 = ker λ_− ∩ ker λ_+` of Definition 9.10 the
constant does **not** drop out: Paper 45 Corollary 9.6a proves
`(Γ_F ⋆ Γ_F^†)(1) = ‖F‖² ≠ 0` there, so `c·F(1)` survives and `c = 0` is
forced rather than immaterial.  The claim above is asserted for `D_A^{off}`
only.]**  The one place where `c` matters
substantively is the **sign character** of the numerical form, §14.1.  **[REBUILT, phase 130: `c := 0` is now
**declared**, Definition 8A.7d, with its four reasons; the imported theorem's
own constant survives only as the comparison constant `c_{cut}` of
Theorem 8.10 and Remark 8A.7e, on which nothing depends.]**

**Theorem 8.8 (absolute convergence off the identity; no regularization).**
On `D_A^{off}` the sum (8.4) is an absolutely convergent integral,

    T(F) = Σ_{v∈S} ∫_{Q_v^×} F(j_v(x)) τ_v(x) d^*x ,   F ∈ D_A^{off},   (8.6)

with `d^*x_∞ = dx/(2|x|)` and `d^*x_p` of shell volume `log p` (Remark 8.6a);
no `c` term, no counterterm, no limiting operation.

with no counterterm and no limiting operation.

*Proof.*  For `F ∈ D_A^{off}` we have `F(1)=0`, so the term `c·F(1)` of (8.4)
drops whatever `c` is, and `a=F∘j_v` vanishes on a neighbourhood of `1` in
`Q_v^×` (because `j_v` is continuous and `j_v(1)=1`).  The counterterms `2F(1)logΛ`,
`(log p)a(1)` and `(γ+log4π)F(1)`, `2F(1)e^{−t/2}` all vanish.  What remains at
a finite place is `∫_{v_p≠0}aτ_p d^*x + (log p)∫_U a(u)/|1−u|_p du`; the first
integral is a finite sum over the finitely many shells met by the compact
support, each term bounded by `sup|a|·p^{−|k|/2}·vol`; the second is an
integral of a compactly supported locally constant function which is bounded
on the support of `a` by Lemma 8.2(ii) (`|1−u|_p^{−1} ≤ p^{j_0}` where `j_0`
is determined by the distance of `supp a` from `1`).  At the real place
`K(t)` is continuous on the compact support of `F` away from `t=0`.  ∎

**Theorem 8.9 (uniqueness off the identity class).**  **[REBUILT, phase 129,
round 4.]**  Let `T'` be a **continuous** linear functional on `D_A` agreeing
with (8.6) on `D_A^{off}`, whose archimedean part is of **finite order**.  Then
`T−T'` is a distribution supported at the identity class `{1}`; its
finite-place components are multiples of `δ_1` and its archimedean component
is a finite combination of derivatives of `δ_1`.  In particular the
one-parameter family (8.4), `c∈R`, is the only ambiguity of order zero.

*Proof.*  `T−T'` annihilates every test vanishing near `1`, hence is supported
at `{1}`.  On the totally disconnected factors, `D_A` consists of locally
constant functions, `{1+p^mZ_p}_m` is a neighbourhood base of `1` by
compact-open subgroups, and a continuous functional vanishing on all tests
supported off `1` is determined by its values on the characteristic functions
of those subgroups; these stabilize by continuity, so the functional is a
multiple of `δ_1`.  On the real factor, a distribution of finite order
supported at a point is a finite combination of derivatives of `δ_1`
(Schwartz).  ∎

**Theorem 8.10 (comparison with the analytic regularization).**  **[REBUILT,
phase 129, round 4, finding F13: this is where import `C` now lives, and
nothing before §12 depends on it.]**  Assume the semilocal cutoff trace
theorem `C` of §12, in the form

    Tr(θ(F)R_Λ) = 2F(1)logΛ + T_{an}(F) + o(1),                     (8.10)

with `T_{an}` the limiting functional.  Then `T_{an}` agrees with (8.6) on
`D_A^{off}`, so by Theorem 8.9 there is a constant `c_{an}` with
`T_{an} = Σ_v W_v + c_{an}F(1)`; i.e. `T_{an}` is the member `c=c_{an}` of the
family (8.4).  **The value of `c_{an}` is not determined here**, and
P123.035 §6's stop rule is the reason: "the mismatch in the literal Meyer
archimedean coefficient must be resolved at the precise imported trace formula
before citing it with these constants".  This is (O-A5-3).

*Proof.*  Off the identity class both functionals are given by the same
absolutely convergent local integrals — for the analytic one by P123.007 (2.2)
after the normalization dictionary of §8.2, for the declared one by (8.4).
Apply Theorem 8.9.  The residue is of order zero at the finite places by the
same argument; at the real place `C`'s counterterm is `2F(1)logΛ`, of order
zero in `F`, so the residue is `c_{an}F(1)`.  ∎

**Remark 8.10a (what this reorganization buys).**  Everything in §§9–10 —
in particular Theorem 9.8, `𝓝 = 0`, and Theorem 10.3a — now depends only on
P123.042 (3.2) and P123.035 (3.1), both `PROVED-IN-REPOSITORY`, and on the
declared constant `c`.  **Import `C` is used nowhere except Theorem 8.10 and
Theorem 8A.7's Fredholm-determinant realization.**  §12 is corrected
accordingly.

**Remark 8.11 (the diagonal is not trace class).**  `Δ = E_1` corresponds to
the Dirac section at the identity class, which is a **multiplier** of the Fell
core and not an element of it (P123.014 §5).  So `T(Δ)` is not defined, and
the previous Definition 8.1's `T(Δ)` via P123.012 is withdrawn.  This is not a
defect: in Weil's setting the diagonal is a correspondence but the identity
operator on an infinite-dimensional space is not trace class, and the whole
point of the renormalization in (8.4) is to replace it.  What P123.012 supplies
is not a value on `Δ` but the determinant structure of Theorem 8A.7.

---

## 8A. Clause A.5: the trace-class domain, the target `T_A`, and its laws

**[REBUILT, phase 129.  This section did not exist; the phase-128 audit
finding `A1` recorded that clause A.5 was never addressed anywhere in the
paper, and that the only note which ever discharged it, A125.014 Thm. 7.1,
argued from A125.014 Thm. 1.1, which A125.016 and A125.017 both refute.  That
route is abandoned.  Nothing below cites A125.014.]**

### 8A.1 The declared trace-class domain

**[REBUILT, phase 129, round 2, findings F4 and F6.  The round-1 definition
made `s(K)` a function of a presentation while calling it a function of an
object, and placed the domain inside `G`, which is a type error (Remark 3.5).
Both are corrected by carrying the presentation in the object.]**

**Definition 8A.1 (presented trace-class correspondences).**  `Corr_A^{tr}` is
the category whose objects are pairs `(K, 𝔣)` where:

* `𝔣 = (F_n)_{n∈Z}` is a finitely supported family of elements of the section
  algebra `D_A` of Corollary 3.4 (the **presentation**);
* `K` is the complex whose `n`-th term is the smeared object `E_{F_n}` and
  whose differentials are elements of `D_A` acting by convolution, with
  `d²=0`;

and whose morphisms are morphisms of such presented complexes.  Set

    s(K,𝔣) := Σ_n (−1)^n F_n ∈ D_A .                                (8A.0)

`Corr_A^{tr}` carries `⊕` (concatenate presentations), shift
(`𝔣[1]_n = F_{n+1}`), cone (`Cone(f)_n = F^L_n ⊕ F^K_{n+1}`) and `⋆`
(convolution of presentations, totalized), and

    s(K⊕L) = s(K)+s(L),   s(K[1]) = −s(K),
    s(Cone f) = s(L)−s(K),   s(K⋆L) = s(K)⋆s(L).                   (8A.0′)

*Proof of (8A.0′).*  The first three are immediate from the term
descriptions.  For the last, the `n`-th term of `K⋆L` is
`⊕_{i+j=n} E_{F_i ⋆ G_j}` by Corollary 3.4, so
`s(K⋆L) = Σ_n(−1)^n Σ_{i+j=n}F_i⋆G_j = (Σ_i(−1)^iF_i)⋆(Σ_j(−1)^jG_j)`,
absolute rearrangement being legitimate because the families are finite.  ∎

**Remark 8A.1a (what is and is not claimed about `s`).**  `s` is defined on
objects of `Corr_A^{tr}`, i.e. on **presented** complexes, and `Tr_A` below is
therefore well defined on those objects.  Whether `s` descends to
isomorphism classes of the underlying complexes — equivalently whether
`E_F ≅ E_G` forces `F=G` — is **not** proved here; I do not know it, and
Remark 3.5 already records that `E_F` is not an object of `C_tw(U)`, so the
classification (3.3) does not apply to it.  This is obstacle (O-A5-4).  The
generators `E_a` themselves are not in `Corr_A^{tr}`: a point mass is not a
compactly supported smooth section, which is the honest content of
"trace-class domain" here and the reason Theorem 9.5 is stated for sections.

### 8A.2 The target

**Definition 8A.2.**  `T_A := Pic_b(C)`, the Picard groupoid of **based**
one-dimensional complex lines (P123.012 §3), a determinant target in the sense
of clause A.5.  The trace is

    Tr_A : Corr_A^{tr} → T_A,   Tr_A(K) := Det_S^{rel}(s(K)) = (C, e^{T(s(K))}),
                                                                    (8A.10)

with scalar shadow `T(K) := T(s(K)) ∈ C`.

**Definition 8A.2′ (the target, enlarged — [REBUILT, phase 130]).**  The
determinant target of row A is

    T_A := Pic_b(B_T),  B_T = C_−⊕C_+ ,                             (8A.10′)

the Picard groupoid of **based invertible `B_T`-modules**, equivalently of
**pairs** of based one-dimensional complex lines, with `♯` of P123.008 (2.1)
exchanging the two factors.  This is a determinant target in the sense of
clause A.5 — it is the target of the Knudsen–Mumford determinant of
P123.008 §6, where a finite-cell `B_T`-module has two determinant lines and
`♯` exchanges them.  The scalar target of Definition 8A.2 embeds as the first
factor,

    Pic_b(C) ↪ T_A ,  L ↦ (L, \text{unit}) ,

and `Tr_A` takes its values there.  The enlargement is **forced**, not
convenient: Theorem 10.8 proves that the scalar factor alone is trivial on the
ruling plane, so no scalar determinant can extend the ruling determinant
`λ_{per}`.  *Normalization of norms, fixed once and for all:* a based line
`(C,s)`, `s ∈ C^×`, carries the metric

    ‖1‖ := |s|^{−1/2} ,   equivalently   ‖1‖² = |s|^{−1} .          (8A.10″)

With this convention `Det_S^{rel}(F⋆G^†) = (C, e^{I_A(F,G)})` has
`‖1‖² = e^{−\mathrm{Re}\,I_A(F,G)}`, which is the convention of
`A_MIXED_METRIC_WORK` (6)–(7) ("it also fixes the norm convention:
`||1||=exp(-I(F,F)/2)`"); and the fixed ruling line of Paper 42, of norm
`\exp(−d_1d_2)`, is `λ_{per}` of Definition 10.6a below.  Every metric
statement in §§10–14 is in this convention.  No finite scalar **degree** is
asserted anywhere in this paper, so the clause's degree requirement is vacuous
here; the two Tate functionals of §9.1 are degrees on `D_A`, not on `T_A`.

### 8A.3 The laws

**Theorem 8A.3 (additivity).**  `Tr_A(K⊕L) ≅ Tr_A(K)⊗Tr_A(L)`,
`Tr_A(K[1]) ≅ Tr_A(K)^{−1}`, and for a distinguished triangle
`K→L→Cone→[1]` in `Corr_A^{tr}`, `Tr_A(Cone) ≅ Tr_A(L)⊗Tr_A(K)^{−1}`.
Equivalently `T(K⊕L)=T(K)+T(L)`, `T(K[1])=−T(K)`, `T(Cone(f))=T(L)−T(K)`.

*Proof.*  The three identities for `s` are (8A.0′).  `T` is linear on `D_A`: the limit
(8.4) is a finite sum of integrals of `F` against fixed distributions, each
linear in `F`.  Multiplicativity of the based line is P123.012 (3.2)–(3.3),
`d_S^{rel}(h+k)=d_S^{rel}(h)d_S^{rel}(k)`, proved there from
`det_F(exp A exp B)=det_F(exp A)det_F(exp B)` for trace-class `A,B`, whether or
not they commute.  ∎

**Theorem 8A.4 (cyclicity, and `†`-reality).**
`Tr_A(K⋆L) ≅ Tr_A(L⋆K)` for all `K,L ∈ Corr_A^{tr}`; and
`Tr_A(K^†) ≅ conj(Tr_A(K))`, i.e. `T(K^†) = conj(T(K))`.

*Proof.*  *Cyclicity.*  `s(K⋆L) = s(K)⋆s(L)` and `s(L⋆K)=s(L)⋆s(K)` by
(8A.0′).  `C_S` is abelian, so the convolution algebra `D_A` is commutative
and `s(K)⋆s(L)=s(L)⋆s(K)`; apply `T`.  Independently, P123.012 (3.5) proves
`Det_S^{rel}(f⋆g) ≅ Det_S^{rel}(g⋆f)` for convolution tests, listed under its
`PROVED-IN-REPOSITORY` heading "additivity, dagger reality, cyclicity and
gauge (inner/principal) invariance of the limiting determinant character".
*Honesty note:* on the graph sector cyclicity is therefore **not a strong
constraint** — it holds because the external class group is abelian.  Clause
A.5 asks for it and it is proved; it is not evidence of a noncommutative
trace.

*`†`-reality.*  With the half-density dagger (8.2),
`T(F^†) = Σ_v ∫' conj(F(j_v(x^{-1}))) τ_v(x) d^*x`.  Substituting `x↦x^{−1}`
(multiplicative Haar is inversion-invariant) and using Lemma 8.4,
`τ_v(x^{−1})=τ_v(x)`, gives `conj(Σ_v ∫' F(j_v(x))τ_v(x)d^*x) = conj(T(F))`.
The identity counterterms are real multiples of `F(1)` and
`F^†(1)=conj(F(1))`, so they transform the same way.  For a complex,
`s(K^†) = conj(s(K)^{\,\vee})` degreewise by Definition 5.2, and the shift
signs are preserved since `†` is degree-preserving on `Corr_A^{tr}`.
Cross-check: this is P123.012 (3.4), `Det_S^{rel}(h^†)≅conj(Det_S^{rel}(h))`,
in the other normalization.  ∎

**Theorem 8A.5 (chart comparison: the trace is not chart-invariant, and the
defect is explicit).**  **[REBUILT, phase 129, round 2, finding F1.  The
round-1 Theorem 8A.5 asserted `T_T∘J_{ST}=T_S` for a map `J_{ST}` defined as
"extension by the unit ball".  The auditor is right that no such map existed
as written: extension-by-one of arbitrary lifts is refuted by P123.018 §2, the
displayed formula is not `Γ_T`-invariant (take `γ=3` for `S={∞,2}⊂T={∞,2,3}`),
and P123.018 §2 adds that "projection … also does not automatically descend …
reversing the arrow without a construction is not a repair".  The splitting of
Lemma 3.3 supplies exactly the missing construction — and once the map is
correct, the equality is **false** and is replaced by a comparison formula.]**

Let `S ⊆ T` be finite sets of places, `∞∈S`.  By Lemma 3.3 applied to `S` and
to `T`,

    C_S ≅ R^× × ∏_{p∈S_f} Z_p^× ,   C_T ≅ R^× × ∏_{p∈T_f} Z_p^× ,

and the coordinate projection

    π_{TS} : C_T ↠ C_S                                              (8A.11)

is a continuous surjective group homomorphism with **compact** kernel
`∏_{ℓ∈T_f∖S_f}Z_ℓ^×`.  Hence `J_{ST} := π_{TS}^* : D_A(S) → D_A(T)` is a
well-defined continuous algebra `†`-homomorphism.  Then, for every
`F ∈ D_A(S)`,

    T_T(J_{ST}F) = T_S(F) + (c_T − c_S)·F(1)
                 + Σ_{ℓ∈T_f∖S_f} (log ℓ) Σ_{k≠0} ℓ^{−|k|/2} F( δ_S(ℓ^{−k}) ),
                                                                    (8A.12)

where `δ_S(t) := (t at ∞, t at every p∈S_f) ∈ C_S` and the inner sum is
finite.  On `D_A^{off}(S)` the constant term drops and the correction is `0` if
and only if `F` vanishes on the countable discrete set
`{δ_S(ℓ^{−k}) : k≠0}` for every new place `ℓ`.

**[REBUILT, phase 129, round 3, finding G1:]** round 2 omitted `(c_T−c_S)F(1)`,
silently assuming the identity constant of (8.4) is chart-independent.  It is
not known to be: `c_S` is *defined* as the residue `T_S − Σ_{v∈S}W_v`, which
is exactly what (O-A5-3) leaves open, so nothing in the corpus determines its
dependence on `S`.  I attempted a proof of `c_T = c_S` and failed.  What the
attempt does yield, and what is used below, is that at a **new** place the
test `J_{ST}F` is constant on `Z_ℓ^×`, so the *unit packet* of (8.5) vanishes
identically there — that is why the correction has the displayed pure-shell
shape.  But the identity counterterm of the semilocal cutoff is not the unit
packet, and no argument here separates them.  `c_T = c_S` is folded into
(O-A5-3).  **On `D_A^{off}` the ambiguity disappears**, and that is the only form
of (8A.12) used anywhere.

*Proof.*  *The map exists.*  `π_{TS}` is the projection of a direct product of
groups, hence a homomorphism; it is surjective and its kernel is the compact
group `∏_{ℓ new}Z_ℓ^×`.  Compactness of the kernel is what makes `π^*F`
compactly supported, so `J_{ST}` lands in `D_A(T)`; it preserves smoothness in
the `R^×` coordinate, local constancy, convolution (pullback along a surjective
