homomorphism with compact kernel is multiplicative up to the normalization of
Haar measure, fixed here by giving every `Z_ℓ^×` total mass one) and `†`.
*The P123.018 counterexample does not apply.*  There `S={∞,2}`, `T={∞,2,3}`,
`a=(1,1)`, `a'=(2,2)` represent the same class of `C_S`, and extension-by-one
of the two **lifts** gives `(1,1,1)` and `(2,2,1)`, which are different classes
of `C_T`.  With the splitting, the canonical representatives are computed
first: `φ_S(a)=2^{v_2(1)}=1` so the representative of `[a]` is `(1,1)`, and
`φ_S(a')=2^{v_2(2)}=2` so the representative of `[a']` is `a'/2=(1,1)` as well.
Both give the same point of `C_S`, and the ambiguity that P123.018 exploits is
removed.  `π_{TS}` is moreover defined in the other direction from the map
P123.018 refutes, which is why it is a homomorphism at all.

*The local terms at old places agree.*  Fix `p∈S_f` and `x=p^ku ∈ Q_p^×`.  The
idele with `x` at `p` and `1` elsewhere has `φ_T`-value `p^k`, so its canonical
representative in `C_T` is

    j_{T,p}(x) = ( p^{−k} at ∞, u at p, p^{−k} at every other place of T ).

Applying `π_{TS}` deletes the new coordinates and leaves exactly
`j_{S,p}(x)`.  So `(J_{ST}F)∘j_{T,p} = F∘j_{S,p}` as functions on `Q_p^×`, and
all three ingredients of (8.5) — the shell integral, the unit packet and the
value `a(1)` — coincide.  The same computation at `v=∞` gives
`j_{T,∞}(x)=(x,1,…,1)` and `π_{TS}j_{T,∞}=j_{S,∞}`, so `W_∞` agrees.  This is
the chart statement P123.042 §4 makes ("for `S` contained in `T` and `p` old,
`j_{T,p}` projects to `j_{S,p}`.  Hence … all operators and relative traces in
(3.2) coincide on these charts"), and P123.046 §4 makes at the Hilbert-space
level; the splitting is what turns it into an identity of class-group maps.

*The local terms at new places.*  Fix `ℓ ∈ T_f∖S_f` and `x=ℓ^k u`.  As above
`j_{T,ℓ}(x) = (ℓ^{−k} at ∞, u at ℓ, ℓ^{−k} at every other place of T)`, so

    a(x) := (J_{ST}F)(j_{T,ℓ}(x)) = F( δ_S(ℓ^{−k}) ),

independent of `u`.  Hence `a` is constant on each valuation shell.  For `k=0`
this gives `a(u)=F(δ_S(1))=F(1)=a(1)` for every `u∈Z_ℓ^×`, so the unit packet
of (8.5) vanishes identically.  For `k≠0`, `τ_ℓ(ℓ^ku)=ℓ^{−|k|/2}` by
Lemma 8.2(i), and the shell `ℓ^kZ_ℓ^×` has `d^*x`-volume `log ℓ` (P123.042 §1),
so the shell integral is `(log ℓ)Σ_{k≠0}ℓ^{−|k|/2}F(δ_S(ℓ^{−k}))`.  Only
finitely many `k` contribute, `F` having compact support in the `R^×`
coordinate and `|δ_S(ℓ^{−k})|_S = ℓ^{−k}`.  Summing over `v∈T` and using
(8.4) — **[REBUILT, phase 129, round 4, finding F2: the round-3 proof still
asserted here that `c` is "the same on both sides, being a property of the
cutoff family and not of `S`", contradicting its own statement.  That clause
is deleted; `c_S` and `c_T` are declared constants of the two charts and
nothing relates them.]**  Since `(J_{ST}F)(1)=F(1)`, the residue is
`(c_T−c_S)F(1)`, left unevaluated, and (8A.12) follows.  ∎

**Remark 8A.5a (reading (8A.12), and obstacle (O-A5-2)).**  **[REBUILT, phase
129, round 2.]**  The defect is not an error: enlarging the chart adds places,
and a sum of local fixed-point contributions must gain their local factors.
(8A.12) is the exact statement of that gain, and it is the arithmetically
expected one: the new factor is `(log ℓ)Σ_{k≠0}ℓ^{−|k|/2}F(δ_S(ℓ^{−k}))`,
i.e. the prime `ℓ` entering with its half-density weights.  But clause A.5 asks
for a trace "compatible with restriction", and if that is read as
**invariance**, then:

  **(O-A5-2)**  No chart-invariant trace is constructed on this domain, and
  (8A.12) shows that `T_S` is not the restriction of a single global object
  along these maps.  What is missing is either (i) a target `T_A` enlarged so
  that the chart maps are not the identity on it — a colimit or a Deligne-type
  product over places carrying the omitted factors — or (ii) a proof that the
  correction terms vanish on the subspace of `D_A(S)` that the numerical
  theory actually needs.  Neither is constructed.  On `D_A^{off}` the correction
  does **not** automatically vanish, since `δ_S(ℓ^{−k})≠1`.

**[REBUILT, phase 130: superseded by Theorem 8A.7c, which constructs a trace
that is chart-invariant on the nose over the colimit chart `D_A^∞`; the
paragraph is kept as the statement of (O-A5-2).]**
I therefore record clause A.5's restriction requirement as **PARTIAL**: the
chart maps exist and are homomorphisms (new, and not available before Lemma
3.3), the comparison formula (8A.12) is proved and explicit, and invariance is
false.


**Remark 8A.6.**  The appeal, in versions before this one, to P123.014 §4 for a
restriction functor on **objects** remains withdrawn: P123.018 §2 refutes it,
and Theorem 8A.5 does not restore it.  What Theorem 8A.5 gives is a map of
**test algebras** and an explicit comparison of their traces.

**Theorem 8A.7 (determinant compatibility, pointwise; the functorial statement
is `OPEN`).**  **[REBUILT, phase 129, round 2, finding F7.]**

*Proved, for each fixed `F ∈ D_A`:*  `exp T(F)` is the limit of renormalized
**Fredholm determinants of trace-class operators**,

    e^{T(F)} = lim_{Λ→∞} det_F( exp( θ(F)R_Λ ) ) · e^{−2F(1)logΛ},   (8A.13)

**[REBUILT, phase 130, round 2, finding F13 of `R130.002`: (8A.13) as
displayed is **superseded** and must not be used.  With `c := 0` declared
(Definition 8A.7d) the imported limit equals `e^{T_{an}(F)}` and
`T_{an} = T + c_{cut}F(1)` with `c_{cut}` undetermined, so the correct
statement is (8A.13′) of Remark 8A.7e.  (8A.13) is exact on `D_A^{off}`, where
`F(1)=0`.  Nothing in this paper uses either form.]**

the limit exists and is nonzero, and (8A.10) is the corresponding based line
(P123.012 §2, (2.1)–(2.4)).  `L_S(F,G) := Det_S^{rel}(F⋆G^†)` is additive in
`F`, conjugate-additive in `G`, Hermitian under exchange, with logarithmic
determinant character `I_A(F,G) = T(F⋆G^†)` (P123.012 §4).  Gauge/principal
invariance `Det_S^{rel}(a⋆F⋆a^{−1}) ≅ Det_S^{rel}(F)` holds **under the
hypothesis P123.012 §5 states**: "If `a` is such a multiplier **and the
commutators remain in the trace domain**".  On `D_A` that hypothesis is
vacuous, because `D_A` is commutative (Corollary 3.4) and the conjugation is
trivial; so principal invariance is proved here, but it is proved for a reason
that makes it empty, and it must not be transported to a noncommutative
enlargement without checking the commutator condition.

*Open.*  What clause A.5 wants from "compatible with determinant" is that
`Tr_A` be a determinant **on the completed category**, i.e. continuous for a
trace-class topology on the sections.  That is exactly the gate the two
relevant notes declare open:

* P123.014 §7: "The next gate has one descent and one analytic part: construct
  the line twist/splitting which lifts scalar tests faithfully into (5.1),
  then **put a complete trace-class topology on the resulting compact sections
  and prove that the relative determinant of P123.012 is continuous for it.**"
  (Lemma 3.3 supplies the splitting half of that gate; the topology half it
  does not.)
* P123.012 §7, listing why A.8 "is still not green": "1. the determinant is
  not yet proved continuous on the complete rapid infinite-label space".

  **(O-A5-1) = (A.8-III)**  A complete trace-class topology on `D_A` for which
  `F ↦ Det_S^{rel}(F)` is continuous, hence a determinant functor on the
  completion rather than a family of values on a dense core.  Not constructed.  **[REBUILT, phase 130: CLOSED.  The topology is
  `‖·‖_tr` of (8A.15) and the completion is the Banach `†`-algebra `𝓐_tr`;
  see Theorem 8A.7b.  The paragraph above is kept as the statement of the
  obstacle that §8A.3bis answers.]**

**[REBUILT, phase 129, round 4, findings F3 and F4.  Round 3 wrote that the
conductor seminorms "reduce the obstacle to a precise estimate" and that it is
"no longer a structural unknown".  That overstates it, and the overstatement
is withdrawn: P123.105 is proved for locally constant tests **on the units
only**, its §4 disclaims the non-unit shells ("Nonunit dilations do not have
that commutation property and their products mix valuation shells.  Their
determinant and its coupling to the tangential induced action require a
further calculation"), and P123.107 §5 disclaims multiplicativity.  What
follows is a genuine but partial estimate, for the **trace** and at the
**finite** places.]**

**Proposition 8A.7a (the conductor seminorms dominate the finite-place
trace).**  Let `p ∈ S_f` and let `a = F∘j_p` have shell–character Fourier
coefficients `a_{k,κ}` as in (2.2).  Then

    |W_p(F)| ≤ (log p)·[ Σ_{k≠0} p^{−|k|/2}|a_{k,1}| + Σ_κ c_p(κ)|a_{0,κ}| ]
             ≤ (log p)·[ v_{0,0}(a) + v_{0,1}(a) ] .                (8A.14)

*Proof.*  Split (8.5).  The shell part is
`(log p)Σ_{k≠0}p^{−|k|/2}∫_{Z_p^×}a(p^ku)du = (log p)Σ_{k≠0}p^{−|k|/2}a_{k,1}`
by orthogonality.  The unit packet is
`(log p)∫_U(a(u)−a(1))/|1−u|_p du = −(log p)Σ_κ c_p(κ)a_{0,κ}` by (9.14),
i.e. by P123.105 §3 (4)–(5).  Now `v_{0,0}(a)=Σ_{k,κ}|a_{k,κ}|` dominates the
first sum (`p^{−|k|/2}≤1`) and `v_{0,1}(a)=Σ_{k,κ}c_p(κ)|a_{k,κ}|` dominates
the second.  **[The round-3 display bounded both by `v_{0,1}` alone, which is
false: `c_p(1)=0`, so `v_{0,1}` does not see the `κ=1` coefficients that carry
the shell sum.  Corrected.]**  ∎

So `T` is continuous at the finite places for `v_{0,0}+v_{0,1}`, and P123.121
§1 proves `v_{l,N}(fg) ≤ v_{l,N}(f)v_{l,N}(g)`, so these seminorms are
submultiplicative **there**.  At the archimedean place the relevant seminorms
are the `E_R` seminorms `sup_t e^{l|t|}|D^mf|` of P123.121 §1(5), and I have
not proved them submultiplicative; nor is `W_∞` bounded by (8A.14).  Hence:

  **(O-A5-1a) — an inequality.**  `|Σ_χ c(χ)a_χ^r| ≤ C ρ^r` with `ρ<1` in
  terms of `v_{0,0}+v_{0,1}`, uniformly in `r`, so that P123.107 (6)'s germ
  `log Δ_φ(z) = −Σ_{r≥1}(−1)^{r+1}z^r[Σ_χ c(χ)a_χ^r]/r` is holomorphic and
  continuous on a ball.  The case `r=1` is (8A.14).  This one really is an
  estimate.
  **(O-A5-1b) — a construction.**  Determinant germs for the **non-unit
  shells** and for the **archimedean** place, and their multiplicativity.
  Neither exists: P123.105 §4 disclaims the first ("Nonunit dilations do not
  have that commutation property … require a further calculation"), and
  P123.107 §5 disclaims multiplicativity of the germ it constructs.  This is
  not an inequality; it is missing mathematics, and it is the larger half of
  (O-A5-1).  **[REBUILT, phase 130: both parts are now proved,
  Theorem 8A.7b(d)–(f).  The round-4 assessment was correct about the
  **operator-cutoff** germ, which is what P123.105 §4 and P123.107 §5 discuss,
  and wrong to identify that germ with the determinant of this theory; see
  Remark 8A.7b1.]**

So determinant compatibility is `PROVED` pointwise on `D_A` and `OPEN` as a
statement about the completion.

### 8A.3bis The trace Banach algebra `𝓐_tr`, and the determinant on it

**[REBUILT, phase 130.  This subsection closes (O-A5-1a) and (O-A5-1b).  The
key observation is that the obstacle was posed for the wrong determinant: the
germ that P123.105 §4 and P123.107 §5 disclaim is the **operator-cutoff** germ
`det(1+zR_Λ(F))`, whose non-unit shells genuinely require a further
calculation; the determinant of this theory is P123.012's **exp-type**
renormalized relative determinant `exp T`, and Theorem 9.7(d) has already
performed the cutoff limit for it, in the shape of the symbol `T̂`.  On the
symbol side the estimate is elementary and covers all shells and all places at
once.]**

**Definition 8A.7b0 (the trace norm).**  **[REBUILT, phase 130, round 2,
finding F1 of `R130.002`.  The round-1 norm was `‖F‖_{L^1} + N(F)`, for which
`λ_±` are **unbounded** — the spread Tate characters of (9.21) have
`‖F_n‖_{L^1} → 0`, `N(F_n) → 0` and `λ_+(F_n) = 1` — so `M` had no continuous
extension and the Tate component of `λ_{RR}` was undefined on the completion.
The norm is corrected by adding the two Tate weights, which are multiplicative
characters and therefore keep the algebra submultiplicative with constant 1.]**
For `F ∈ D_A` put

    N(F) := ∫_{Ĉ_S} |F̂(χ)|(|T̂(χ)|+|c|)dχ ,
    Λ(F) := ∫_{C_S} |F(w)|(|w|_S^{1/2}+|w|_S^{−1/2}) d^*w ,
    ‖F‖_tr := Λ(F) + N(F) .                                         (8A.15)

`N(F) < ∞` by Lemma 9.14a0 and `Λ(F) < ∞` because `F` has compact support;
`‖·‖_tr` is a norm, and by AM–GM (`|w|^{1/2}+|w|^{−1/2} ≥ 2`)

    ‖F‖_{L^1} ≤ ½Λ(F) ≤ ½‖F‖_tr ,   |λ_∓(F)| ≤ Λ(F) ≤ ‖F‖_tr .      (8A.15′)

Let `𝓐_tr` be the completion of `D_A` for `‖·‖_tr`.

**Theorem 8A.7b (`𝓐_tr` is a Banach `†`-algebra; `T` and `Det_S^{rel}` are
continuous on it; the determinant germ exists for every `F` and is exactly
multiplicative).**

  (a) *Banach algebra.*  For all `F,G ∈ D_A`,

        ‖F⋆G‖_tr ≤ ‖F‖_tr ‖G‖_tr ,   ‖F^†‖_tr = ‖F‖_tr .            (8A.16)

      Hence `𝓐_tr` is a commutative Banach `†`-algebra.  The natural map
      `𝓐_tr → L^1(C_S)` is injective, so `𝓐_tr` is **realized as a
      subalgebra** of `L^1(C_S)`, contained in
      `\{H ∈ L^1 : Λ(H)+N(H) < ∞\}` with the norm (8A.15).  **[REBUILT,
      phase 130, round 2, finding F10: equality with that space would require
      density of `D_A` in it, which is not proved; the statement is an
      inclusion.]**
  (a′) *The Tate characters extend.*  **[REBUILT, phase 130, round 2,
      finding F1.]**  `|λ_∓(F)| ≤ ‖F‖_tr` by (8A.15′), so `λ_−,λ_+` extend
      uniquely to continuous linear functionals on `𝓐_tr`, of norm `≤ 1`;
      they remain multiplicative (`λ_∓(H⋆K) = λ_∓(H)λ_∓(K)` by continuity from
      Theorem 9.1), satisfy `λ_∓(H^†) = \overline{λ_±(H)}`, and therefore
      `M = (λ_−,λ_+) : 𝓐_tr → B_T` is a continuous unital-free `†`-algebra
      homomorphism extending that of Theorem 9.1.  Consequently the
      **Tate component of `λ_{RR}` is defined and continuous on `𝓐_tr`**,
      which is what Definition 10.6c and Theorem 10.7(b) need.
  (b) *The trace extends.*  `T(F) = ∫ F̂(T̂+c)dχ` satisfies `|T(F)| ≤ N(F) ≤
      ‖F‖_tr`, so `T` extends uniquely to a continuous linear functional on
      `𝓐_tr`, still given by that integral, and `Det_S^{rel} = \exp T` extends
      to a continuous character `𝓐_tr → C^×` for the additive structure:
      `Det_S^{rel}(H+K) = Det_S^{rel}(H)Det_S^{rel}(K)`.
  (c) *The power estimate.*  For every `H ∈ 𝓐_tr` and every `r ≥ 1`,

        |T(H^{⋆r})| ≤ ‖H‖_{L^1}^{\,r−1}·N(H) ≤ ‖H‖_{L^1}^{\,r−1}‖H‖_tr .
                                                                    (8A.17)

  (d) *The determinant germ.*  For `|z| < 1/‖F‖_{L^1}` the series

        \log Δ_F(z) := Σ_{r≥1} (−1)^{r+1} z^r T(F^{⋆r})/r            (8A.18)

      converges absolutely, is holomorphic in `z` on that disc, continuous in
      `F` for `‖·‖_tr`, and equals

        \log Δ_F(z) = ∫_{Ĉ_S} \mathrm{Log}(1+zF̂(χ))(T̂(χ)+c)dχ ,     (8A.19)

      `Log` the principal branch (legitimate: `|zF̂| ≤ |z|‖F‖_{L^1} < 1`).
  (e) *Exact multiplicativity.*  For `F,G ∈ 𝓐_tr` and
      `|z| < \min(1/‖F‖_{L^1},1/‖G‖_{L^1})`, with
      `H := F+G+zF⋆G`,

        Δ_F(z)·Δ_G(z) = Δ_H(z),                                     (8A.20)

      where `Δ_H(z) := \exp∫\mathrm{Log}(1+zĤ)(T̂+c)dχ`; and
      `Δ_{F^†}(\bar z) = \overline{Δ_F(z)}`.
  (f) *(O-A5-1a) holds.*  For every `p ∈ S_f` and every `F ∈ 𝓐_tr`,
      `|Σ_κ c_p(κ)a_κ^{(r)}| ≤ ‖F‖_{L^1}^{r−1}·(\log p)^{−1}N_p^{u}(F)`
      uniformly in `r`, where `a^{(r)}` are the unit-block Fourier
      coefficients of `F^{⋆r}` and `N_p^u(F) := ∫|F̂|c_p(κ_p)\log p\,dχ ≤
      N(F)`.  Consequently the germ of P123.107 (6) is holomorphic on the disc
      `|z| < 1/‖F‖_{L^1}` — after the harmless rescaling `F ↦ ρF/‖F‖_{L^1}`
      the obstacle's literal form, `|Σ_κ c(κ)a_κ^r| ≤ Cρ^r` with `ρ<1`, holds
— and it is exactly the unit-packet component of (8A.19).

*Proof.*  **(a)**  **[REBUILT, phase 130, round 2.]**  For `s = ±½` write
`‖F‖_{1,s} := ∫|F(w)||w|_S^{s}d^*w`, so `Λ = ‖·‖_{1,1/2}+‖·‖_{1,−1/2}`.  Since
`w ↦ |w|_S^{s}` is a **multiplicative character** of `C_S`,
`|F⋆G(w)||w|^s ≤ ∫|F(u)||u|^s|G(u^{−1}w)||u^{−1}w|^s d^*u`, and Fubini gives
the weighted Young inequality `‖F⋆G‖_{1,s} ≤ ‖F‖_{1,s}‖G‖_{1,s}`.  Adding the
two values of `s`,

    Λ(F⋆G) ≤ ‖F‖_{1,1/2}‖G‖_{1,1/2} + ‖F‖_{1,−1/2}‖G‖_{1,−1/2} ≤ Λ(F)Λ(G),

the last step because all four quantities are nonnegative.  Since
`(F⋆G)^∧ = F̂Ĝ` and `‖Ĝ‖_∞ ≤ ‖G‖_{L^1} ≤ ½Λ(G)` (Riemann–Lebesgue and
(8A.15′)),

    N(F⋆G) = ∫|F̂||Ĝ|(|T̂|+|c|)dχ ≤ ½Λ(G)N(F) ≤ Λ(G)N(F).

Adding, `‖F⋆G‖_tr ≤ Λ(G)(Λ(F)+N(F)) = Λ(G)‖F‖_tr ≤ ‖F‖_tr‖G‖_tr`: the
constant is **1**.  For the involution, `d^*w` is inversion-invariant and
`|w^{−1}|_S^{s} = |w|_S^{−s}`, so the substitution `w ↦ w^{−1}` exchanges the
two weights and `Λ(F^†) = Λ(F)`; and `(F^†)^∧ = \overline{F̂}` (computed in the
proof of Theorem 9.8), so `N(F^†)=N(F)`.  *Realization as an inclusion.*  A
`‖·‖_tr`-Cauchy sequence `(F_j)` is `L^1`-Cauchy, hence converges in `L^1` to
some `H`, and `F̂_j → Ĥ` uniformly; it is also Cauchy in
`L^1((|T̂|+|c|)dχ)` and in `L^1((|w|^{1/2}+|w|^{−1/2})d^*w)`, and by Fatou the
limits are `Ĥ` and `H` with `Λ(H)+N(H) ≤ \liminf‖F_j‖_tr`.  So the map to
`L^1` is injective and lands in the stated space.
**(a′)**  `|λ_∓(F)| = |∫F|w|^{∓1/2}d^*w| ≤ ‖F‖_{1,∓1/2} ≤ Λ(F) ≤ ‖F‖_tr`,
whence the unique continuous extension of norm `≤ 1`.  Multiplicativity,
the `†`-rule and surjectivity onto `B_T` pass to the completion because both
sides of each identity are `‖·‖_tr`-continuous and agree on the dense
subalgebra `D_A` (Theorem 9.1).

**(b)**  The bound is immediate from (9.13) and (8A.15), and additivity of
`T` gives the character property.  Continuity of `\exp` finishes it.

**(c)**  `(H^{⋆r})^∧ = Ĥ^r`, so `|T(H^{⋆r})| ≤ ∫|Ĥ|^r(|T̂|+|c|)dχ ≤
‖Ĥ‖_∞^{r−1}N(H) ≤ ‖H‖_{L^1}^{r−1}N(H)`.

**(d)**  By (c), `Σ_r |z|^r|T(F^{⋆r})|/r ≤ N(F)Σ_r(|z|‖F‖_{L^1})^{r}/(r‖F‖_{L^1})
< ∞` for `|z|‖F‖_{L^1} < 1`, giving absolute convergence and holomorphy
(a locally uniformly convergent power series).  Continuity in `F`: each
`T(F^{⋆r})` is `‖·‖_tr`-continuous by (a)–(b), and the tail bound is uniform on
`‖·‖_tr`-bounded sets.  For (8A.19), insert `T(F^{⋆r}) = ∫F̂^r(T̂+c)dχ` and
interchange sum and integral; this is legitimate by dominated convergence,
since `Σ_r|z F̂|^r/r ≤ |z||F̂|/(1−|z|‖F‖_{L^1})` and `|F̂|(|T̂|+|c|)` is
integrable, and `Σ_r(−1)^{r+1}(zF̂)^r/r = \mathrm{Log}(1+zF̂)` pointwise.

**(e)**  For each `χ`, `1+zF̂(χ)` and `1+zĜ(χ)` lie in the open disc of radius
`1` centred at `1`, hence in the right half plane with argument in
`(−π/2,π/2)`; so the sum of their principal logarithms has imaginary part in
`(−π,π)` and therefore equals the principal logarithm of the product,
`\mathrm{Log}((1+zF̂)(1+zĜ)) = \mathrm{Log}(1+zĤ)` with
`Ĥ = F̂+Ĝ+zF̂Ĝ = (F+G+zF⋆G)^∧`.  Integrating against `(T̂+c)dχ` and
exponentiating gives (8A.20).  Reality: `T̂+c` is real (Theorem 9.7(c)) and
`(F^†)^∧ = \overline{F̂}`, so
`∫\mathrm{Log}(1+\bar z\overline{F̂})(T̂+c)dχ = \overline{∫\mathrm{Log}(1+zF̂)(T̂+c)dχ}`.

**(f)**  By (9.11) the unit-packet part of the symbol at `p` is the **bounded**
function `Ŵ_p^{u}(χ) := −c_p(κ_p)\log p`, and by Proposition 8A.7a's proof the
unit packet of `W_p(H)` equals `∫Ĥ Ŵ_p^{u}dχ = −(\log p)Σ_κ c_p(κ)a_κ(H)`.
Applying this to `H = F^{⋆r}` and estimating as in (c),
`|(\log p)Σ_κ c_p(κ)a^{(r)}_κ| = |∫F̂^r Ŵ_p^u dχ| ≤ ‖F‖_{L^1}^{r−1}N_p^u(F)`,
and `N_p^u(F) = ∫|F̂|c_p(κ_p)\log p\,dχ < ∞` by the same argument as
Lemma 9.14a0 (the integrand is `|F̂|`, integrable over the finitely many
components met by `supp F̂`, times a function bounded on them).  (No inequality
between `N_p^u` and `N` is asserted or needed; `N_p^u` is a seminorm of the
same kind, continuous on `𝓐_tr` by the same estimate.)  Summing the
geometric series gives holomorphy of P123.107 (6)'s germ on
`|z|<1/‖F‖_{L^1}`, and comparison of (8A.19) with the decomposition
`T̂ = Ŵ_∞+Σ_p Ŵ_p` identifies it with the `Ŵ_p^u`-component of (8A.19).  ∎

**Remark 8A.7b0a (why the corrected norm is necessary).**  **[REBUILT, phase
130, round 2, finding F1.]**  With the round-1 norm `‖F‖_{L^1}+N(F)` the
spread Tate characters `F_n` of (9.21) satisfy `‖F_n‖_{L^1} ≤ ‖ψ‖_1e^{−na/2}`
and `N(F_n) = O(n^{−1}\log n)` (Theorem 9.14c(b)'s estimate), hence
`‖F_n‖_{tr} → 0`, while `λ_+(F_n) = 1` for every `n`: `λ_+` was unbounded on
the unit ball and `M` had no continuous extension.  With (8A.15),
`‖F_n‖_{tr} ≥ Λ(F_n) ≥ λ_+(F_n) = 1`, and the obstruction is removed — indeed
(8A.15′) makes `‖M‖ ≤ 1`.  The added term is not a device: `Λ` is the natural
`L^1` norm of the **Tate-twisted** test, and it is exactly the quantity that
Theorem 10.3b's AM–GM step already used.

**Remark 8A.7b1 (what is and is not claimed, against P123.105 §4 and
P123.107 §5).**  **[REBUILT, phase 130.]**  P123.105 §4 says: "Nonunit
dilations do not have that commutation property and their products mix
valuation shells.  Their determinant and its coupling to the tangential
induced action require a further calculation."  P123.107 §5 disclaims
multiplicativity of the germ it constructs.  **Both statements are about the
operator-cutoff germ `z ↦ det(1+zR_Λ(F))` and its `Λ→∞` behaviour**, where a
Szegő-type anomaly may occur, and this paper does **not** claim anything about
it.  The determinant of row A is `Det_S^{rel} = \exp T` of P123.012 (2.2)–(2.4)
— an `\exp`-type determinant with the generic-orbit countercharacter already
subtracted — and Theorem 9.7(d) expresses it by the symbol.  Theorem 8A.7b
constructs its germ, for **all** shells and the archimedean place at once, and
proves multiplicativity (8A.20) exactly.  On the unit block the two germs
agree, by (f), which recovers P123.105 (3)–(6).  The distinction is recorded
because conflating the two determinants is what made (O-A5-1b) look like
missing mathematics.

**Obstacles (O-A5-1a) and (O-A5-1b) are CLOSED**, by Theorem 8A.7b(f) and
Theorem 8A.7b(d)–(e) respectively; and with them **(A.8-III)**.

### 8A.3ter The colimit chart, the chart-invariant trace, and the constant

**[REBUILT, phase 130.  This closes (O-A5-2), (O-A5-3), (O-A1-1) and
(O-A1-2).]**

**Theorem 8A.7c (the colimit trace).**  For `F ∈ D_A(S)` let
`R(F) := \sup\{|\log|w|_S| : w ∈ supp F\} < ∞` be its **radius**.  Then:

  (a) *Support window.*  For `ℓ ∉ S_f` with `\log ℓ > R(F)`, the correction
      term of (8A.12) vanishes identically:
      `(\log ℓ)Σ_{k≠0}ℓ^{−|k|/2}F(δ_S(ℓ^{−k})) = 0`.
  (b) *Stabilization.*  Consequently, if `c` is declared chart-independently
      (see Definition 8A.7d), then for all finite `T ⊇ T_0(F) := S ∪ \{ℓ :
      \log ℓ ≤ R(F)\}` the value `T_T(J_{ST}F)` is **independent of `T`**.
      Write `T_∞(F)` for this common value; explicitly

        T_∞(F) = T_S(F) + Σ_{ℓ∉S_f,\ \log ℓ ≤ R(F)} (\log ℓ)
                   Σ_{k≠0} ℓ^{−|k|/2} F(δ_S(ℓ^{−k})),                (8A.21)

      a finite sum.
  (c) *Exact chart invariance.*  `T_∞ ∘ J_{ST} = T_∞` for all `S ⊆ T`.
      Hence, on the filtered colimit of `†`-algebras

        D_A^∞ := \mathrm{colim}_{S} D_A(S)   (transition maps J_{ST}),  (8A.22)

      `T_∞` is a well-defined linear `†`-real functional, and
      `I_∞(F,G) := T_∞(F⋆G^†)` is a well-defined Hermitian form.
  (d) *The Tate boundary is chart-invariant already.*  `M ∘ J_{ST} = M`, so
      `M` descends to `D_A^∞`, and (9.17) holds verbatim over `D_A^∞`.
  (e) *Window comparison.*  If `R(F⋆G^†) < \log ℓ_{next}(S)`, where
      `ℓ_{next}(S)` is the least prime not in `S_f`, then
      `I_∞(F,G) = I_A^S(F,G)` exactly.

*Proof.*  **(a)**  `δ_S(ℓ^{−k}) ∈ C_S` has `|δ_S(ℓ^{−k})|_S = ℓ^{−k}` by
Lemma 3.3, so `|\log|δ_S(ℓ^{−k})|_S| = |k|\log ℓ ≥ \log ℓ > R(F)` for `k ≠ 0`;
hence `F` vanishes there.  **(b)**  Apply (8A.12) for `S ⊆ T`: the constant
term `(c_T−c_S)F(1)` vanishes when `c` is chart-independent, and the sum over
`ℓ ∈ T_f∖S_f` is by (a) the finite sum over `\log ℓ ≤ R(F)` only, which is the
same for every `T ⊇ T_0(F)`.  **(c)**  Both sides equal `T_U(J_{SU}(F))` for
any `U ⊇ T_0(F) ∪ T`, using `J_{TU}∘J_{ST} = J_{SU}` (both are pullback along
`π_{US} = π_{TS}∘π_{UT}`) and `R(J_{ST}F) = R(F)` (the chart map preserves
`|·|`, Lemma 3.3).  Well-definedness on the colimit is exactly (c).
`†`-reality and linearity are inherited termwise from Theorem 8A.4.
**(d)**  For `w ∈ C_T` the modulus `|w|_T` equals the modulus of its
archimedean coordinate, as does `|π_{TS}(w)|_S`; and Haar on `C_T` is
`dt ⊗ (\text{probability})`, as on `C_S`.  Hence
`λ_∓(J_{ST}F) = ∫_{C_T}F(π_{TS}w)|w|_T^{∓1/2}d^*w = ∫_R F_{prof}(t)e^{∓t/2}dt
 = λ_∓(F)`.  **(e)**  is (a) applied to `H = F⋆G^†` with every `ℓ ∉ S_f`.  ∎

**Obstacle (O-A5-2) is CLOSED.**  Remark 8A.5a asked for "(i) a target `T_A`
enlarged so that the chart maps are not the identity on it … or (ii) a proof
that the correction terms vanish on the subspace the numerical theory needs".
Theorem 8A.7c supplies a third and stronger answer: an **enlarged domain**,
`D_A^∞`, carrying a trace that is chart-invariant **on the nose**, together
with the window statement (e) which is (ii) in its exact form.  The comparison
formula (8A.12) is unchanged; it is now read as the statement that `T_S` is the
truncation of `T_∞` to the places of `S`.

**Definition 8A.7d (the constant is declared: `c := 0`).**  **[REBUILT, phase
130.]**  From here on

    c := 0 ,  so  T(F) = Σ_{v∈S} W_v(F),  T̂ = Ŵ_∞ + Σ_{p∈S_f}Ŵ_p.  (8A.23)

**[phase 132]** Four reasons are recorded; **reasons (i)–(iii) are
normalization choices and are not derivations**, and reason (iv), added in
phase 132, **is** a derivation relative to the Weil comparison — more precisely
a *characterization*: `c = 0` is the unique value compatible with it (Paper 45
Cor. 9.6a, Rem. 9.6b(3)).  For reasons (i)–(iii), `c` is a
normalization of a **declared** trace (Definition 8.6), and the point of
declaring it is that nothing is left dangling.

  (i)  Since round 4, `T` is *defined* by (8.4).  `c` is therefore a free
       parameter of the definition, not an unknown of a theorem; leaving it
       free is a choice, and the choice is now made.
  (ii) `c` must be declared **chart-independently** for Theorem 8A.7c to give a
       chart-invariant trace, and `c := 0` does that.  (Uniqueness is *not*
       claimed here: any constant declared uniformly in `S` has the same
       effect.  What `c := 0` adds is (iii).)
  (iii) `c := 0` is the unique choice for which the identity coefficient of the
       symbol is the sum of the **intrinsic** Mittag–Leffler constants of the
       local factors and nothing else: by Theorem 9.7b, `Ŵ_p` carries the
       intrinsic constant `−\log p` and `Ŵ_∞(0,·)` the intrinsic constant
       `\log π + γ`, so `T̂ = Σ_v(\text{local intrinsic constant}) +
       (\text{local pole parts}) + c`, and `c = 0` is the statement that no
       global constant is added on top of the local ones.
  (iv) **[phase 132; replaces the former reason (iv).]**  **It is
       characterized, not chosen:**
       `c = 0` is the **unique** real constant for which the declared trace
       satisfies the Weil comparison `I_∞(Γ_F,Γ_F) = B_nuc(F,F)` on radial
       classes — Paper 45 **Corollary 9.6a**, the difference for any other `c`
       being `c‖F‖²_{L²} ≠ 0`, which does not vanish on the primitive sector.
       (The former reason (iv) cited the convention `χ_A = Σ_pW_p + W_∞` of
       the **old** Paper 45; that assembly is withdrawn in Paper 45 §13 W45.3,
       so the reason is replaced rather than kept.)  Reasons (i)–(iii) are
       independent of this and stand — in particular reason (iii), the
       intrinsic Mittag–Leffler constants, is untouched by the comparison.

*Consequences of the declaration.*  Theorem 9.8 (`𝓝=0`) is unaffected, being
proved for every `c`.  Theorem 14.1(c) now holds unconditionally, so the form
`I_A` is **indefinite** with infinite index on both sides.  `D_A^{off}` statements
are unchanged.  **Obstacle (O-A5-3) is CLOSED as a normalization of this
theory**; what remains is a *comparison* datum, treated next, on which nothing
in this paper depends.

**Remark 8A.7e (the cutoff constant `c_{cut}`: an attempt, and its outcome).**
**[REBUILT, phase 130.]**  Theorem 8.10 produces, from the imported semilocal
cutoff theorem `C`, a functional `T_{an} = Σ_v W_v + c_{an}F(1)`.  With
`c := 0` the comparison constant is `c_{cut} := c_{an}`, and Theorem 8.10 says
`T_{an} = T + c_{cut}F(1)`.  I attempted to compute `c_{cut}` from P123.046's
declared cutoff and P123.035 §3's folding, as follows.  P123.046 §1 converts
the normal half-density to multiplicative Haar by the unitary
`(T_pv)(x) = \sqrt{c_p|x|_p}v(x)`, `c_p = 1−p^{−1}` (the **additive**
normalization), after which the uncut normal representation is the
multiplicative regular representation; its finite-place counterterm is
therefore a multiple of `(\log Λ)F(1)` with the multiple fixed by the number of
places, not by `c_p`.  At the real place P123.035 §3 folds `x` and `−x` and
retains "exactly one half of the sum of the two real kernels", which is the
factor `½` declared in Remark 8.6a.  Combining the two would give `c_{cut}` as
the difference between `C`'s identity counterterm `2F(1)\logΛ` and the
identity counterterms of (8.5)/(8.5∞) — but `C`'s own coefficient is exactly
what the corpus forbids using: P123.004 §4 records that "its real coefficient
cannot be imported without reconciling full-line Haar and even-function
conventions", and P123.035 §6 states the stop rule.  **The computation
therefore does not close, and I do not claim a value.**  `c_{cut}` stays a
comparison constant of Theorem 8.10.

*A correction this forces, and it is a real one.*  Theorem 8A.7's display
(8A.13) asserts `e^{T(F)} = \lim_Λ \det_F(\exp(θ(F)R_Λ))e^{−2F(1)\logΛ}`.  With
`c := 0` that is proved only **up to the undetermined factor
`e^{−c_{cut}F(1)}`**: what import `C` gives is
`e^{T_{an}(F)} = \lim_Λ(\dots)`, and `T_{an} = T + c_{cut}F(1)`.  Accordingly
(8A.13) is corrected to

    e^{T(F)}·e^{c_{cut}F(1)} = \lim_{Λ→∞}\det_F(\exp(θ(F)R_Λ))e^{−2F(1)\logΛ},
                                                                   (8A.13′)

an identity on all of `D_A` with one undetermined real constant, and an exact
identity on `D_A^{off}`, where `F(1)=0`.  **Nothing in this paper uses (8A.13)**:
the determinant of the theory is defined by `\exp T` (Definition 8A.2) and its
analytic properties are Theorem 8A.7b, which is import-free.  (8A.13′) records
the comparison with the imported operator determinant and no more.

### 8A.3quater Objects, presentations, and the two traces

**Theorem 8A.9 (O-A5-5: the analytic trace is the Haar-regularized Euler
characteristic of the categorical shadow).**  **[REBUILT, phase 130.]**  Let
`w` be an external class and let `Sh(E_w)` be the full relative Hochschild
shadow computed in Theorem 7.1.  Then, place by place:

  (a) at a finite `p` with `v_p(w_p) ≠ 0`, the local factor of `Sh(E_w)` is the
      single origin class `C[0]`, of **rank 1**; the arithmetic weight is
      **not** in the shadow but in the half-density–Lefschetz factor

        w_p(σ) := |σ|_p^{1/2}·(1/|1−σ|_p) = τ_p(σ),                 (8A.23a)

      the product of the §5.2 half-density `|σ|_p^{1/2}` and the fixed-point
      (Lefschetz) weight `1/|1−σ|_p` of the fixed point `x_p=0`: for
      `v_p(σ)>0` the Lefschetz factor is `1`, for `v_p(σ)<0` it is
      `|σ|_p^{−1}`, and in both cases the product is `p^{−|v_p(σ)|/2}`, which
      is Lemma 8.4's inversion invariance and Appendix D.2.  **[REBUILT,
      phase 130, round 2, finding F4 of `R130.002`, F1 of `R130.003`: round 1
      attributed the whole weight to the half-density, and called `τ_p` "the
      Haar-regularized rank" at shells where the rank is `1`.]**;
  (b) at a finite `p` with `w_p ∈ Z_p^×`, `w_p ≠ 1`, the local factor is
      `C ⊕ \widehat{⊕}_j C^{N_k}`, of infinite rank, with
      `N_k = (p−1)p^{k−1} = c_p/|1−w_p|_p` (`c_p = 1−p^{−1}`, the **additive**
      normalization), and its **Haar-regularized** rank — the sum of the shell
      ranks against their Haar weights, computed in §7.2 and Appendix D.4 — is
      the finite number `rk^{Haar}(w_p) = 1/|1−w_p|_p`, while the half-density
      factor is `|w_p|_p^{1/2} = 1`; so here the whole weight `τ_p(w_p)` **is**
      the regularized rank, and this is the regime in which (8A.24) is a
      theorem about `Sh`;
  (c) consequently, for every `F ∈ D_A`,

        T(F) = Σ_{v∈S} ∫'_{Q_v^×} F(j_v(x))·rk^{Haar}(x)·w_v(x)\,d^*x ,
                                                                   (8A.24)

      with the regularization of (8.5)/(8.5∞) and `c=0`, where `rk^{Haar}(x)`
      is the Haar-regularized rank of the local shadow factor at `x` and
      `w_v(x)` the half-density–Lefschetz weight, the two satisfying
      `rk^{Haar}·w_v = τ_v` and distributed as

        non-unit shells:  rk^{Haar} = 1,     w_p = τ_p ;
        unit shells:      rk^{Haar} = τ_p,   w_p = 1 .              (8A.24′)

*Proof.*  (a) and the rank statement in (b) are Theorem 7.1(c), Remark 7.3 and
Appendix D.3–D.4, quoted verbatim; the weights are Lemma 8.2(i)–(ii)
(`τ_p(p^ku) = p^{−|k|/2}` for `k≠0`, `τ_p(u) = 1/|1−u|_p` for a unit `u ≠ 1`),
and the identity `N_k = c_p/|1−u|_p` with the Haar summation is the paragraph
of §7.2 ("the normal cutoff trace of P123.042 sums the shells with their Haar
weights against a compact test to the finite value `1/|1−u|_p`, which is (8.1)
at a unit"), whose source is P123.042.  (c) is then Definition 8.6 with
`c = 0`, since by (a)–(b) the integrand `τ_v` of (8.5) **is** the
Haar-regularized rank.  ∎

**Remark 8A.9a (in what sense this closes (O-A5-5), and in what sense it must
not be overread).**  (O-A5-5) asked for the comparison of the categorical
shadow `Sh` with the analytic trace `Tr_A`.  Theorem 8A.9 is that comparison,
and it is exact: `Tr_A` is not *equal* to any additive invariant of `Sh` —
Corollary 7.5 proves that no such invariant exists, being constant or
undefined — but it is the **declared regularization of the one numerical
invariant of `Sh` that does exist**, its Haar-regularized rank, and the
regularization is the one already proved in P123.042.  So the two objects of
§2.4 and §8A are joined by an explicit formula, which is what the obstacle
named.  Two honesty notes.  (1) The regularization is a declaration, not a
theorem about `Sh`; what is a theorem is that the regularized ranks are the
proved local densities `τ_v`.  (2) A second comparison — with the *analytic*
regularization of the imported cutoff theorem — is Theorem 8.10, and its
constant `c_{cut}` is undetermined (Remark 8A.7e).  **Obstacle (O-A5-5) is
CLOSED**, in the form (8A.24).

**Theorem 8A.10 (O-A5-6: `F ↦ E_F` is faithful).**  **[REBUILT, phase 130.]**
The map `D_A → \{\text{Fell sections}\}`, `F ↦ E_F = ∫F(u)E_{σ(u)}d^*u`, is
**injective**, and so is its composition with the regular representation.

*Proof.*  Two independent proofs.  (i) *Coefficientwise.*  By Corollary 3.4 the
section `σ` trivializes the Fell bundle strictly, so every fibre has a
canonical vector `1_{E_u}`, and (Proposition 10.2b) the coefficient of `E_F` at
the label `u` is exactly `F(u)`, with no coefficient at any nontrivial gauge
label.  So `E_F = E_G` forces `F = G` pointwise.  (ii) *Operator-theoretically.*
`D_A ⊆ L^1(C_S)` and the regular representation `λ` of `L^1(C_S)` on
`L^2(C_S)` is faithful: if `λ(F)=0` then `F⋆G = 0` for every `G ∈ L^2∩L^1`,
so `F̂Ĝ = 0` a.e. for every such `G`; taking `Ĝ` nonvanishing on a prescribed
compact set gives `F̂ = 0` a.e., hence `F=0` by Fourier inversion for `L^1`
functions with continuous transform.  Under the trivialization of
Corollary 3.4 the action of `E_F` on the Fell carrier is `λ(F)` (P123.014
(5.2)), so faithfulness of `λ` is faithfulness of `F ↦ E_F` into the sections.
∎  **Obstacle (O-A5-6) is CLOSED.**

**Theorem 8A.11 (O-A5-4 was mis-posed; the correct statement).**  **[REBUILT,
phase 130.]**

  (a) *The requirement is not one that any trace of correspondences satisfies.*
      (O-A5-4)(a) asked for `E_F ≅ E_G ⟹ F = G`, in order that `Tr_A` be an
      invariant of **objects** rather than of presentations.  No trace of
      correspondences has that property, in this theory or in Weil's: for an
      algebraic correspondence `Γ` on a surface and `n ≥ 2`, the cycles `nΓ`
      and `Γ` have the same underlying closed subscheme, hence are isomorphic
      as geometric objects, while `\mathrm{Tr}(nΓ) = n\mathrm{Tr}(Γ)`.  The
      classical trace is a function of a **cycle** — an element of a free
      abelian group on subvarieties, i.e. a presentation with multiplicities —
      and not of the underlying object.  Requirement (a) therefore asks for
      something whose failure is a feature of the classical theory it is
      modelled on, and it is **deleted**.
  (b) *What is true and what is needed.*  `F ↦ E_F` is injective
      (Theorem 8A.10), so the presentation is faithfully recorded by the
      smeared objects: `Corr_A^{tr}` is a category of **based** objects, the
      exact analogue of cycles with coefficients, and `s` of (8A.0) is the
      analogue of the cycle class with its multiplicities.  This is what
      clause A.5 asks for when it says "a declared trace-class domain".
  (c) *Idempotent splitting is not needed.*  (O-A5-4)(b) asked for idempotents
      to split in the additive category of smeared objects, in order to
      decompose contractible complexes.  With (a) deleted, the decomposition is
      not required: Proposition 8A.8 already gives invariance under
      isomorphisms of presented complexes and under elementary contractions,
      and §3.3 withdrew the idempotent-completeness declaration in round 2.
  (d) *What is still not claimed.*  It is **not** claimed that `Tr_A` descends
      to quasi-isomorphism classes of the underlying complexes; by (a) it
      should not, and clause A.5 does not ask it to.

**Obstacle (O-A5-4) is CLOSED** — by correction of its statement, in the form
(a)–(d).

**Theorem 8A.12 (the trace-class category over `𝓐_tr`; closure under
`‖·‖_tr`-limits).**  **[REBUILT, phase 130, round 2, finding F8; the
statement was announced in `R130.004` and audited in `R130.005` and
`PANEL_3`, but the text never entered this document.  Entered in phase 137
(record repair), with the proof the audit accepted.]**  Let `λ` denote the
regular representation of `L^1(C_S)` on `L^2(C_S)`, `λ(H)f := H⋆f`, and for
`H ∈ 𝓐_tr ⊆ L^1(C_S)` (Theorem 8A.7b(a)) put `E_H := λ(H)`.

  (a) *Boundedness and compatibility.*  `‖λ(H)‖_{op} ≤ ‖H‖_{L^1} ≤
      ½‖H‖_tr` (by (8A.15′)); `λ(H⋆K) = λ(H)λ(K)`, and `λ(H^†) = λ(H)^*` for
      the involution `H^†(w) = \overline{H(w^{−1})}` of Theorem 8A.7b(a).
      Hence `H ↦ E_H` is a bounded, multiplicative, `†`-compatible linear map
      `𝓐_tr → B(L^2(C_S))`.
  (b) *Faithfulness.*  `H ↦ E_H` is injective on `𝓐_tr`.
  (c) *Extension of `F ↦ E_F`.*  On `D_A` the operator `E_H` coincides with
      the action of the smeared object `E_F` of Corollary 3.4 on the Fell
      carrier (Theorem 8A.10(ii)); so `F ↦ E_F` extends `‖·‖_tr`-continuously
      and injectively from `D_A` to `𝓐_tr`.
  (d) *The category.*  Let `Corr_A^{tr,𝓐}` be defined as in Definition 8A.1
      with `D_A` replaced by `𝓐_tr` throughout (presentations `𝔣 = (H_n)` with
      `H_n ∈ 𝓐_tr`, terms `E_{H_n}`, differentials in `𝓐_tr` acting by
      convolution).  Then `s`, (8A.0′), Theorem 8A.3, Proposition 8A.8,
      Theorems 8A.10–8A.11 and the trace `Tr_A := T∘s` hold on
      `Corr_A^{tr,𝓐}` verbatim, and `Corr_A^{tr,𝓐}` is **closed under
      `‖·‖_tr`-limits of presentations**: if `𝔣^{(k)} = (H^{(k)}_n)` are
      presentations of objects of `Corr_A^{tr,𝓐}` with a common finite
      support in `n`, with `H^{(k)}_n → H_n` and differentials `d^{(k)}_n →
      d_n` in `‖·‖_tr`, then `(H_n)`, `(d_n)` is a presentation of an object
      of `Corr_A^{tr,𝓐}`, and `Tr_A(𝔣^{(k)}) → Tr_A(𝔣)`.

*Proof.*  (a) `‖H⋆f‖_2 ≤ ‖H‖_1‖f‖_2` is Young's inequality for the unimodular
group `C_S` with its Haar measure `d^*w`; the first inequality of (8A.15′)
gives the bound by `‖H‖_tr`.  Multiplicativity is associativity of
convolution in `L^1 ⋆ L^2`; `⟨H⋆f,g⟩ = ⟨f,H^†⋆g⟩` is the change of variable
`w ↦ w^{−1}` together with unimodularity, so `λ(H^†) = λ(H)^*`.
(b) Faithfulness of the regular representation of `L^1` of a locally compact
abelian group: if `λ(H) = 0` then `H⋆G = 0` for all `G ∈ L^1∩L^2`, hence
`ĤĜ = 0` a.e. on `Ĉ_S`; choosing `Ĝ` nonvanishing on any prescribed compact
set gives `Ĥ = 0` a.e., and `H = 0` in `L^1` by uniqueness of the Fourier
transform.  Composed with the injection `𝓐_tr → L^1(C_S)` of
Theorem 8A.7b(a), this is injectivity on `𝓐_tr`.
(c) The identification on `D_A` is Theorem 8A.10(ii); continuity is (a);
injectivity is (b).
(d) Every statement listed is an identity in the commutative Banach
`†`-algebra `𝓐_tr` (Theorem 8A.7b(a)) involving finitely many elements: the
term descriptions of `⊕`, shift, cone and `⋆`, the identities (8A.0′)
(absolute rearrangement of finite sums), the invariances of
Proposition 8A.8 (which use only `s(F−F) = 0` and additivity of `s`), the
correction Theorem 8A.11 (which uses only injectivity, i.e. (b)), and
`Tr_A = T∘s` with `T` continuous on `𝓐_tr` by Theorem 8A.7b(b).  None of
their proofs uses compact support or smoothness of the entries, only the
algebra structure and the trace, so they hold verbatim.  For closure: the
relations `d_{n+1}⋆d_n = 0` are `‖·‖_tr`-limits of the relations
`d^{(k)}_{n+1}⋆d^{(k)}_n = 0` by submultiplicativity (8A.16), so `(H_n),(d_n)`
is a presented complex over `𝓐_tr`; and `Tr_A(𝔣^{(k)}) = T(Σ_n(−1)^nH^{(k)}_n)
→ T(Σ_n(−1)^nH_n) = Tr_A(𝔣)` by continuity of `T` (Theorem 8A.7b(b)) and the
finiteness of the common support.  ∎

**Remark 8A.12a (which completion is declared).**  The numerical quotient
`N_A` of §9 (Theorem 9.5) and the distributions dual to the trace index
**no** objects of any category here; the completion declared for clause A.2
is the analytic one, `Corr_A^{tr,𝓐}`, and Theorem 8A.12 is the statement
that it is a category of presented complexes on which every result of §8A
holds and which is closed under the limits its norm defines.  Nothing is
claimed about limits of presentations without a common finite support.

**Proposition 8A.8 (invariance of `Tr_A`, in the form that is provable).**
**[REBUILT, phase 129, round 2, finding F5.  The round-1 Theorem 8A.8 split
idempotents in a Karoubi envelope and then evaluated `s` on the summands,
where `s` is not defined — the presentation does not survive the splitting.
The argument is withdrawn.]**  `Tr_A` is invariant under:

  (i)  isomorphism of presented complexes inducing the identity on
       presentations;
  (ii) addition of an **elementary contractible** presented complex, i.e. one
       of the form `0→E_F→^{≅}E_F→0` in adjacent degrees;
  (iii) the operations of Theorem 8A.3 (shift, sum, cone).

*Proof.*  (i) is immediate: `Tr_A` depends only on `𝔣`.  (ii) such a complex
has `s = F−F = 0`, so `T = 0`, and `s` is additive on `⊕` by (8A.0′).  (iii)
is Theorem 8A.3.  ∎

**Obstacle (O-A5-4) (invariance).**  Whether `Tr_A` descends to
quasi-isomorphism or even homotopy-equivalence classes of the **underlying**
complexes is not proved.  Two missing ingredients: (a) `E_F ≅ E_G ⟹ F=G`
(Remark 8A.1a), without which `s` is not a function of an object; (b) the
decomposition of a bounded contractible complex into elementary pieces, which
needs idempotents in the additive category of smeared objects to split, and
`D_A` is a non-unital commutative convolution algebra in which I have not
exhibited such splittings.  Until (a) and (b), `Tr_A` is an invariant of
**presented** complexes only.  **[REBUILT, phase 130: this obstacle is
mis-posed; see Theorem 8A.11, which deletes requirement (a) with its reason
and shows (b) is then unnecessary.  The paragraph is kept as the statement of
what was being asked.]**  Clause A.5 does not literally demand more — it
asks for a trace on a declared trace-class domain — but the honest reading is
that the domain is a category of presentations.

### 8A.4 Clause A.5: verdict `PROVED`, and the disposal of every obstacle

**[REBUILT, phase 130.  Round 2 declared A.5 `PARTIAL` with six named
obstacles.  All six are disposed of above — five by proof, one (O-A5-4) by
correction of its statement — and the verdict is revised.  The round-1
declaration of `PROVED` was wrong for the reasons round 2 gave; this one rests
on Theorems 8A.7b, 8A.7c, 8A.9, 8A.10, 8A.11 and Definition 8A.7d, each with a
complete proof.]**

`PROVED`.
* **Trace-class domain** declared: `Corr_A^{tr}`, presented complexes over the
  section algebra `D_A` (Def. 8A.1), with `F ↦ E_F` faithful (Thm. 8A.10), so
  the domain is a category of **based** objects — the analogue of cycles with
  multiplicities (Thm. 8A.11).  Its analytic completion is the Banach
  `†`-algebra `𝓐_tr` (Def. 8A.7b0, Thm. 8A.7b(a)), and its chart-stable
  enlargement is `D_A^∞` (Thm. 8A.7c).
* **Target** declared: `T_A := Pic_b(B_T)`, based invertible `B_T`-modules,
  equivalently pairs of based Hermitian lines (Def. 8A.2′), with
  `Pic_b(C) ↪ T_A` as the first factor; `Tr_A` takes values in that factor.
  **[REBUILT, phase 130, round 2, finding F16: `Tr_A` uses only the first
  factor, so clause A.5 is in fact verified against `Pic_b(C)`; the
  enlargement is needed by clause A.8 (Thm. 10.8) and is declared here so that
  one target serves both.]**
* **Additivity**, shift, cone (Thm. 8A.3).
* **Cyclicity** `T(K⋆L)=T(L⋆K)` (Thm. 8A.4), with the honest caveat that it
  follows from commutativity of `C_S`.
* **`†`-reality** (Thm. 8A.4), from Lemma 8.4.
* **Restriction**: the chart maps `J_{ST}` exist (Thm. 8A.5), the comparison
  formula (8A.12) is exact, and on the colimit `D_A^∞` the trace `T_∞` is
  **chart-invariant on the nose** (Thm. 8A.7c), with the window statement
  (8A.7c)(e) saying exactly when the finite-chart form already equals it.
* **Determinant compatibility**, stated for what it is.  **[REBUILT, phase
  130, round 2, finding F16 of `R130.002`: `Det_S^{rel} := \exp T` by
  Definition 8A.2, so "the trace is compatible with the determinant" is
  definitionally true and is not the content.]**  The content is: `\exp T` is
  continuous for a **complete** algebra norm (Thm. 8A.7b(a),(b)); its
  determinant germ `Δ_F(z)` exists for every `F ∈ 𝓐_tr`, is holomorphic on
  `|z| < 1/‖F‖_{L^1}`, and is **exactly multiplicative**,
  `Δ_F(z)Δ_G(z) = Δ_{F+G+zF⋆G}(z)` (Thm. 8A.7b(d),(e)) — for all valuation
  shells and the archimedean place at once.  That is a theorem and it is what
  (O-A5-1) asked for.
* **Normalization**: `c := 0`, declared with its four reasons
  (Def. 8A.7d).
* **Comparison with the categorical shadow**: `Tr_A` is the exponential of the
  Haar-regularized Euler characteristic of `Sh` (Thm. 8A.9, (8A.24)).
* **Invariance** under isomorphism of presentations and elementary
  contractions (Prop. 8A.8); the stronger demand of the old (O-A5-4) is
  deleted, with its reason (Thm. 8A.11).
* No finite scalar **degree** is asserted, so the clause's degree requirement
  is vacuous.

**Disposal of the obstacle list.**

| obstacle | disposal |
|---|---|
| (O-A5-1a) | **CLOSED**, Thm. 8A.7b(f): `|Σ_κ c(κ)a^{(r)}_κ| ≤ ‖F‖_1^{r−1}(\log p)^{−1}N_p^u(F)`, uniformly in `r` |
| (O-A5-1b) | **CLOSED**, Thm. 8A.7b(d)–(e): the germ exists for every `F ∈ 𝓐_tr`, all shells and `∞` at once, and is exactly multiplicative.  Rem. 8A.7b1 records that P123.105 §4 / P123.107 §5 disclaim the **operator-cutoff** germ, which is a different object and is not used |
| (O-A5-2) | **CLOSED**, Thm. 8A.7c: `T_∞` on `D_A^∞` is chart-invariant exactly |
| (O-A5-3) | **CLOSED** as a normalization, Def. 8A.7d (`c := 0`).  The imported theorem's own constant `c_{cut}` remains undetermined (Rem. 8A.7e) and nothing in this paper depends on it; (8A.13) is corrected to (8A.13′) |
| (O-A5-4) | **CLOSED by correction**, Thm. 8A.11: requirement (a) is deleted — no trace of correspondences is an invariant of unbased objects, Weil's included — and (b) is then not needed |
| (O-A5-5) | **CLOSED**, Thm. 8A.9: `Tr_A = \exp(\text{Haar-regularized Euler characteristic of } Sh)`, with Cor. 7.5 explaining why no *additive* invariant of `Sh` can do it |
| (O-A5-6) | **CLOSED**, Thm. 8A.10: `F ↦ E_F` is injective, by the canonical trivialization and by faithfulness of the regular representation of `L^1(C_S)` |

**Clause A.5: `PROVED`** on the declared domain and target.  Two limitations
are stated, not hidden: cyclicity is weak because the sector is commutative,
and the comparison with the **imported** operator determinant (8A.13′) carries
one undetermined constant, which no statement of this paper uses.

## 9. Numerical equivalence (clause A.6)

**[REBUILT, phase 129.]**

### 9.1 The two Tate functionals, constructed directly on the external classes

The previous Theorem 9.1 obtained the ruling plane from `ρ_T: C_Q→C⊕C` of
P123.008, which is defined on the **internal rational gauge-label** algebra.
P123.008's own status block reads "`OPEN` for descent of this representation to
the external idele-class dynamics (P123.013 proves it cannot descend)", and
P123.013 Prop. 2.1 proves the obstruction exactly: on `H_S=L²(Γ_S\A_S)` every
`θ(γ)`, `γ∈Γ_S`, is the identity.  That route is withdrawn.  No descent is
needed, because the modulus is already a character of the **external** group.

**Theorem 9.1 (external Tate characters).**  `|γ|_S = 1` for every `γ∈Γ_S`;
hence

    ρ_T^{ext}(u) = ( |u|_S^{−1/2}, |u|_S^{1/2} )                     (9.1)

is a well-defined multiplicative map on the idele **class** group `C_S`,
independent of the lift, with `ρ_T^{ext}(u^{−1}) = ρ_T^{ext}(u)^♯` for the swap
`♯`.  Integrating, the two **Tate functionals**

    λ_∓(F) := ∫_{C_S} F(u) |u|_S^{∓1/2} d^*u,   F ∈ D_A,            (9.2)

are continuous, satisfy `λ_∓(F⋆G)=λ_∓(F)λ_∓(G)`, `λ_∓(F^†)=conj(λ_±(F))`, and
`ρ_T = (λ_−,λ_+) : D_A → C²` is a surjective continuous algebra map.  With
`e_−=(1,0)`, `e_+=(0,1)` the Hermitian form
`β_T(z,w)=z_−conj(w_+)+z_+conj(w_−)` has matrix `[[0,1],[1,0]]`.

*Proof.*  `|γ|_S=1` is the product formula for a positive `S`-unit, whose
absolute value is `1` outside `S` (P123.014 (6.1); also §5.2 above).  Hence
`|·|_S^{±1/2}` is constant on `Γ_S`-orbits and (9.1) descends to `C_S`; this
is P123.014 §6, whose status line reads `PROVED-IN-REPOSITORY` for "the
external two-Tate realization and its exact hyperbolic ruling form", and the
note states explicitly: "Unlike the internal map of P123.008, (6.2) is trivial
on gauge arrows and therefore descends to the physical quotient."  The audit
override P123.018 on P123.014 concerns the unitized lift-change map (§1) and
extension-by-one on classes (§2); neither is used in (9.1), which is a
character of `C_S` and nothing else.  Multiplicativity of `λ_∓` is the Fourier
transform of convolution at a fixed character; continuity is continuity of
integration against a locally bounded character on each fixed-support step;
`λ_∓(F^†) = ∫ conj(F(u^{−1}))|u|^{∓1/2}d^*u = conj(∫F(v)|v|^{±1/2}d^*v)
= conj(λ_±(F))` by `u↦v=u^{−1}`, which is (6.3) of P123.014.  Surjectivity:
`|·|_S : C_S → R_{>0}` is surjective, so `λ_∓` are the Mellin transforms of
the radial push-forward of `F` at the two points `s=∓1/2`, and a compactly
supported smooth function on `R_{>0}` may have prescribed Mellin values at two
distinct points.  ∎

**What Theorem 9.1 does not yet give.**  `λ_±` are functionals on `D_A`.
Whether they **descend to `N_A`** — equivalently whether they annihilate the
numerical radical `𝓝` of Definition 9.2 — is not proved here and I do not
know a proof from the repository.  See §9.3.

### 9.2 Pairing, radical, quotient

**Definition 9.2.**  For `K,L ∈ Corr_A^{tr}`,

    ⟨K,L⟩ := T(K ⋆ L^†) = T( s(K) ⋆ s(L)^† ) = I_A(s(K),s(L)).      (9.3)

`𝓝 := {K ∈ Corr_A^{tr} : ⟨K,L⟩=0 for all L}`; `N_A := Corr_A^{tr}/𝓝`, with the
quotient topology from the nuclear LF topology of `D_A` transported by `s`.

**Theorem 9.3 (biadditivity, and the shape of the pairing).**  `⟨·,·⟩` is
linear in the first and conjugate-linear in the second variable for `⊕`, and
satisfies

    ⟨K[1],L⟩ = −⟨K,L⟩,   ⟨Cone(f:K→L),M⟩ = ⟨L,M⟩−⟨K,M⟩,            (9.4)

and is Hermitian: `conj(⟨K,L⟩) = ⟨L,K⟩`.  It is jointly continuous.

*Proof.*  **[REBUILT, phase 129; biadditivity on sums, cones and shifts was
never proved.]**  All three identities follow from the corresponding
identities for `s` (§8A.1: `s(K⊕L)=s(K)+s(L)`, `s(K[1])=−s(K)`,
`s(Cone f)=s(L)−s(K)`), the bilinearity of `(F,G)↦F⋆G^†` on `D_A`, and
linearity and continuity of `T` (Definition 8.6).  Hermitian symmetry: `D_A` is
commutative, so `F⋆G^† = G^†⋆F = (G⋆F^†)^†`, and `T(H^†)=conj(T(H))` by
Theorem 8A.4; hence `⟨K,L⟩ = T((s(L)⋆s(K)^†)^†) = conj(⟨L,K⟩)`.  Joint
continuity: `⋆` and `†` are continuous on each fixed-support step of `D_A`
(P123.014 (5.2); Fubini and the pentagon), and `T` is continuous on each step
(Definition 8.6).  ∎

**Theorem 9.4 (A.6, the algebraic half — proved).**  `𝓝` is closed, a
two-sided `⋆`-ideal, `†`-stable, and stable under shifts and cones; `N_A` is
a Hausdorff locally convex space carrying continuous descended `⋆`, `†` and a
**nondegenerate** Hermitian pairing.

*Proof.*  **[REBUILT, phase 129; the previous proof of ideal-stability used the
undefined symbol `T(E_w)` and the `†` step was asserted.]**

*Closed.*  `𝓝 = ⋂_L ker(⟨·,L⟩)`, an intersection of kernels of continuous
functionals (Theorem 9.3).

*Ideal.*  For `K∈𝓝` and arbitrary `M,L`, using commutativity of `D_A` and
`(M⋆L^†)^† = L⋆M^†`:
`⟨K⋆M, L⟩ = T(s(K)⋆s(M)⋆s(L)^†) = T(s(K)⋆(s(L)⋆s(M)^†)^†) = ⟨K, L⋆M^†⟩ = 0`,
and `L⋆M^†∈Corr_A^{tr}` by §8A.1.  Commutativity gives both sides.  This is
P123.007 (3.3) transported to `D_A`.

*`†`-stable.*  **[REBUILT, phase 129, round 3, finding G5: the round-2 chain
was circular; here is the one-liner.]**  For `K∈𝓝` and any `L`,

    ⟨K^†,L⟩ = T(s(K)^†⋆s(L)^†) = T((s(K)⋆s(L))^†)
            = conj(T(s(K)⋆s(L))) = conj(⟨K,L^†⟩) = 0,

using `(a⋆b)^†=b^†⋆a^†` together with commutativity of `D_A`, then
Theorem 8A.4's `T(H^†)=conj(T(H))`, then `s(L^†)=s(L)^†` and `(L^†)^†=L`.
The final vanishing is `K∈𝓝` applied to `L^†∈Corr_A^{tr}`.  Involutivity of
`†` gives `𝓝^†=𝓝`.  This is P123.007 (3.4) transported.

*Shifts and cones.*  Immediate from (9.4).

*Hausdorff and nondegenerate.*  The quotient of a locally convex space by a
closed subspace is Hausdorff; if a class pairs to zero with every class, any
representative lies in `𝓝` by definition, so the descended pairing is
nondegenerate on `N_A`.  Continuity of `⋆`, `†` on the quotient follows from
continuity upstairs and the fact that `𝓝` is a `⋆`- and `†`-stable closed
subspace.  ∎

*Completion caveat, stated because P123.007 §4 states it.*  Nondegeneracy is
proved on `N_A` itself.  It is **not** automatically inherited by an arbitrary
locally convex completion of `N_A`: the boundary radical
`R_∂ = {x ∈ completion : I_A(x,y)=0 ∀y}` is again a closed `†`-ideal, and
whether it vanishes for a proved perfect-pairing topology is `OPEN`
(P123.007 §4).  A.6 asks that the topology and continuity of all operations be
explicit; they are, on `N_A`.  It also asks for a "separating completion";
that is the open point.

**Theorem 9.5 (`N_A ≠ 0`: `T` separates two sections of equal modulus
profile, by an exact computation with no regularization).**
**[REBUILT, phase 129, round 2, findings F8 and F9.  Round 1 billed this as
"the mixed sector is nonzero"; that overstates it, because "mixed sector"
presupposed the ruling plane inside `N_A`, which was then open.
**[REBUILT, phase 129, round 3:]** it is no longer open — Theorems 9.8 and
9.12 supply it — but Theorem 9.5's own content is still exactly what its title
says, and it is used in Theorem 9.12 (T3) to exhibit a nonzero **primitive**
class.  Three defects of the round-1 proof — a
false statement about the archimedean slice, unstated hypotheses making
`μ>0`, and an uncited step — are repaired.]**

Fix `p ∈ S_f` and units `u,u' ∈ 1+pZ_p` with
`j := v_p(1−u) ≠ j' := v_p(1−u')`.  Fix `m > max(j,j')`.  Let
`θ := min_{q∈S_f} q^{1/2} > 1` and let `h ∈ C_c^∞(R^×)` satisfy `h ≥ 0`,
`supp h ⊂ (θ^{−1},θ)`, `h(1) > 0`.  Using the coordinates of Lemma 3.3,
`C_S ≅ R^× × ∏_{q∈S_f}Z_q^×`, define

    F (x_∞, (w_q)) := h(x_∞) · 1_{u(1+p^mZ_p)}(w_p) · ∏_{q≠p} 1_{Z_q^×}(w_q),
    F'(x_∞, (w_q)) := the same with u replaced by u'.               (9.5a)

Then `F,F' ∈ D_A^{off}`, and with `ν := h(1)·vol(1+p^mZ_p) > 0` (probability Haar
on `Z_p^×`):

    T(F) = ν (log p) p^{j},    T(F') = ν (log p) p^{j'},
    T(F) − T(F') = ν (log p) ( p^{j} − p^{j'} ) ≠ 0,                 (9.5)

while `λ_−(F)=λ_−(F')` and `λ_+(F)=λ_+(F')`.  Consequently `[F] ≠ [F']` in
`N_A`, so `N_A ≠ 0`, and `T` distinguishes two sections with identical
push-forward to the modulus group.

*Proof.*  **`F,F' ∈ D_A^{off}`.**  They are finite products of a compactly
supported smooth function of `x_∞` and characteristic functions of compact-open
subsets of the compact groups `Z_q^×`, hence lie in `D_A`.  The identity class
is `1=(1,(1)_q)`; since `u ∈ 1+pZ_p` with `v_p(1−u)=j<m`, we have
`1 ∉ u(1+p^mZ_p)`, and that set is open and closed, so `F` vanishes on a
neighbourhood of `1`.  In particular `F(1)=0`, and by Theorem 8.8 the value
`T(F)` is the absolutely convergent sum (8.6), with **no counterterm, no
constant `c`, and no dependence on Definition 8.6's choice or on
Theorem 8.9**.

**The slice maps.**  By Lemma 3.3, for `q ∈ S_f` and `x = q^k w ∈ Q_q^×`,

    j_q(x) = ( q^{−k} at ∞, w at q, q^{−k} at every other place of S ),
    j_∞(x) = ( x at ∞, 1 at every finite place of S ).              (9.5b)

(This is the computation of Theorem 8A.5's proof, for `T=S`.)

**`W_∞(F)=0`.**  **[Round-1 defect (i) repaired: the round-1 proof asserted
that `W_∞` "depends only on the radial push-forward", which is false — by
(9.5b) it depends on `F` along the single slice `(x,1,…,1)`.]**  On that slice
the `p`-coordinate is `1 ∉ u(1+p^mZ_p)`, so `F∘j_∞ ≡ 0` and `W_∞(F)=0`.  The
same for `F'`.

**`W_q(F)=0` for `q ∈ S_f`, `q ≠ p`.**  By (9.5b) the `p`-coordinate of
`j_q(q^kw)` is `q^{−k}`.  For `k=0` this is `1 ∉ u(1+p^mZ_p)`, so `F∘j_q`
vanishes on the units and both the unit packet and `a(1)` vanish.  For `k≠0`
the `∞`-coordinate is `q^{−k} ∉ supp h`, because `|k|≥1` and
`supp h ⊂ (θ^{−1},θ)` with `θ ≤ q^{1/2} < q`.  So `F∘j_q ≡ 0` and `W_q(F)=0`.
**[Round-1 defect (ii) repaired: the round-1 proof took "sections supported in
`Z_ℓ^×` at the other places", which by (9.5b) does not make the off-unit shell
integral vanish; the support condition on `h` does.]**

**`W_p(F) = ν(log p)p^{j}`.**  By (9.5b), `a(p^kw) = h(p^{−k})1_{u(1+p^mZ_p)}(w)`.
For `k≠0`, `p^{−k}∉supp h`, so the shell integral of (8.5) vanishes.  For
`k=0`, `a(w) = h(1)1_{u(1+p^mZ_p)}(w)` and `a(1)=0`.  Hence

    W_p(F) = (log p) ∫_{Z_p^×} a(w)|1−w|_p^{−1} dw
           = (log p) h(1) ∫_{u(1+p^mZ_p)} |1−w|_p^{−1} dw .

On `u(1+p^mZ_p)` write `w = u(1+p^m y)`, `y∈Z_p`; then
`1−w = (1−u) − u p^m y`, and since `v_p(1−u)=j < m` we get `v_p(1−w)=j`
exactly, so `|1−w|_p^{−1} = p^{j}` is **constant** on the coset.  Therefore
`W_p(F) = (log p)h(1)p^{j}vol(1+p^mZ_p) = ν(log p)p^{j}`, and `ν>0` because
`h(1)>0`.  The same with `j'`.  Subtracting gives (9.5), nonzero since `j≠j'`.

**The Tate functionals agree.**  By Lemma 3.3, `|w|_S = |w_∞|` on `C_S`, so

    λ_∓(F) = (∫_{R^×} h(x)|x|^{∓1/2}d^*x) · vol(u(1+p^mZ_p)) · ∏_{q≠p}vol(Z_q^×),

and `vol(u(1+p^mZ_p)) = vol(1+p^mZ_p) = vol(u'(1+p^mZ_p))` because Haar
measure on `Z_p^×` is translation invariant.  So `λ_∓(F)=λ_∓(F')`.

**`[F]≠[F']`.**  It suffices that `T(F−F')≠0` and that `T` annihilates `𝓝`.
**[Round-1 defect (iii) repaired: this step was used without proof.]**  Let
`K∈𝓝`.  Let `(e_ε)` be an approximate identity of `D_A` (available: `C_S` is a
locally compact abelian group, and the normalized characteristic functions of a
neighbourhood base of `1` by compact-open-times-interval sets lie in `D_A`).
Then `s(K)⋆e_ε^† → s(K)` in `D_A`, in the fixed-support Fréchet step
determined by a fixed compact neighbourhood of `supp s(K)`, and `T` is
continuous on that step (Definition 8.6); hence
`T(s(K)) = lim_ε ⟨K,E_{e_ε}⟩ = 0`.  So `T` is well defined and zero on `𝓝`,
while `T(F−F')≠0`; therefore `F−F' ∉ 𝓝` and `[F]≠[F']`.  ∎

**Remark 9.6 (what (9.5) does and does not establish).**
**[REBUILT, phase 129, round 2, finding F8.]**

* It establishes `N_A ≠ 0`, and more: `T` separates two elements with the
  same image under the modulus map, i.e. the numerical theory is strictly
  finer than any theory of the modulus alone.  Written multiplicatively,
  `p^{j} = 1/|1−u|_p`, so the separating quantity is the Haar-regularized
  orbit count of Appendix D.4, which is proved there on `1+pZ_p` — and that is
  where `u,u'` are taken.
* It does **not** by itself establish "a nonzero **mixed** sector": that
  statement needs the ruling directions, and it is completed in §9.3.
  **[REBUILT, phase 129, round 3:]** what §9.3 adds is that `Φ = F−F'` lies in
  `ker λ_− ∩ ker λ_+ = D_A^{prim}` and is nonzero there (Theorem 9.12 (T3)),
  so the **primitive** sector — the kernel of the Tate boundary map — is
  nonzero.  The phase-128 correction is kept and is still right about `F` and
  `F'` individually: only their **difference** is annihilated by `λ_±`, so at
  most one of the two is primitive.


### 9.3 Fourier analysis of `T`, and the vanishing of the numerical radical

**[REBUILT, phase 129, round 3.  Round 2 argued that (O-A6-1) was
structurally blocked, because `T` is carried by Haar-null slices while `λ_±`
are Haar integrals, and because the only route it could see ran through the
explicit formula, which A.10 forbids.  **That claim is false and is withdrawn
in full**, together with its "Haar-null" display and the paragraph
"Why the only known route is forbidden here".  The auditor's objection is
decisive: support in the *space* variable says nothing about the radical,
which is a condition on Fourier support — `c·δ_1` is carried by a single point
and has full Fourier support.  Once the question is posed correctly it is a
computation with the local distributions already proved, it uses no zeta zero
and no explicit formula, and it comes out.]**

#### 9.3.1 The dual group and the Fourier formulation of `𝓝`

By Lemma 3.3, `C_S ≅ R^× × ∏_{q∈S_f}Z_q^×`, so its Pontryagin dual is

    Ĉ_S = { χ = (ε, τ; (κ_q)) },
    χ(w) = sgn(w_∞)^ε |w_∞|^{iτ} ∏_{q∈S_f} κ_q(w_q),                (9.6)

`ε∈{0,1}`, `τ∈R`, `κ_q` a finite-order character of `Z_q^×` of conductor
exponent `c(κ_q)≥0`.  As a topological space `Ĉ_S` is a **disjoint union of
copies of `{0,1}×R`**, one for each of the countably many systems `(κ_q)`;
call these its **components**.  Write

    F̂(χ) := ∫_{C_S} F(w) conj(χ(w)) d^*w ,   F ∈ D_A.              (9.7)

For `F ∈ D_A` of unit level `ℓ`, `F̂` is supported on the finitely many
components with `c(κ_q)≤ℓ` for all `q`, and on each of those it is a Schwartz
function of `τ` (the `R^×`-profile of `F` is `C_c^∞`).  The two Tate
functionals are the values of the **analytic continuation** of `F̂` off the
unitary axis:

    λ_∓(F) = ∫F(w)|w|_S^{∓1/2}d^*w = F̂(ε=0, τ = ∓i/2; (1)),        (9.8)

legitimate because the `R^×`-profile is compactly supported, so its Mellin
transform is entire.  `λ_±` are therefore **not** points of `Ĉ_S`; this is why
the round-2 "spectral measure" phrasing was ill posed, and (9.8) replaces it.

**Definition/Notation.**  **[REBUILT, phase 129, round 3, finding G9.]**  From
here on the numerical radical and quotient are taken on the section algebra:

    𝓝 := { F ∈ D_A : T(F⋆G^†)=0 for every G ∈ D_A },   N_A := D_A/𝓝.  (9.9)

Definition 9.2's `𝓝 ⊆ Corr_A^{tr}` is its preimage under `s`, and every
statement of Theorems 9.3–9.4 holds in both readings, `s` being surjective
onto `D_A` and additive.

#### 9.3.2 The symbol of `T`

**Theorem 9.7 (the symbol).**  **[REBUILT, phase 129, round 3; re-derived in
round 4 with the archimedean measure (8.5b) in place (finding F1) and with
reality stated (finding F9).]**  Define, for `χ ∈ Ĉ_S`,

    T̂(χ) := Ŵ_∞(χ) + Σ_{p∈S_f} Ŵ_p(χ),
    Ŵ_v(χ) := ∫'_{Q_v^×} χ(j_v(x)) τ_v(x) d^*x                     (9.10)

with the same regularization as in (8.5)/(8.5∞) and the same measures as in
Definition 8.6 — in particular `d^*x_∞ = dx/(2|x|)`.  Then each integral
converges, and:

  (a) *finite places.*  Put `η_p(χ) := p^{−iτ}∏_{q∈S_f, q≠p}κ_q(p)^{−1}`, of
      modulus one, and `ζ_p(χ) := p^{−1/2}\,\overline{η_p(χ)}`.  Then

        Ŵ_p(χ) = (log p)·2Re( ζ_p/(1−ζ_p) )        if κ_p = 1,
        Ŵ_p(χ) = −c(κ_p)·log p                      if κ_p ≠ 1.     (9.11)

      In both cases `Ŵ_p` is **real**, bounded on each component by
      `2(log p)p^{−1/2}/(1−p^{−1/2})` resp. `c(κ_p)log p`, and real-analytic
      in `τ`.

  (b) *the real place.*  `Ŵ_∞` is **real**, real-analytic in `τ` on each
      component, bounded above, and

        Ŵ_∞(ε,τ) = −log|τ| + O(1)   as |τ|→∞, uniformly in ε.       (9.12)

      For `ε=0` the exact value is P123.035 (4.1),
      `Ŵ_∞(0,τ) = log π − Re ψ(1/4+iτ/2)`, and
      `log π − Re ψ(1/4+iτ/2) = −log|τ| + log 2π + O(τ^{−2})`.

  (c) *consequently* `T̂` is real-valued, real-analytic on each component,
      bounded above by a finite constant `m := sup_{Ĉ_S} T̂ < ∞`, and
      `T̂(ε,τ;(κ_q)) → −∞` as `|τ|→∞`.  Hence for every `c ∈ R` the function
      `T̂+c` vanishes identically on **no** component, and its zero set is a
      **discrete** subset of each component.

  (d) *Plancherel form of the trace.*  For every `H ∈ D_A`,

        T(H) = ∫_{Ĉ_S} Ĥ(χ) ( T̂(χ) + c ) dχ ,                      (9.13)

      `dχ` the Plancherel measure dual to `d^*w`, the integral absolutely
      convergent.

  (d′) *Place-wise form.*  **[phase 133, PANEL_1 finding C1: this was
      established inside the proof of (d) but never stated as a numbered
      assertion, and Paper 45 Lemma 5.1a needs it in that form.]**  The
      interchange in (d) is justified **termwise**, so for every `v ∈ S` and
      every `H ∈ D_A`,

        W_v(H) = ∫_{Ĉ_S} Ĥ(χ) Ŵ_v(χ) dχ ,                          (9.13′)

      each integral absolutely convergent, and (9.13) is the sum of (9.13′)
      over `v ∈ S` plus `c∫Ĥ dχ`.

*Proof.*  **(a)**  By (9.5b), `j_p(p^k w) = (p^{−k} at ∞, w at p, p^{−k} at
every other place of S)`, so, `sgn(p^{−k})=+1`,

    χ(j_p(p^k w)) = p^{−ikτ} κ_p(w) ∏_{q≠p}κ_q(p)^{−k} = η_p(χ)^k κ_p(w).

Insert this into (8.5) with `a = χ∘j_p`, which has `a(1)=χ(1)=1`.

*Shell part.*  `∫_{p^kZ_p^×}a τ_p d^*x = (log p)p^{−|k|/2}η_p^k∫_{Z_p^×}κ_p(w)dw`
by Lemma 8.2(i) and `vol_{d^*x}(p^kZ_p^×)=log p` (P123.042 §1, the
*multiplicative* normalization; cf. the flag in §7.2).  The unit integral is
`1` if `κ_p=1` and `0` otherwise, by orthogonality.  For `κ_p=1` the sum over
`k≠0` is `Σ_{k≥1}p^{−k/2}(η_p^k+η_p^{−k})`; with `ζ := p^{−1/2}\barη_p` and
`η_p^{-1}=\barη_p` this is `Σ_{k≥1}(\barζ^k+ζ^k) = 2Re(ζ/(1−ζ))`,
absolutely convergent since `|ζ|=p^{−1/2}<1`, and manifestly real.

*Unit packet.*  It is `(log p)∫_{Z_p^×}(κ_p(u)−1)/|1−u|_p du` (probability
Haar), because `j_p(u)=(1 at ∞, u at p, 1 elsewhere)` for `k=0`.  This
integral is computed **exactly** in P123.105 §3: its (4) reads
`Tr(E_{MN}A_φ E_{MN})−(L+1)φ(1) = −Σ_χ c(χ)a_χ`, and its (5) identifies the
right-hand side with `∫_U(φ(u)−φ(1))/|1−u|_q du`.  Taking `φ=κ_p` gives
`a_χ = δ_{χ,κ_p}` and hence

    ∫_{Z_p^×}(κ_p(u)−1)/|1−u|_p du = −c(κ_p),                       (9.14)

a real number (an integer conductor exponent).  For `κ_p=1`, `c(1)=0` and the
packet vanishes, consistently.  Combining gives (9.11); the two cases are
exclusive because the shell part carries `δ_{κ_p,1}` and the packet carries
`c(κ_p)`, zero when `κ_p=1`.  Real-analyticity: `ζ_p = p^{−1/2}e^{i(τ\log
p+θ_p)}` for a constant `θ_p := arg∏_{q≠p}κ_q(p)`, so `2Re(ζ/(1−ζ))` is a
real-analytic `2π/\log p`-periodic function of `τ`; the other case is
constant.

**(b)**  **[REBUILT, phase 129, round 4: the round-3 derivation reached the
right asymptotic through two compensating slips — it integrated `∫_0^1`
where the full line is meant, and it "absorbed" the folding factor rather
than carrying it.  Here is the derivation with the measure (8.5b) declared
and every constant carried.]**
`j_∞(x) = (x at ∞, 1 at every finite place)`, so
`χ(j_∞(x)) = sgn(x)^ε|x|^{iτ}`, and with `x = ±e^t`, `dx/|x| = dt`, the
densities (8.5a) and the measure (8.5b) give

    Ŵ_∞(ε,τ) = ½ ∫'_R e^{iτt}\frac{dt}{2|\sinh(t/2)|}
             + (−1)^ε · ½ ∫_R e^{iτt}\frac{dt}{2\cosh(t/2)} .       (9.12a)

*Reality.*  Both kernels are even in `t`, so both integrals are real (the odd
part of `e^{iτt}` integrates to zero); the regularization at `t=0`, which
subtracts the value `1` of `χ∘j_∞` at the identity, preserves evenness.

*The second term.*  `1/(2\cosh(t/2))` is smooth and exponentially decaying, so
the integral converges absolutely; explicitly
`∫_R e^{iτt}/(2\cosh(t/2))\,dt = π/\cosh(πτ)`, which is bounded by `π` and
tends to `0`.  It contributes `O(1)`, uniformly in `ε`, and it is the only
place where the parity enters.

*The first term.*  `1/(2|\sinh(t/2)|) = 1/|t| + r(t)` with `r` even, bounded
on `|t|≤1` and exponentially decaying; so

    ∫'_R e^{iτt}\frac{dt}{2|\sinh(t/2)|}
       = ∫_{|t|≤1}\frac{e^{iτt}−1}{|t|}dt + O(1)
       = 2∫_0^1\frac{\cos τt − 1}{t}dt + O(1)
       = −2\,\mathrm{Cin}(|τ|) + O(1) = −2\log|τ| + O(1),

using `Cin(y)=∫_0^y(1−\cos u)/u\,du = \log y + γ − \mathrm{Ci}(y)
= \log y + O(1)`, and the `O(1)`'s being uniform in `τ` (absolute convergence
away from `t=0`, and Riemann–Lebesgue for the tail).  Multiplying by the
declared `½` of (8.5b) gives `−\log|τ| + O(1)`, i.e. (9.12), **uniformly in
`ε`** — the parity never enters the divergence, because the singularity of
`τ_∞` is at `x=+1` and `sgn(+1)^ε=1` for both parities.

*Cross-check and boundedness above.*  For `ε=0`, P123.035 (4.1) gives the
closed form `log π − Re ψ(1/4+iτ/2)`, proved there with its interchange
justification, and `Re ψ(1/4+iτ/2) = \log|τ/2| + O(τ^{−2})`, so
(4.1) `= −\log|τ| + \log 2π + O(τ^{−2})`, agreeing with (9.12) including the
`½`.  `Re ψ(1/4+iτ/2)` is increasing in `|τ|`, so `Ŵ_∞(0,·)` attains its
maximum at `τ=0`:

    max_τ Ŵ_∞(0,τ) = log π − ψ(1/4) = log π + γ + 3\log 2 + π/2
                    ≈ 1.1447 + 0.5772 + 2.0794 + 1.5708 ≈ 5.372 .   (9.12b)

For `ε=1` the same two displays bound `Ŵ_∞(1,·)` above by
`max_τ Ŵ_∞(0,τ) + π/2`, the parity term `(−1)^ε·\tfrac12·π/\cosh(πτ)` being
bounded by `π/2` in absolute value.  **[phase 133, PANEL_1 finding A7: the
bound was written with `π`, true but loose by a factor 2.  Cosmetic; nothing
uses the constant.]**
Either way `Ŵ_∞` is bounded above.  **[The odd archimedean symbol is nowhere
computed in closed form in the repository; (9.12) and this bound are proved
here directly and are all that is used.]**

**(c)**  `S_f` is finite and each `Ŵ_p` is real and bounded by (a), while
`Ŵ_∞` is real, bounded above and `→−∞` by (b); so `T̂` is real, bounded above
by a finite `m`, and `→−∞` along every component.  Adding any constant `c`
preserves all of this, so `T̂+c` is not the zero function on any component.  A
real-analytic function on `R` that is not identically zero has isolated zeros.

**(d)**  Insert Fourier inversion `H(w)=∫Ĥ(χ)χ(w)dχ` into (8.4) and
interchange; the `c·H(1)` term becomes `c∫Ĥ(χ)dχ`, i.e. the constant `c` added
to the symbol, since `δ_1` has symbol identically `1`.  The interchange is
justified termwise: `Ĥ` is supported on finitely many components (unit level)
and Schwartz in `τ` there; the shell sums are dominated by `Σ_k p^{−|k|/2}`
uniformly in `χ`; the unit packets are dominated by `c(κ_p)\log p`, finite on
the finitely many active components by (9.14); and `Ŵ_∞` grows only
logarithmically by (b).  The product of a Schwartz function with a
logarithmically growing one is integrable.  ∎

**Remark 9.7a (what `T̂` is, said plainly — [REBUILT, phase 129, round 4,
finding F12]).**  Expanding (9.11) at an unramified place,

    Ŵ_p(χ) = Σ_{k≥1} 2 Λ(p^k) p^{−k/2} \cos( kτ\log p + kθ_p ),     (9.15)

since `Λ(p^k)=\log p`; and `Ŵ_∞(0,τ) = \log π − Re ψ(1/4+iτ/2)`.  So **`T̂` is
verbatim the prime-power-plus-Gamma side of the Weil explicit formula**, in
the half-density normalization of this paper.  Three things must be said about
that, because the specification's closure rule (§4) asks exactly this
question.

1. *A.10 is not violated.*  Clause A.10 forbids **using** `Λ(n)`, a
   distinguished `Γ_n`, Meyer's explicit formula, a zeta zero, RH or
   positivity.  None is used.  `Λ` is **derived**: (9.15) is the Fourier
   expansion of a geometric series over valuation shells whose weights are the
   proved orbital integrals `τ_p(p^k)=p^{−|k|/2}` of P123.042, and `\log p`
   appears as the shell volume, again from P123.042 §1.  Nothing selects an
   arithmetic family, and no zero of any `L`-function occurs anywhere in §9.
2. *`𝓝 = 0` is not a renaming of the explicit formula.*  The explicit formula
   is an **identity** between this expression and a sum over zeros.
   Theorem 9.8 uses no such identity.  It uses only: `T̂` is real-analytic;
   the finite-place terms are **bounded**; the Gamma term **diverges to
   `−∞`**.  That is a statement about the *size* of a symbol, and it would
   hold for any Dirichlet-type symbol with a dominant logarithmic archimedean
   factor.  Nothing about the location of zeros is used, asserted or implied.
3. *What it does mean.*  It means the numerical theory of row A has the right
   symbol.  That is the point of the construction and it is why the invariant
   is worth having; it is not a proof of anything about zeros, and §14.1
   records the negative consequence (no Hodge-index shape) alongside.


**Theorem 9.7b (Mittag–Leffler form of the local symbols; the universal Tate
residue; and the contour identity).**  **[REBUILT, phase 130.]**  Fix a
component `𝔠 = (ε,(κ_q))` of `Ĉ_S` and write `τ` for its real coordinate.

  (a) *Finite places.*  Let `p ∈ S_f` with `κ_p = 1`, `L := log p`,
      `θ_p := arg ∏_{q∈S_f, q≠p} κ_q(p)`.  Then `Ŵ_p(𝔠,·)` extends from `R`
      to a **meromorphic** function on `C`, `2π/L`-periodic in `τ`, given by

        Ŵ_p(τ) = −log p
                 − i Σ_{k∈Z}^{sym} [ 1/(τ − τ_k^+) − 1/(τ − τ_k^−) ],
        τ_k^± := ±i/2 + (2πk − θ_p)/log p ,                         (9.7b.1)

      the sum taken symmetrically (`Σ^{sym}_k := lim_{K→∞}Σ_{|k|≤K}`) and
      locally uniformly convergent off the poles.  All poles are simple; the
      residue at `τ_k^+` is `−i` and at `τ_k^−` is `+i`, for **every** `k` and
      every `p`.  For `κ_p ≠ 1`, `Ŵ_p(𝔠,·) ≡ −c(κ_p)log p` is constant, hence
      entire with no poles.

  (b) *The real place, even part.*  With `ω_n := i(2n+½)`, `n ≥ 0`,

        Ŵ_∞(0,τ) = log π + γ
                   + Σ_{n≥0} [ −i/(τ−ω_n) + i/(τ+ω_n) − 1/(n+1) ],  (9.7b.2)

      locally uniformly convergent off the poles; `Ŵ_∞(0,·)` is meromorphic
      on `C` with simple poles at `±ω_n` and residues `∓i`.

  (c) *The universal Tate residue, and the overcount.*  Both `±i/2` occur in
      (9.7b.1) (as `τ_0^±`) and in (9.7b.2) (as `±ω_0`) exactly when
      `θ_p ≡ 0 (mod 2π)`, in particular on the principal component
      `𝔠_0 := (ε=0, κ_q = 1 ∀q)`.  On `𝔠_0` every place of `S` therefore
      contributes a simple pole at `τ = ±i/2` with residue `∓i`, and

        Res_{τ=+i/2} T̂_S(𝔠_0,·) = −i(|S_f|+1),
        Res_{τ=−i/2} T̂_S(𝔠_0,·) = +i(|S_f|+1).                     (9.7b.3)

  (d) *Contour identity at a finite place.*  Normalize `dχ|_𝔠 = dτ/2π`, as in
      P123.035 (4.1).  Let `F ∈ D_A` be supported on `𝔠_0` with profile
      `f ∈ C_c^∞(R)` in `t = log|w|_S`, `F̂(τ) = ∫ f(t)e^{−iτt}dt`.  Then for
      every `h > ½`

        W_p(F) = Σ_{k∈Z} F̂( i/2 + 2πk/log p )
                 + (1/2π) ∫_{Im τ = h} F̂(τ) Ŵ_p(τ) dτ ,            (9.7b.4)

      both terms absolutely convergent, and the sum is a Poisson
      periodization of the Tate-twisted profile,

        Σ_{k∈Z} F̂( i/2 + 2πk/log p ) = (log p) Σ_{m∈Z} f(m log p) p^{m/2}.
                                                                    (9.7b.5)

  (e) *Contour identity at the real place.*  For the same `F` and any
      `½ < h < 5/2`,

        W_∞(F) = F̂(i/2) + (1/2π)∫_{Im τ = h} F̂(τ)Ŵ_∞(0,τ)dτ
               = λ_+(F) + (1/2π)∫_{Im τ = h} F̂(τ)Ŵ_∞(0,τ)dτ .      (9.7b.6)

*Proof.*  **(a)**  By (9.11) with `ζ = ζ_p(χ) = p^{−1/2}\overline{η_p(χ)}
 = e^{−L/2 + i(τL+θ_p)}` one has, for real `τ`,

    Ŵ_p = L·2Re( ζ/(1−ζ) ) = L[ 1/(1−e^{−w}) + 1/(1−e^{−w̃}) − 2 ],
    w := L/2 − i(τL+θ_p),   w̃ := L/2 + i(τL+θ_p),                  (9.7b.7)

since `ζ/(1−ζ) = 1/(1−ζ) − 1` and `\barζ = e^{−w̃}` for real `τ`.  The right
side of (9.7b.7) is a meromorphic function of `τ ∈ C` (two exponentials), and
it is `2π/L`-periodic because `τ ↦ τ + 2π/L` sends `w ↦ w − 2πi`,
`w̃ ↦ w̃ + 2πi`.  Now use the classical partial-fraction expansion

    1/(1−e^{−z}) = ½ + ½ coth(z/2) = ½ + Σ_{k∈Z}^{sym} 1/(z − 2πik),  (9.7b.8)

valid for `z ∈ C ∖ 2πiZ` with locally uniform symmetric convergence (it is
`coth` — Mittag–Leffler for the meromorphic function `½coth(z/2)`, whose poles
are simple at `2πik` with residue `1`; the symmetric partial sums converge
because the `k` and `−k` terms combine to `2z/(z²+4π²k²)`).  Apply (9.7b.8) to
`z = w` and to `z = w̃`:

    Ŵ_p = L[ 1 + Σ^{sym}_k ( 1/(w−2πik) + 1/(w̃−2πik) ) − 2 ]
        = −L + L Σ^{sym}_k ( 1/(w−2πik) + 1/(w̃−2πik) ).

Finally `w̃ − 2πik = iL(τ − τ_k^+)` with `τ_k^+ = i/2 + (2πk−θ_p)/L`
(indeed `iL(τ − i/2 − (2πk−θ_p)/L) = iLτ + L/2 + iθ_p − 2πik = w̃ − 2πik`),
and `w − 2πik = −iL(τ − τ_{−k}^−)` with `τ_{−k}^− = −i/2 − (2πk+θ_p)/L`
(indeed `−iL(τ + i/2 + (2πk+θ_p)/L) = −iLτ + L/2 − iθ_p − 2πik = w − 2πik`).
Hence `L/(w̃−2πik) = −i/(τ−τ_k^+)` and `L/(w−2πik) = +i/(τ−τ_{−k}^−)`;
re-indexing `k ↦ −k` in the second family (legitimate inside a symmetric sum)
gives (9.7b.1).  Residues: `−i` and `+i` respectively, read off the displayed
simple poles.  The ramified case is (9.11).

**(b)**  By Theorem 9.7(b), `Ŵ_∞(0,τ) = log π − Re ψ(¼+iτ/2)` for real `τ`.
Use the Gauss series `ψ(z) = −γ + Σ_{n≥0}[1/(n+1) − 1/(n+z)]`, locally
uniformly convergent off `−N_0`.  For real `τ`,
`Re ψ(¼+iτ/2) = ½[ψ(¼+iτ/2) + ψ(¼−iτ/2)]`, and both terms are meromorphic in
`τ ∈ C`, so the right-hand side of (9.7b.2) is the analytic continuation.  Now

    n + ¼ + iτ/2 = (i/2)(τ − ω_n),   n + ¼ − iτ/2 = (−i/2)(τ + ω_n),

with `ω_n = i(2n+½)`, so `1/(n+¼+iτ/2) = −2i/(τ−ω_n)` and
`1/(n+¼−iτ/2) = 2i/(τ+ω_n)`.  Therefore

    ½[ψ(¼+iτ/2)+ψ(¼−iτ/2)]
      = −γ + Σ_{n≥0}[ 1/(n+1) + i/(τ−ω_n) − i/(τ+ω_n) ],

and subtracting from `log π` gives (9.7b.2).  The poles are simple at `±ω_n`
with residues `∓i`.  (The bracketed terms are `O(n^{−2})` for fixed `τ`, so the
series converges absolutely and locally uniformly off the poles.)

**(c)**  `τ_0^± = ±i/2 − θ_p/L` equals `±i/2` iff `θ_p ≡ 0 (mod 2π)`; on `𝔠_0`
every `κ_q = 1`, so `θ_p = 0`.  `ω_0 = i/2`.  Summing the residues of the
`|S_f|` finite terms and the archimedean term gives (9.7b.3).

**(d)**  Restricting (9.13) to the single component `𝔠_0` with the stated
normalization gives `W_p(F) = (1/2π)∫_R F̂(τ)Ŵ_p(τ)dτ` (the other components
do not meet `supp F̂`, and the constant `c` is not part of `W_p`).  `F̂` is
entire of exponential type with `|F̂(σ+iy)| ≤ C_N e^{R|y|}(1+|σ|)^{−N}` for
every `N`, `R` the radius of `supp f` (Paley–Wiener; integrate by parts `N`
times).  Fix `h > ½`.  On the closed strip `0 ≤ Im τ ≤ h` the poles of `Ŵ_p`
are exactly the points `i/2 + 2πk/L`, `k ∈ Z` — all of them at height `½`, by
(9.7b.1) with `θ_p = 0` (the family `τ_k^−` has height `−½`).  Let
`R_j := 2π(j+½)/L`, `j ∈ N`, and integrate `F̂Ŵ_p` over the boundary of the
rectangle `[−R_j,R_j] × [0,h]`, positively oriented.  By `2π/L`-periodicity of
`Ŵ_p` in `Re τ`, `|Ŵ_p|` is bounded on the two vertical sides by a constant
independent of `j` (each side is a translate by an integer multiple of
