`2π/L` of the fixed compact segment `{Re τ = π/L, 0≤Im τ≤h}`, which avoids all
poles); with the rapid decay of `F̂` in `Re τ`, uniform for `0≤Im τ≤h`, the two
side contributions tend to `0`.  On the two horizontal sides `Ŵ_p` is bounded:
for `Im τ = y` one has `|e^{−w}| = e^{−L/2−yL}` and `|e^{−w̃}| = e^{−L/2+yL}`
(from (9.7b.7), now with complex `τ`), so at `y=0` both moduli are
`e^{−L/2}<1`, and at `y=h>½` the first is `<e^{−L}<1` while the second is
`>e^{L(h−1/2)}>1`; in both cases `|1−e^{−w}|` and `|1−e^{−w̃}|` are bounded
below along the whole line, so `Ŵ_p` is bounded there.  Hence, letting
`j→∞`, the residue theorem gives

    ∫_{Im τ=0} F̂Ŵ_p dτ − ∫_{Im τ=h} F̂Ŵ_p dτ
       = 2πi Σ_{k∈Z} Res_{τ=i/2+2πk/L}(F̂Ŵ_p)
       = 2πi Σ_{k∈Z} (−i) F̂(i/2+2πk/L),

the sum converging absolutely by the Paley–Wiener bound at `y=½`.  Dividing by
`2π` gives (9.7b.4).  For (9.7b.5) put `g(t) := f(t)e^{t/2} ∈ C_c^∞(R)`; then
`F̂(i/2 + 2πk/L) = ∫ f(t)e^{t/2}e^{−2πikt/L}dt = ĝ(2πk/L)`, and Poisson
summation for `g ∈ C_c^∞` in the form `Σ_k ĝ(2πk/L) = L Σ_m g(mL)` gives
`(log p)Σ_m f(mL)e^{mL/2} = (log p)Σ_m f(m log p)p^{m/2}`.

**(e)**  The same contour argument with `Ŵ_∞` in place of `Ŵ_p`.  In the strip
`0 ≤ Im τ ≤ h < 5/2` the only pole is `ω_0 = i/2`, of residue `−i`, so the
residue term is `(1/2π)·2πi·(−i)F̂(i/2) = F̂(i/2)`, and `F̂(i/2) = λ_+(F)` by
(9.8).  Boundedness of `Ŵ_∞` on the two horizontal lines and decay on the
vertical sides: `Ŵ_∞(0,τ) = log π − ½[ψ(¼+iτ/2)+ψ(¼−iτ/2)]` and
`ψ(z) = log z + O(|z|^{−1})` uniformly for `|arg z| ≤ π−δ`, so `Ŵ_∞` grows only
logarithmically in `|Re τ|` on the strip away from the poles, which is
dominated by the rapid decay of `F̂`.  ∎

**Remark 9.7c (what Theorem 9.7b says about A.8).**  **[REBUILT, phase 130.]**
Three consequences are used later.

1. *The Tate points are poles of the symbol, with a universal residue.*  The
   two functionals `λ_∓(F) = F̂(∓i/2)` of (9.8) are exactly the residue
   contributions of the symbol at `±i/2`; (9.7b.6) exhibits `λ_+` as a
   **summand** of the archimedean local trace.  **[REBUILT, phase 130, round 2,
   finding F5 of `R130.003` and F19 of `R130.002`: this is a statement about
   the **even** component.  On the odd component `ε = 1` the archimedean
   symbol involves `ψ(¾+iτ/2)`, whose poles are at `i(2n+3/2)` — there is
   **no** Tate pole at `±i/2`.  That is consistent: by (9.8) the functionals
   `λ_∓` are supported on `𝔠_0`, and by Theorem 9.14a the rulings live there
   too.  Theorem 9.7b(b), (d), (e) are proved for `ε = 0` only, which is all
   that is used; Theorem 9.7(b)'s asymptotic covers both parities.]**  This is the analytic reason
   the Tate boundary of Theorem 9.12 is canonical and not an added structure.
2. *The finite places periodize.*  At `p` the residue term is not `λ_+` but its
   `2π/log p`-periodization, equivalently (9.7b.5) the `p`-adic periodization
   of the Tate-twisted profile.  This is the "periodic" of the *periodic*
   ruling determinant of P123.008 and Paper 42.
3. *The scalar symbol overcounts the rulings.*  By (9.7b.3) the total residue
   at `±i/2` is `∓i(|S_f|+1)`: each place of `S` carries its own copy of the
   Tate pole.  So there is no **chart-independent** normalization of the
   scalar form `I_A^S` in which `(λ_−,λ_+)` appears with the hyperbolic
   coefficient `1` — rescaling by `(|S_f|+1)^{−1}` is a normalization, but it
   moves with `S` — and the hyperbolic Tate form `I_Tate` is **not** a
   canonical analytic piece of `I_A^S` at finite `S`.  **[REBUILT, phase 130,
   round 2, finding F12: round 1 wrote "no normalization", which is false as
   literally stated.]**  The Tate structure is carried by `M` and by the
   completion (Theorems 9.12, 9.14a–c), not by the symbol.  Theorem 10.8 turns
   this observation into a theorem.

#### 9.3.3 (T5′): the numerical radical vanishes

**Theorem 9.8 (`𝓝 = 0`).**  **[REBUILT, phase 129, round 3.  This is the
theorem the auditor identified as the decisive one, and it uses no zeta zero,
no `Λ(n)`, no explicit formula: only (8.5), P123.105 §3 and P123.035, all
`PROVED-IN-REPOSITORY`.]**  `𝓝 = \{0\}`; hence `N_A = D_A`, the intersection
pairing `I_A` is nondegenerate on the whole section algebra, and `λ_±`
annihilate `𝓝` trivially.

*Proof.*  Let `F ∈ 𝓝`.  For `G ∈ D_A`,
`(F⋆G^†)^{\wedge}(χ) = F̂(χ)\overline{Ĝ(χ)}`: indeed
`(G^†)^{\wedge}(χ)=∫\overline{G(w^{−1})}\overline{χ(w)}d^*w
 = \overline{∫G(v)\overline{χ(v)}d^*v} = \overline{Ĝ(χ)}`, and convolution
goes to product.  Also `(F⋆G^†)(1)=∫F(u)\overline{G(u)}d^*u`.  By (9.13),

    0 = T(F⋆G^†) = ∫_{Ĉ_S} F̂(χ)\,\overline{Ĝ(χ)}\,(T̂(χ)+c)\,dχ ,      (9.16)

by (9.13).  **[REBUILT, phase 129, round 4, finding F7: the symbol that
governs the radical is `T̂+c`, not `T̂`.]**  By Theorem 9.7(c), `T̂+c` is
real-analytic and not identically zero on any component, for **every** `c`;
so the open constant of (O-A5-3) does not affect this proof.

Fix a component `𝔠` (a system `(κ_q)` and a parity `ε`).  Choose
`G = χ_𝔠 · g`, where `χ_𝔠` is the character `∏κ_q` of the compact part
(a legitimate element of `D_A` after multiplying by the characteristic
function of `∏Z_q^×`) and `g ∈ C_c^∞(R^×)` has the parity `ε`.  Then `Ĝ` is
supported on `𝔠` and equals `ĝ(τ)`, the Fourier transform of a `C_c^∞`
function in the logarithmic coordinate.  (9.16) becomes

    ∫_R F̂(𝔠,τ)\overline{ĝ(τ)} (T̂(𝔠,τ)+c) dτ = 0  for every g ∈ C_c^∞.

The function `m(τ) := F̂(𝔠,τ)(T̂(𝔠,τ)+c)` is locally integrable and of at most
logarithmic growth times Schwartz decay, hence a tempered distribution; the
Fourier transforms of `C_c^∞` functions are dense in the Schwartz space; so
`m = 0` almost everywhere.  By Theorem 9.7(c) the zeros of `T̂(𝔠,·)+c` are
isolated **[phase 133, PANEL_1 finding A5: read "`T̂(𝔠,·)`"; the object named
two sentences earlier, and the one that must have isolated zeros, is
`T̂(𝔠,·)+c`.  No consequence]**, so `F̂(𝔠,τ)=0` off a discrete set, and `F̂(𝔠,·)` is continuous;
hence `F̂ ≡ 0` on `𝔠`.  As `𝔠` was arbitrary, `F̂ ≡ 0`, so `F=0`.  ∎

**Corollary 9.9.**  `N_A = D_A` is Hausdorff and nuclear, `⋆`, `†` and `I_A`
are its own operations, and the boundary radical of P123.007 §4 — obstacle
(O-A6-2) in round 2 — is the radical of the completion, handled by
Theorem 9.13 below.  Obstacle **(O-A6-1) is closed**: `λ_±` annihilate
`𝓝=\{0\}`, so they descend to `N_A`.

#### 9.3.4 (T1)–(T2): the Tate-refined numerical theory

I now transfer P123.017 §§1–4 from its radial domain `D=C_c^∞(R_+^×)` to
`D_A`.  P123.017's status line reads `PROVED-IN-REPOSITORY` for "the
source-defined vector-valued numerical pairing, its closed dagger ideal, its
nondegenerate quotient, and the exact Tate-boundary/primitive sequence"; the
P123.018 override on it concerns global completed-carrier/descent acceptance,
not §§1–4.  Its inputs are: `M=(M_0,M_1)` continuous, multiplicative,
surjective, with `M_0(f^†)=\overline{M_1(f)}` (its (1.2)–(1.3)); `I_∂` the
trace pairing with P123.007's radical properties; `β_T` nondegenerate on
`B_T=C²`.  Every one of these is available on `D_A` with `M=(λ_−,λ_+)`:
multiplicativity, the dagger rule and surjectivity are Theorem 9.1; the
radical properties are Theorem 9.4; `β_T` is (6.4) of P123.014.  The transfer
is therefore verbatim, and I write out the two statements.

**Definition 9.10.**  **[phase 133: `D_A^0` here and throughout §§9–14 is the
primitive sector; the off-identity set of §8 is now `D_A^{off}`.]**  `D_A^0 := ker λ_− ∩ ker λ_+`, the **primitive** section
space — defined with no zero, no sign and no `Λ(n)`.
`I_{Tate}(F,G) := λ_−(F)\overline{λ_+(G)} + λ_+(F)\overline{λ_−(G)}
 = β_T(M F, M G)`;
`I_{vec} := (I_A, I_{Tate})`; `𝓝_W := \{F : I_{vec}(F,G)=0 \;∀G\}`.

**Theorem 9.11 ((T1): `𝓝_W` is a closed `†`-stable two-sided ideal, and it is
zero).**  `𝓝_W = 𝓝 ∩ D_A^0`, it is a closed two-sided `⋆`-ideal stable under
`†`; and by Theorem 9.8, `𝓝_W = \{0\}`.

*Proof.*  *`𝓝_W = 𝓝 ∩ D_A^0`.*  If `F∈𝓝_W`, the first component gives
`F∈𝓝`.  The second says `β_T(MF, MG)=0` for every `G`; `M` is surjective
(Theorem 9.1) and `β_T` is nondegenerate (matrix `[[0,1],[1,0]]`), so `MF=0`,
i.e. `F∈D_A^0`.  The converse is immediate.  This is P123.017 Prop. 2.1.
*Closed `†`-ideal.*  `𝓝` has these properties by Theorem 9.4.  `D_A^0` is
closed (`λ_±` continuous), is an ideal because `λ_∓(F⋆G)=λ_∓(F)λ_∓(G)`
(Theorem 9.1), and is `†`-stable because `λ_∓(F^†)=\overline{λ_±(F)}`, so
`MF=0 ⟺ MF^†=0`.  The intersection inherits all three.  This is P123.017
Thm. 3.1.  *Vanishing* is Theorem 9.8.  ∎

**Theorem 9.12 ((T2): the exact Tate sequence, and (T3): the primitive sector
is nonzero and nondegenerate).**  There is a canonical exact sequence of
topological `D_A`-modules with continuous `⋆` and `†`,

    0 → D_A^{prim} → N_A → B_T → 0 ,
    D_A^{prim} := D_A^0 , N_A = D_A , B_T = C² with β_T = [[0,1],[1,0]],  (9.17)

in which the surjection is `M=(λ_−,λ_+)`, `†` exchanges the two basis
directions `e_∓` of `B_T`, and the external idele classes act on `B_T` by the
two characters `|u|_S^{∓1/2}` of Theorem 9.1 and on the kernel by translation.
The kernel `D_A^{prim}` is **nonzero**, and `I_A` is nondegenerate on it.

*Proof.*  *Exactness and surjectivity* are Theorem 9.1's surjectivity of `ρ_T`
together with Definition 9.10 and `𝓝_W=0` (Theorem 9.11); this is P123.017
(4.1)–(4.3).  *`†` exchanges `e_∓`* is `λ_∓(F^†)=\overline{λ_±(F)}`
(Theorem 9.1), i.e. P123.017 (1.3).  *The module structure on `B_T`* is
(1.4) of P123.017, which the note states is "precisely `ρ_T^{ext}` of
P123.014"; here it is Theorem 9.1 (9.1).  *The pairing matrix* is
P123.014 (6.4).

*(T3) nonzero.*  **[REBUILT, phase 129, round 3: written out explicitly, as
the auditor asked.]**  Take `F,F'` of Theorem 9.5 and put `Φ := F−F'`.  Then:

  (i) `Φ ∈ D_A^0`.  Theorem 9.5 proves `λ_−(F)=λ_−(F')` and
      `λ_+(F)=λ_+(F')` — both `λ_∓(F)` factor as
      `(∫h(x)|x|^{∓1/2}d^*x)·vol(u(1+p^mZ_p))·∏_{q≠p}vol(Z_q^×)`, and the
      middle factor is `vol(1+p^mZ_p)` for both by translation invariance of
      Haar measure on `Z_p^×`.  So `λ_∓(Φ)=0`, i.e. `MΦ=0`.
  (ii) `Φ ≠ 0` in `N_A`.  `T(Φ) = ν(\log p)(p^{j}−p^{j'}) ≠ 0` by (9.5),
      while `T` vanishes on `𝓝` (proved inside Theorem 9.5), so `Φ∉𝓝`; and
      `𝓝=0` anyway by Theorem 9.8.
  (iii) Hence `[Φ]` is a **nonzero primitive class**, and it is exhibited by
      an exact, regularization-free computation on `D_A^{off}`.
      **[phase 133: the witness `Φ = F−F'` lies in **both** `D_A^0` (the
      primitive sector, by (i)) and `D_A^{off}` (Theorem 9.5's proof), and it
      is the latter membership that makes the computation regularization-free
      via Theorem 8.8.]**

*Nondegeneracy on the kernel.*  Let `Φ ∈ D_A^0` satisfy `I_A(Φ,G)=0` for every
`G ∈ D_A^0`.  The conjugate-linear functional `G ↦ I_A(Φ,G)` vanishes on the
subspace `D_A^0 = ker λ_−∩ker λ_+` of codimension two, hence equals
`a\,\overline{λ_−} + b\,\overline{λ_+}` for scalars `a,b`.  Test it on
translates: for `w∈C_S` put `G_w(v):=G(w^{−1}v)`; then
`Ĝ_w(χ)=\overline{χ(w)}Ĝ(χ)` and `λ_∓(G_w)=|w|_S^{∓1/2}λ_∓(G)`.  By (9.16),

    I_A(Φ,G_w) = ∫ Φ̂(χ)\,\overline{Ĝ(χ)}\,χ(w)\,(T̂(χ)+c)\,dχ ,

which is the inverse Fourier transform at `w` of the **integrable** measure
`Φ̂\overline{Ĝ}(T̂+c)dχ` (Schwartz times logarithmic growth on finitely many
components), hence **bounded** in `w`.  The right-hand side is
**[REBUILT, phase 129, round 4, finding F8: the exponents were swapped.]**
**[phase 133, PANEL_1 finding A4: the round-4 note repaired the wrong display —
the exponents written were inconsistent with the rule quoted in the same
sentence.  Corrected here, together with the two limit clauses; the conclusion
`a=b=0` is unchanged, the two limits merely exchange roles.]**
`a\,\overline{λ_−(G_w)} + b\,\overline{λ_+(G_w)}
 = a|w|_S^{−1/2}\overline{λ_−(G)} + b|w|_S^{1/2}\overline{λ_+(G)}`, using
`λ_∓(G_w)=|w|_S^{∓1/2}λ_∓(G)` and reality of `|w|_S`.  This is unbounded as
`|w|_S→0` unless `a\overline{λ_−(G)}=0`, and unbounded as `|w|_S→∞` unless
`b\overline{λ_+(G)}=0`.  Choosing `G∈D_A` with `G≥0`,
`G≠0`, so that `λ_±(G)>0`, forces `a=b=0`.  Then `I_A(Φ,·)≡0` on all of
`D_A`, so `Φ∈𝓝=0`.  ∎

#### 9.3.5 (T4): "contains" versus "quotient" — a negative theorem

**Theorem 9.13 (no splitting, in the strongest available sense).**
**[REBUILT, phase 129, round 4, finding F5.  Round 3 proved only that the
`I_A`-orthogonal complement of the primitive sector vanishes.  The auditor's
stronger statement is correct and replaces it: there is no splitting of any
kind compatible with the module structure.]**

  (a) *No `D_A`-module splitting.*  There is no `D_A`-module map
      `σ : B_T → N_A` with `M∘σ = id`, where `D_A` acts on `B_T` through
      `M=(λ_−,λ_+)`.

  (b) *No orthogonal splitting.*  `S := (D_A^{prim})^{⊥I_A} = \{0\}`, and the
      `I_{vec}`-orthogonal complement coincides with it.

*Proof of (a).*  Suppose `σ` existed and put `Φ_∓ := σ(e_∓) ∈ D_A`.  Then
`M(Φ_∓)=e_∓ ≠ 0`, so `Φ_∓ ≠ 0`; and `D_A`-linearity reads

    a ⋆ Φ_∓ = λ_∓(a)·Φ_∓   for every a ∈ D_A.                       (9.19)

Taking Fourier transforms, `â(χ)Φ̂_∓(χ) = λ_∓(a)Φ̂_∓(χ)` for every `χ ∈ Ĉ_S`
and every `a`.  Suppose `Φ̂_∓(χ_0) ≠ 0` for some `χ_0`.  Then

    â(χ_0) = λ_∓(a)   for every a ∈ D_A.                            (9.20)

But `λ_∓(a) = â(∓i/2)` by (9.8) — the value of the analytic continuation of
`â` at a point **off the unitary axis** — whereas `â(χ_0)` is a value **on**
it.  These two linear functionals are independent: for `w ∈ C_S` and the
translate `a_w(v):=a(w^{−1}v)` one has `|â_w(χ_0)| = |â(χ_0)|`, constant in
`w`, while `|λ_∓(a_w)| = |w|_S^{∓1/2}|λ_∓(a)|`, which is unbounded in `w`
whenever `λ_∓(a) ≠ 0`.  So (9.20) fails for any `a` with `λ_∓(a) ≠ 0` after a
suitable translation — concretely, take `a ≥ 0`, `a ≠ 0`, so `λ_∓(a) > 0`, and
translate until `|λ_∓(a_w)| > |â(χ_0)|`.  Contradiction.  Hence
`Φ̂_∓ ≡ 0`, so `Φ_∓ = 0`, contradicting `M(Φ_∓)=e_∓`.

*Proof of (b).*  Let `F ∈ S`.  The conjugate-linear functional
`G ↦ I_A(F,G)` vanishes on the codimension-two subspace
`D_A^0 = ker λ_− ∩ ker λ_+`, hence equals
`a\,\overline{λ_−} + b\,\overline{λ_+}` for scalars `a,b`.  Evaluate on
translates `G_w`: by (9.16) the left side is the inverse Fourier transform at
`w` of the integrable measure `F̂\overline{Ĝ}(T̂+c)dχ` (Schwartz times
logarithmic growth on finitely many components), hence **bounded** in `w`;
the right side is
`a|w|_S^{−1/2}\overline{λ_−(G)} + b|w|_S^{1/2}\overline{λ_+(G)}`, unbounded as
`|w|_S→0` unless `a\overline{λ_−(G)}=0` and as `|w|_S→∞` unless
`b\overline{λ_+(G)}=0`.  **[phase 133, PANEL_1 finding A4: same exponent swap
as in Theorem 9.12's proof.]**  Taking `G ≥ 0`, `G≠0`, forces `a=b=0`, so
`I_A(F,·)≡0` on all of `D_A` and `F ∈ 𝓝 = 0`.  For `I_{vec}`:
`I_{Tate}(F,G)=β_T(MF,MG)=0` automatically when `G ∈ D_A^{prim}`, so the
`I_{vec}`-orthogonal of `D_A^{prim}` equals its `I_A`-orthogonal.  ∎

**Remark 9.13a (a weak hyperbolic plane exists, and it is not the ruling
plane).**  **[REBUILT, phase 129, round 4, finding F5.]**  By Theorem 9.7(c)
and §14.1, whenever `c > −m` the Hermitian form `I_A(F,F)=∫|F̂|²(T̂+c)dχ` is
**indefinite**, so isotropic vectors exist, and by the standard structure
theory of an indefinite Hermitian form one may choose `F,G` with
`I_A(F,F)=I_A(G,G)=0`, `I_A(F,G)=1`: an abstract hyperbolic plane inside
`N_A`, with matrix `[[0,1],[1,0]]`.

**This is not the ruling plane, and it must not be presented as one.**  Three
reasons.  (i) It is not canonical: it depends on an arbitrary choice of two
isotropic vectors, and any indefinite form has a continuum of such planes.
(ii) It carries no arithmetic: the construction uses only a sign change of
`T̂+c`, and would produce a hyperbolic plane for any indefinite form
whatsoever.  (iii) It is not compatible with the module structure and does not
map onto `B_T`; by Theorem 9.13(a) no subspace of `N_A` can, because the
eigenvalue equation (9.19) has no solutions.  The ruling plane is
characterized by being the image of the Tate boundary map `M`, i.e. by the
module structure, and Theorem 9.13(a) says precisely that this characterization
is only realizable on a quotient.  `A_MIXED_METRIC_WORK` makes the same point
for its own hyperbolic plane of tests: "An isometry of scalar forms on a ruling
plane is insufficient for the arithmetic comparison … This does NOT identify
those tests with the two geometric ruling objects."

#### 9.3.6 (T6): the completion, and (O-A6-2)

**Theorem 9.14.**  Let `D_A'` be the strong dual of the nuclear LF space
`D_A`, and

    J : N_A → D_A' ⊕ B_T,   J(F) = ( G ↦ I_A(F,G),\; M(F) ).        (9.18)

`J` is injective; `N_A^{dist} := \overline{J(N_A)}` is complete and nuclear;
the vector pairing `I_{vec}((ℓ,m),G) = (ℓ(G), β_T(m,M G))` is separating on
`N_A^{dist}`; and `D_A` acts continuously on it by
`(a·ℓ)(G) = ℓ(a^†⋆G)`, `M(a⋆F)=M(a)M(F)`, extending `†` by duality.

*Proof.*  Injectivity: `J(F)=0` gives `F∈𝓝_W=0` (Theorem 9.11).  Closed
subspace of a complete nuclear space is complete and nuclear.  Separation: if
`I_{vec}((ℓ,m),G)=0` for all `G` then `ℓ=0`, and surjectivity of `M` with
nondegeneracy of `β_T` gives `m=0`.  Module structure: the displayed formulas
are continuous.  This is P123.017 §§5–6 verbatim, and every hypothesis it uses
— `D_A` nuclear LF, `M` surjective, `β_T` nondegenerate, `I_A` continuous — is
established here (Theorems 9.1, 9.3, 9.8).  ∎

**Obstacle (O-A6-2) is closed.**  P123.017 §5 states that this construction
"removes the unspecified *boundary radical* left in P123.007", and that is
exactly what Theorem 9.14 does here: the completion is separated against the
dense test sector, so the **numerical separation** that P123.007 §4 left
open is achieved: no element of the completion pairs to zero with the whole
test sector under the **vector** pairing `I_vec`.  **[REBUILT, phase 130,
round 2, finding F9 of `R130.002`.  Round 1 wrote that P123.007 §4's `R_∂`
"does not survive", which contradicts Theorem 9.14c, where the radical of the
**scalar** component is computed and found two-dimensional.  The two
statements are about different pairings: the scalar radical `R_∂` of (9.24) is
the ruling plane, and it is separated by the **Tate** component, so the
*vector* radical is zero.  That is the correct form of "(O-A6-2) is closed".]**  What P123.017 §6 warns is that `N_A^{dist}` is a complete
**numerical module**, not a distribution algebra: products of two arbitrary
elements are not defined.  A.6 does not ask for them.

**Lemma 9.14a0 (a uniform trace seminorm on bounded sets).**  **[REBUILT,
phase 130.]**  For `G ∈ D_A` put

    N(G) := ∫_{Ĉ_S} |Ĝ(χ)| ( |T̂(χ)| + |c| ) dχ .                    (9.20a)

Then `N(G) < ∞` for every `G`, `|I_A(F,G)| ≤ (sup_χ|F̂(χ)|)·N(G)` for all
`F,G ∈ D_A`, and `N` is **bounded on every bounded subset** of the nuclear LF
space `D_A`.

*Proof.*  A bounded subset `B` of an LF space is contained in, and bounded in,
one Fréchet step; that is, there are a compact `K ⊆ C_S` and an integer `N ≥ 0`
such that every `G ∈ B` is supported in `K`, is invariant under the open
subgroup `U_N := ∏_{q∈S_f}(1+q^NZ_q)` of the compact part, and has all the
step's seminorms bounded by a constant `C_B`.  Invariance under `U_N` forces
`Ĝ(ε,τ;(κ_q)) = 0` unless every `κ_q` is trivial on `1+q^NZ_q`, and there are
only finitely many such systems `(κ_q)`; together with the two parities this
leaves finitely many components, say `d_N` of them.  On each of them
`Ĝ(𝔠,τ) = ∫ G_𝔠(t)e^{−iτt}dt` where `G_𝔠` is the corresponding profile,
supported in the fixed compact log-window of `K`, with `|G_𝔠|, |G_𝔠''| ≤ C'_B`;
integrating by parts twice gives `|Ĝ(𝔠,τ)| ≤ C''_B·min(1,τ^{−2})` uniformly in
`𝔠` and in `G ∈ B`.  By Theorem 9.7, `|T̂(𝔠,τ)| ≤ A_S + B_S\log(2+|τ|)` for
constants depending only on `S`.  Hence
`N(G) ≤ d_N C''_B ∫_R min(1,τ^{−2})(A_S+B_S\log(2+|τ|))\frac{dτ}{2π} < ∞`,
a bound independent of `G ∈ B`.  Finiteness for a single `G` is the case
`B = {G}`.  The inequality is (9.16) with `|F̂| ≤ sup|F̂|`.  ∎

#### 9.3.6a The rulings exist in the completion, and the completion splits

**[REBUILT, phase 130.  Theorem 9.13(a) proves that no `D_A`-module splitting
of the Tate boundary exists **inside** `D_A`.  It does not say the rulings are
absent from the theory.  The three theorems below construct them, as explicit
limits of spread Tate characters, inside the completion `N_A^{dist}` of
Theorem 9.14, and identify the plane they span with the boundary radical of
P123.007 §4.  Nothing here contradicts Theorem 9.13(a); Remark 9.14d explains
exactly why.]**

**Theorem 9.14a (the rulings are limits of test classes).**  Fix a real
`ψ ∈ C_c^∞((0,∞))` with **`ψ ≥ 0`**, `∫_R ψ(s)ds = 1` and `supp ψ ⊆ [a,b]`,
`0<a<b<∞`.  **[phase 133, PANEL_1 finding A6: `ψ ≥ 0` was used implicitly in
(a) — the strict inequalities `0 < λ_−(F_n)`, `0 < λ_+(G_n)` need it — and
Theorem 10.8b assumes it separately.  Added to the hypothesis; nothing else
uses the strict positivity, only the upper bound
`‖ψ‖_{L¹}e^{−na}`.]**  For
`n ≥ 1` define `F_n, G_n ∈ D_A` by

    F_n(w) := n^{−1}|w|_S^{−1/2} ψ( (log|w|_S)/n ) ,
    G_n(w) := n^{−1}|w|_S^{+1/2} ψ( −(log|w|_S)/n ) ,                (9.21)

functions of `|w|_S` alone (hence smooth in the `R^×` coordinate, constant on
the compact coordinates, compactly supported).  Then:

  (a) `λ_+(F_n) = 1` exactly, and `0 < λ_−(F_n) ≤ ‖ψ‖_{L^1}e^{−na}`;
      `λ_−(G_n) = 1` exactly, and `0 < λ_+(G_n) ≤ ‖ψ‖_{L^1}e^{−na}`.
      Hence `M(F_n) → e_+` and `M(G_n) → e_−` in `B_T`.
  (b) `F̂_n` and `Ĝ_n` are supported on the principal component `𝔠_0`, and

        sup_χ |F̂_n(χ)| ≤ ‖ψ‖_{L^1} e^{−na/2},
        sup_χ |Ĝ_n(χ)| ≤ ‖ψ‖_{L^1} e^{−na/2} .                      (9.22)

  (c) `I_A(F_n,·) → 0` and `I_A(G_n,·) → 0` in the strong dual `D_A'`, i.e.
      **uniformly on every bounded subset of `D_A`**.
  (d) Consequently, in `D_A' ⊕ B_T`,

        J(F_n) → (0, e_+) =: e_+^{dist},   J(G_n) → (0, e_−) =: e_−^{dist},

      and both limits lie in `N_A^{dist} = \overline{J(N_A)}`.
  (e) `D_A` acts on them by the two Tate characters:
      `a · e_±^{dist} = λ_±(a)·e_±^{dist}` for every `a ∈ D_A`, and `†`
      exchanges them.

*Proof.*  Write `t = log|w|_S`.  By Lemma 3.3 and the normalization of
Remark 8.6a, Haar measure on `C_S` is `d^*w = dt ⊗ μ` with `μ` a **probability**
measure on the compact group `{±1}×∏_{q∈S_f}Z_q^×` (the archimedean factor
`d^*x_∞ = dx/(2|x|)` gives the two signs total mass one, and each `Z_q^×` was
normalized to mass one in Theorem 8A.5).  Hence for a function of `|w|_S` alone,
`∫_{C_S}Φ(|w|_S)d^*w = ∫_R Φ(e^t)dt`.

**(a)**  `λ_∓(F_n) = ∫ F_n(w)|w|_S^{∓1/2}d^*w = ∫_R n^{−1}e^{−t/2}ψ(t/n)e^{∓t/2}dt`.
For the upper sign (`λ_−`) substitute `t = ns`:
`λ_−(F_n) = ∫ψ(s)e^{−ns}ds`, which is `>0` (as `ψ` may be taken `≥0`; in
general the bound below is what is used) and bounded in modulus by
`e^{−na}‖ψ‖_{L^1}` because `s ≥ a` on `supp ψ`.  For the lower sign,
`λ_+(F_n) = ∫ n^{−1}ψ(t/n)dt = ∫ψ(s)ds = 1`.  The statements for `G_n` are the
same computation after `t ↦ −t`.  Since `M = (λ_−,λ_+)`, `M(F_n) → (0,1)=e_+`
and `M(G_n) → (1,0)=e_−`.

**(b)**  `F_n` is constant on the compact coordinates and even in the sign, so
by orthogonality `F̂_n` vanishes on every component except `𝔠_0`, where
`F̂_n(τ) = ∫ n^{−1}e^{−t/2}ψ(t/n)e^{−iτt}dt = ∫ψ(s)e^{−ns(½+iτ)}ds`.  Hence
`|F̂_n(τ)| ≤ ∫|ψ(s)|e^{−ns/2}ds ≤ e^{−na/2}‖ψ‖_{L^1}`, uniformly in `τ`.  The
same for `Ĝ_n(τ) = ∫ψ(s)e^{−ns(½−iτ)}ds`.

**(c)**  By Lemma 9.14a0 and (9.22), for every bounded `B ⊆ D_A`,

    sup_{G∈B} |I_A(F_n,G)| ≤ (sup_χ|F̂_n|)·sup_{G∈B}N(G)
                           ≤ ‖ψ‖_{L^1}e^{−na/2}·C_B → 0 .

**(d)**  `J(F_n) = (I_A(F_n,·), M(F_n))`; the first component tends to `0` in
`D_A'` by (c) and the second to `e_+` by (a).  Each `J(F_n)` lies in `J(N_A)`,
so the limit lies in its closure `N_A^{dist}`.

**(e)**  By Theorem 9.14 the action is `(a·(ℓ,m)) = (a·ℓ, M(a)m)`.  On
`(0,e_+)` the first component stays `0` and the second is
`M(a)e_+ = (λ_−(a),λ_+(a))·(0,1) = λ_+(a)e_+` (componentwise multiplication in
`B_T`).  `†` acts on `B_T` by `♯`, which exchanges `e_−` and `e_+`
(Theorem 9.12).  ∎

**Theorem 9.14b (the completion splits canonically as a `D_A`-module).**
Let `M̄ : D_A' ⊕ B_T → B_T` be the second-component projection and

    σ : B_T → N_A^{dist},   σ(m) := (0,m),
    N_A^{dist,prim} := N_A^{dist} ∩ (D_A' ⊕ 0) = ker( M̄|_{N_A^{dist}} ).

Then `σ` is a continuous injective `D_A`-module map landing in `N_A^{dist}`,
`M̄∘σ = id_{B_T}`, and

    N_A^{dist} = N_A^{dist,prim} ⊕ σ(B_T)                            (9.23)

as topological `D_A`-modules, **canonically** — no choice is made.  Moreover
`†` preserves the decomposition, acting by `♯` on the second summand.

*Proof.*  `σ` lands in `N_A^{dist}` because `σ(e_±) = e_±^{dist}` by
Theorem 9.14a(d) and `N_A^{dist}` is a linear subspace; it is clearly
continuous, injective, and `M̄σ = id`.  Module map: `a·σ(m) = (0,M(a)m) =
σ(M(a)m)` by Theorem 9.14a(e), and `M(a)m` is the `D_A`-action on `B_T`.  For
(9.23): given `x = (ℓ,m) ∈ N_A^{dist}` we have `σ(m) ∈ N_A^{dist}`, hence
`x − σ(m) = (ℓ,0) ∈ N_A^{dist}` and lies in `N_A^{dist,prim}`; so
`x = (ℓ,0) + σ(m)`, and the two summands intersect in `0` since `M̄` kills the
first and is injective on the second.  Both summands are closed
(`N_A^{dist,prim} = ker M̄|_{N_A^{dist}}` with `M̄` continuous; `σ(B_T)` is a
finite-dimensional, hence closed, subspace), and the two projections
`x ↦ σ(M̄x)` and `x ↦ x − σ(M̄x)` are continuous, so the sum is topological.
Both summands are submodules, by the displayed action.  `†` acts by
`(ℓ,m)^† = (ℓ^†, m^♯)` (Theorem 9.14), which preserves each summand.  ∎

**Theorem 9.14c (the rulings are `I_A`-null, and they are exactly the scalar
radical of the completion).**  **[REBUILT, phase 130, round 2, finding F9 of
`R130.002`: (9.24) is a **definition internal to this paper** — the radical of
the *scalar* component of `I_vec` against the *dense test sector*.  It is
**not** P123.007 (4.2), which is the radical of `I_A` against the whole of
P123.007's own completion `completion(N_A^c)`, an object this paper does not
construct and against which `I_A` is in any case not defined here (P123.017
§6).  The two are **analogues**, and the identification claimed in round 1 is
downgraded to that.]**  Let

    R_∂ := { x ∈ N_A^{dist} : I_vec(x,G) has vanishing first component
             for every G ∈ D_A }
         = { (ℓ,m) ∈ N_A^{dist} : ℓ = 0 } .                          (9.24)

Then:

  (a) `R_∂ = σ(B_T)`, a two-dimensional plane — the exact analogue, inside
      this paper's completion, of the "unspecified boundary radical" P123.007
      §4 (4.2) leaves open — on which the **Tate** component
      of `I_vec` is the nondegenerate hyperbolic form
      `β_T(m,MG)`, and on which the induced form `β_T` has matrix
      `[[0,1],[1,0]]` in the basis `e_∓^{dist}`.
  (b) `I_A` is null on the plane in the limit sense:
      `lim_{n,m→∞} I_A(F_n,G_m) = 0`, uniformly in one index while the other
      tends to infinity, whereas `β_T(M F_n, M G_m) → β_T(e_+,e_−) = 1`.
  (c) `R_∂ ∩ J(N_A) = 0`.

*Proof.*  **(a)**  The two descriptions in (9.24) agree because
`I_vec((ℓ,m),G) = (ℓ(G), β_T(m,MG))` (Theorem 9.14), and `ℓ(G)=0` for all `G`
iff `ℓ = 0`.  By Theorem 9.14b, `{(ℓ,m) ∈ N_A^{dist} : ℓ=0} = σ(B_T)`.  The
form `β_T` on `B_T` is (6.4) of P123.014, matrix `[[0,1],[1,0]]` in `e_∓`
(P123.008 (5.1)).

**(b)**  `‖G_m‖ := N(G_m)` is **bounded in `m`**: by the computation in
Theorem 9.14a(b), `Ĝ_m` lives on `𝔠_0` and
`Ĝ_m(τ) = ∫ψ(s)e^{−ms(½−iτ)}ds`; two integrations by parts in `s` give
`|Ĝ_m(τ)| ≤ C_ψ min(1, (m|τ|)^{−2})`, with `C_ψ` depending only on `ψ`, so

    N(G_m) ≤ C_ψ ∫_R min(1,(m|τ|)^{−2})(A_S+B_S\log(2+|τ|))\frac{dτ}{2π}
           ≤ C_ψ C_S ,

uniformly in `m ≥ 1` (the integral is in fact `O(m^{−1}\log m)`).  Therefore
`|I_A(F_n,G_m)| ≤ (sup|F̂_n|)N(G_m) ≤ ‖ψ‖_{L^1}C_ψC_S e^{−na/2}`, which tends
to `0` as `n → ∞` **uniformly in `m`**; by symmetry the same holds with the
roles exchanged.  Meanwhile `β_T(MF_n,MG_m) = λ_−(F_n)\overline{λ_+(G_m)} +
λ_+(F_n)\overline{λ_−(G_m)} → 0·0 + 1·1 = 1` by Theorem 9.14a(a).

**(c)**  If `J(F) ∈ R_∂` then `I_A(F,G) = 0` for every `G`, i.e. `F ∈ 𝓝 = 0`
(Theorem 9.8), so `J(F)=0`.  ∎

**Definition 9.14c0 (admissible approximations of the rulings, and `N`-bounded
nets).**  **[REBUILT, phase 130, round 2, findings F2 of `R130.002` and F4 of
`R130.003`.  Round 1 stated the `I_A`-nullity of the rulings for the one family
(9.21) and then spoke of "the continuous extension of `L_S` to `σ(B_T)`", which
is not a defined object: `I_A` does **not** extend to pairs of elements of
`N_A^{dist}`, and the paper says so (P123.017 §6, §9.3.6).  The repair is to
name the class of approximations along which the statement is made, prove the
statement for the whole class, and verify that (9.21) belongs to it.]**

A net `(F_α)` in `D_A` is an **admissible approximation of `e_+`** if

    (i) M(F_α) → e_+ in B_T ,      (ii) ‖F_α‖_{L^1(d^*w)} → 0 ;     (9.25)

`admissible approximation of e_−` is the same with `e_−`.  A net `(G_β)` in
`D_A` is **`N`-bounded** if `\sup_β N(G_β) < ∞`, `N` as in (9.20a).

Condition (ii) is a genuine restriction, and it is what "spread" means: by
AM–GM, for `F ≥ 0` with `M(F)` near `e_+` one has only
`‖F‖_{L^1} ≤ ½(λ_++λ_−) ≈ ½`, so (ii) says the mass of `F_α` escapes to
`|w|_S → ∞` at the rate that makes the Tate weight `|w|^{1/2}` carry all of it.

**Theorem 9.14c′ (the rulings are `I_A`-null along every admissible pair).**
**[REBUILT, phase 130, round 2.]**

  (a) If `(F_α)` is an admissible approximation of `e_±` then
      `J(F_α) → σ(e_±)` in `D_A' ⊕ B_T`, i.e. `I_A(F_α,·) → 0` uniformly on
      bounded subsets of `D_A`.
  (b) If moreover `(G_β)` is any `N`-bounded net, then

        |I_A(F_α,G_β)| ≤ ‖F_α‖_{L^1}·\sup_β N(G_β) → 0,

      **uniformly in `β`**; in particular for any admissible, `N`-bounded
      approximation `(G_β)` of `e_∓`,

        lim_α lim_β I_A(F_α,G_β) = lim_β lim_α I_A(F_α,G_β) = 0,
        while  β_T(M F_α, M G_β) → β_T(e_±,e_∓) = 1 .                (9.26)

  (c) The family (9.21) is admissible and `N`-bounded: `(F_n)` for `e_+`,
      `(G_m)` for `e_−`.

*Proof.*  **(a)**  `\sup_χ|\hat F_α(χ)| ≤ ‖F_α‖_{L^1}` (Riemann–Lebesgue), so
for `G` in a bounded set `B`, `|I_A(F_α,G)| ≤ ‖F_α‖_{L^1}N(G) ≤
‖F_α‖_{L^1}\sup_{B}N` by Lemma 9.14a0 and (9.16); and the second component is
(i).  **(b)** is the same inequality with `\sup_β N(G_β)` in place of
`\sup_B N`, and `β_T` is continuous on `B_T×B_T`.  **(c)**
`‖F_n‖_{L^1} = ∫ψ(s)e^{−ns/2}ds ≤ ‖ψ‖_{L^1}e^{−na/2} → 0` and
`M(F_n) → e_+` by Theorem 9.14a(a); `N(F_n) = O(n^{−1}\log n)` by the estimate
proved in Theorem 9.14c(b) (applied with `F` in place of `G`, the two families
being mirror images under `t ↦ −t`).  ∎

**Remark 9.14c″ (what is *not* claimed).**  **[REBUILT, phase 130, round 2,
finding F2.]**  Theorem 9.14c′ is **not** a continuous extension of `I_A` to
`N_A^{dist} × N_A^{dist}`; no such extension is constructed, claimed or needed,
and P123.017 §6 says why one should not expect it (`N_A^{dist}` is a numerical
module, not a distribution algebra).  What is proved is a statement about
**limits along a named class of approximations**, and Definition 10.6c's value
of `λ_{RR}` on `σ(B_T)×σ(B_T)` is a **definition** whose *consistency* with the
dense test sector is Theorem 9.14c′ — that is the exact logical status, and it
is stated again in Theorem 10.8 and in §14.

**Remark 9.14d (why this does not contradict Theorem 9.13(a), and what
P123.007 §4 was seeing).**  **[REBUILT, phase 130.]**

1. *No contradiction.*  Theorem 9.13(a) forbids a `D_A`-eigenvector
   `Φ_∓ ∈ D_A` with `a⋆Φ_∓ = λ_∓(a)Φ_∓`; its proof compares a value `â(χ_0)`
   **on** the unitary axis with `â(∓i/2)` **off** it.  The eigenvectors
   `e_±^{dist}` of Theorem 9.14a(e) have **zero distribution component**:
   there is no `χ_0` at which to evaluate, and the eigenvalue equation holds
   in the `B_T`-component alone, where the action is by the characters
   `λ_∓` **by definition** of the module structure (9.18).  Theorem 9.14c(c)
   says precisely that these eigenvectors lie outside the dense image
   `J(N_A)`, which is what Theorem 9.13(a) is a statement about.  The two
   theorems together say: *the ruling plane is a quotient of the dense test
   sector and a subobject of its completion, and the two facts are
   compatible because the completion is strictly larger.*
2. *A.6 is unaffected.*  Clause A.6, in its amended wording,
   asks for the exact sequence of Theorem 9.12; the splitting (9.23) is a
   statement about the completion, and it is an addition, not a replacement.
   The literal "contains" reading of the old wording remains refuted for
   `N_A` itself, by Theorem 9.13(a).
3. *The scalar boundary radical is computed, and it is the analogue of
   P123.007's.*  **[REBUILT, phase 130, round 2, finding F9.]**  P123.007 §4
   (4.2) leaves an "unspecified boundary radical" `R_boundary` of the scalar
   pairing on **its own** completion, and calls eliminating it part of the A.6
   gate.  This paper's completion is a different object (Theorem 9.14), and
   `I_A` is not defined on pairs from it, so P123.007's definition cannot be
   evaluated here and no identification is claimed.  What **is** proved is the
   exact analogue, (9.24): the radical of the scalar component of `I_vec`
   against the dense test sector is **exactly the ruling plane**,
   two-dimensional, and it is separated by the **Tate** component.  That is
   the theorem behind P123.017's decision to make the pairing vector-valued:
   the vector-valuedness is forced, not a device.
4. *The Weil decomposition is recovered in the completion.*  (9.23) reads
   `N_A^{dist} = (primitive) ⊕ (ruling plane)`, with the scalar form living on
   the first summand (Theorem 9.14c(a)) and the hyperbolic form on the second.
   That is the shape of `NS = Prim ⊥ ⟨rulings⟩` — with the caveat, stated
   once and never forgotten, that no sign is asserted on the first summand
   (Theorem 14.1).

#### 9.3.7 Clause A.6: verdict, under each of the two readings

**[REBUILT, phase 129, round 4, finding F5.]**

`PROVED`, unconditionally: closedness, ideal and `†`-stability of the radical
(Thm. 9.4); **`𝓝 = 0`** (Thm. 9.8), so `N_A = D_A` is Hausdorff and the
pairing is nondegenerate on the whole test algebra; the Tate functionals
descend; the closed `†`-ideal `𝓝_W` and the canonical exact sequence with the
hyperbolic `B_T`, `†` exchanging `e_∓` (Thms. 9.11, 9.12); a nonzero primitive
sector on which `I_A` is nondegenerate (Thm. 9.12 (T3)); a separated complete
nuclear realization (Thm. 9.14); explicit topology and continuity throughout.

The clause's own words are: "its Hausdorff quotient `N_A` **contains the
ruling plane** and a nonzero completed mixed sector".  The second half is
proved.  The first half has two readings and they give opposite answers.

* **Exact-sequence reading** (P123.017 §4, which says "the rulings and
  primitive mixed classes belong to one numerical object as boundary quotient
  and kernel.  No noncanonical splitting and no formal direct sum is used",
  and §8, "this closes A.6 in the appropriate correspondence-module sense"):
  **A.6 is `PROVED`.**
* **Literal subobject reading**: **A.6 is `REFUTED` on this carrier**, by
  Theorem 9.13(a).  The obstruction is sharp and elementary: a subobject
  mapping isomorphically to `B_T` would have to contain eigenvectors `Φ_∓` of
  the whole convolution algebra with eigenvalue `λ_∓(a) = â(∓i/2)`, a value of
  `â` **off** the unitary axis, while `Φ̂_∓` lives on it.  No such eigenvector
  exists.  The abstract hyperbolic planes of Remark 9.13a do not repair this;
  they are not the ruling plane.

**Clause A.6: `PARTIAL`** — proved in the exact-sequence reading, refuted in
the literal one.  **[REBUILT, phase 130: superseded.  The specification was
amended on  to the exact-sequence wording, so **clause A.6 is
`PROVED`**; and §9.3.6a adds that in the *completion* even the literal reading
holds, the ruling plane being the canonical subobject `σ(B_T)` of
Theorem 9.14b.  The paragraphs above are kept as the record of the reading
problem and of how it was resolved.]**  This is a decision about the specification, not a gap in the
mathematics; §14.2 contains the exact amendment text I propose, **for the
author of the specification to accept or reject.**  I have not edited the
specification file.

---

## 10. Effectivity (A.7) and Riemann–Roch (A.8)

**[REBUILT, phase 129.]**

**Definition 10.1.**  A graph element is **effective** if every crossed
coefficient `b_γ` is a real nonnegative function; `Eff_A ⊆ G` is generated by
bounded complexes of effective terms under sums and nonnegative scaling.
Positivity of the Weil form is not used, as A.7 requires.

**Theorem 10.2 (P123.074, with its proof).**  On the fixed-`S` graph-kernel
sector, `Eff_A` is:

  (a) *intrinsic*: an element is effective iff all its half-density row
      measures `Π(b,a)` are positive;
  (b) closed in the rapid topology, closed under addition and nonnegative
      real scaling;
  (c) preserved by `⋆` and by `†`;
  (d) preserved by gauge changes and by the repaired coefficient chart maps;
  (e) **not** the positive-semidefinite operator cone.
  Every `E_w` is effective (coefficient `1` at the single label `(1,w)`).

*Proof.*  **[REBUILT, phase 129; the statement previously carried none.]**
(a) With `Π(b,a)=π(b)U_a=|a|_S^{−1/2}T_{b,a}` of (5.1), the row measures of
`Π(b,a)` are the measures `b_γ dμ` scaled by the positive factor
`|a|_S^{−1/2}`; nonnegativity of every `b_γ` therefore gives positive row
measures.  Conversely, on the conull subset of `X_S` with nonzero real
coordinate the atoms of the row measures at distinct labels `γ` are distinct
(the `Γ_S`-action is free there), so positivity of the total row measure
forces nonnegativity of each coefficient almost everywhere, and then
everywhere by continuity.  This is P123.074's paragraph on the cone, whose
status line is `PROVED for the fixed-S graph-kernel sectors`.
(b) Nonnegativity is a closed condition in the rapid seminorms, and is stable
under sums and nonnegative scalars pointwise.
(c) Each crossed coefficient of `Π(b,a)Π(c,d)=Π(bβ_a(c),ad)` is a convergent
sum of products of nonnegative functions, hence nonnegative; and
`Π(b,a)^*=Π(β_a^{−1}(b^*),a^{−1})` has coefficients `conj(b_γ)∘β_a^{−1}`,
nonnegative when the `b_γ` are.
(d) Gauge changes relabel the coefficients; the repaired chart maps of
P123.020 adjoin nonnegative lattice characteristic functions and constant
coefficients (P123.121 (11)).
(e) The effective permutation kernel exchanging two points is self-adjoint
with eigenvalues `1` and `−1`, so it is effective and not operator-positive;
conversely `(e−t)^†(e−t)` is operator-positive and not effective (P123.021
§1).  ∎

*Scope, stated honestly.*  P123.074's own status line adds: "This gives a
geometric adjoint and effective cone in that sector, **not their full
numerical/RR descent on the stable category**", and "No identification of that
cone with classes of arbitrary complexes or their numerical quotient is made
here."  Theorem 10.2 is therefore asserted for graph **elements** and finite
complexes of them at fixed `S`; the previous version cited P123.074 for the
descended cone, which its status forbids.

**Definition 10.2a (effectivity for smeared objects).**  **[REBUILT, phase 129,
round 2, finding F13.  Round 1 never defined effectivity on `Corr_A^{tr}`, so
Theorem 10.3 was vacuous.]**  A section `F ∈ D_A` is **effective** if
`F(u) ≥ 0` for every `u ∈ C_S`; a presented complex `(K,𝔣)` is effective if
every `F_n` is.  Write `Eff_A^{tr} ⊆ Corr_A^{tr}` for the class so defined.

**Proposition 10.2b (consistency with Definition 10.1).**  In the canonical
trivialization of Corollary 3.4, `E_F` is effective in the sense of
Definition 10.1 — all crossed coefficients real and nonnegative — if and only
if `F ≥ 0`.  Hence Definition 10.2a extends Definition 10.1 along `F ↦ E_F`,
and `Eff_A^{tr}` is closed under `⊕`, nonnegative scaling and `⋆`.

*Proof.*  Corollary 3.4 fixes a group-theoretic section `σ`, hence a
**canonical** vector `1_{E_u}` in every fibre, so `E_F = ∫F(u)E_{σ(u)}d^*u`
has coefficient exactly `F(u)` at the label `u` and no coefficient at any
nontrivial gauge label.  (Without the splitting this statement is not
available; P123.014 §5 says so explicitly: "a scalar test `h` does not define a
canonical element of the faithful Fell algebra without a splitting or the
corresponding line twist".)  By P123.074, effectivity is equivalent to
positivity of the half-density row measures, and the row measure of `E_F` at
`u` is `F(u)|u|_S^{−1/2}` times a positive Haar factor; since
`|u|_S^{−1/2}>0`, positivity of the row measures is equivalent to `F ≥ 0`.
Closure under `⊕` and nonnegative scaling is pointwise.  Closure under `⋆`:
`(F⋆G)(w) = ∫F(u)G(u^{−1}w)d^*u ≥ 0` when `F,G ≥ 0`.  ∎

*Note.*  `†` does **not** preserve `Eff_A^{tr}` as an `R`-linear condition in
the naive sense — `F^†(u)=conj(F(u^{−1}))`, so `F ≥ 0` gives `F^† ≥ 0` only
because the coefficients are real; they are, by definition, so `†` does
preserve it.  This matches P123.074's formula (5.1).

**Theorem 10.3 (descent of the cone, and what properness costs).**
**[REBUILT, phase 129, round 2: now stated for `Eff_A^{tr}`, which
Definition 10.2a makes non-vacuous.]**  The image of `Eff_A^{tr}` in `N_A` is
a convex cone, stable under `⋆` and `†`.  In `D_A` itself the cone is
**proper**: if `F` and `−F` are both effective then `F=0`.

*Proof.*  Convexity and stability descend from Proposition 10.2b because `𝓝`
is a `⋆`- and `†`-stable ideal (Theorem 9.4).  Properness in `D_A` is
pointwise: `F(u)≥0` and `−F(u)≥0` give `F(u)=0`.  ∎

**Theorem 10.3a (properness in `N_A`).**  **[REBUILT, phase 129, round 3.]**
The image of `Eff_A^{tr}` in `N_A` is a **proper** cone.

*Proof.*  By Theorem 9.8, `𝓝 = {0}`, so `N_A = D_A` and the quotient map is
the identity; properness in `D_A` is Theorem 10.3.  ∎

*History.*  Round 1 proved properness by pairing with `e_+`, which presupposed
that `λ_+` descends; round 2 withdrew that as circular and declared properness
`OPEN`, matching P123.010's own status line ("`OPEN` for properness after
numerical quotient/completion").  Theorem 9.8 removes the quotient altogether,
so the item closes — and for a reason P123.010 could not have used, namely
the vanishing of the radical.

**What remains open in A.7 — as of round 4, and its disposal in phase 130.**
**[REBUILT, phase 130: both items below are now closed; the statements of the
obstacles are kept, and the closures follow immediately after.]**


  **(O-A7-1)**  Properness after the **completion** `N_A^{dist}` of
  Theorem 9.14.  P123.017 §7 states exactly this: the source cone maps into
  the completion functorially, "However, proving that the image cone remains
  proper requires excluding `q(Eff) ∩ −q(Eff) ≠ {0}` after numerical
  identification.  Neither vector-valued separation nor source `C*`-positivity
  alone proves this, because the trace component is not a positive `C*`
  trace."  Theorem 10.3a concerns `N_A`, not `N_A^{dist}`.
  **(O-A7-2)**  Effective descent in the stronger sense of lifting local
  positive factorizations, and compatibility with the mixed RR determinant —
  P123.010's other two open items, which depend on A.8.


**Theorem 10.3b ((O-A7-1): the cone is proper in the completion).**
**[REBUILT, phase 130.  P123.017 §7 states that "neither vector-valued
separation nor source `C*`-positivity alone proves this, because the trace
component is not a positive `C*` trace".  That is correct, and it is also not
what the proof below uses: the proof uses the **Tate** component, which *is* a
pair of positive functionals on the cone, and which controls the `L^1` norm.]**
Let `\mathcal{E} := \overline{J(Eff_A^{tr})} ⊆ N_A^{dist}` be the closed cone
generated by the image of the effective cone of Definition 10.2a.  Then

    \mathcal{E} ∩ (−\mathcal{E}) = \{0\} .                          (10.3)

*Proof.*  `\mathcal{E}` is a cone: `Eff_A^{tr}` is closed under addition and
nonnegative scaling (Prop. 10.2b), `J` is linear, and the closure of a convex
cone is a convex cone.  Let `x = (ℓ,m) ∈ \mathcal{E} ∩ (−\mathcal{E})`.  Choose
nets `(F_α)`, `(G_β)` in `Eff_A^{tr}` with `J(F_α) → x` and `J(G_β) → −x` in
`D_A' ⊕ B_T` (nets, because the strong dual of a nuclear LF space need not be
metrizable).

*Step 1: the Tate component vanishes.*  The second-component projection
`M̄` is continuous, so `m = \lim_α M(F_α)` and `−m = \lim_β M(G_β)`.  For
`F ≥ 0`, `λ_∓(F) = ∫F(w)|w|_S^{∓1/2}d^*w ≥ 0`, so `M(F) ∈ R_{≥0}²`; both limits
therefore lie in the closed quadrant `R_{≥0}²`.  Hence
`m ∈ R_{≥0}² ∩ (−R_{≥0}²) = \{0\}`, i.e. `λ_−(F_α) → 0` and `λ_+(F_α) → 0`.

*Step 2: the `L^1` norm is controlled by the Tate component on the cone.*
For every `w ∈ C_S`, `|w|_S^{1/2} + |w|_S^{−1/2} ≥ 2` by AM–GM.  Hence, for
`F ≥ 0`,

    ‖F‖_{L^1(d^*w)} = ∫F\,d^*w
       ≤ ½∫F(w)(|w|_S^{1/2}+|w|_S^{−1/2})d^*w = ½(λ_+(F)+λ_−(F)).   (10.4)

By Step 1, `‖F_α‖_{L^1} → 0`, and therefore
`\sup_χ|\hat F_α(χ)| ≤ ‖F_α‖_{L^1} → 0`.

*Step 3: the distribution component vanishes.*  For every `G ∈ D_A`,
Lemma 9.14a0 gives `|I_A(F_α,G)| ≤ (\sup|\hat F_α|)N(G) → 0`.  Since
`I_A(F_α,·) → ℓ` pointwise on `D_A` (convergence in `D_A'` implies pointwise
convergence), `ℓ(G) = 0` for every `G`, i.e. `ℓ = 0`.

Hence `x = (0,0) = 0`.  ∎

**Obstacle (O-A7-1) is CLOSED**, and with it P123.010 §2's (2.2) and
P123.010 §5's item 1 (properness and closedness in the final separated
numerical completion): `\mathcal{E}` is closed by construction and proper by
(10.3).

**Theorem 10.3c ((O-A7-2): Riemann–Roch compatibility of the cone).**
**[REBUILT, phase 130.  P123.010 §5's item 2 asks for "compatibility of this
cone with the determinant of the identity/excess object completing A.8".  With
`λ_{RR}` constructed in §10.3 this becomes a statement that can be proved, and
here it is, in full.]**  Let `F, G ∈ Eff_A^{tr}`, i.e. `F,G ≥ 0` pointwise.

  (a) *Positive bidegree.*  `M(F) = (λ_−(F),λ_+(F)) ∈ R_{≥0}²`, with both
      entries `> 0` unless `F = 0`.  So the cone maps into the closed positive
      quadrant of the ruling plane, exactly as effective cycles have
      nonnegative bidegree in Weil's theory.
  (b) *Stability.*  `Eff_A^{tr}` is stable under `⊕`, nonnegative scaling, `⋆`
      and `†` (Prop. 10.2b and the Note after it), and `M` carries these to
      `⊕`, scaling, componentwise product and `♯` on the quadrant.
  (c) *The ruling component of `λ_{RR}` is contractive on the cone.*  With the
      norm convention (8A.10″),

        ‖1‖²_{λ_{per}(MF,MG)} = \exp(−β_T(MF,MG))
           = \exp( −λ_−(F)λ_+(G) − λ_+(F)λ_−(G) ) ≤ 1 ,             (10.5)

      with equality iff `F=0` or `G=0`; in particular
      `‖1‖²_{λ_{per}(MF,MF)} = e^{−2λ_−(F)λ_+(F)} ≤ 1`.
  (d) *No sign on the scalar component.*  Nothing above asserts a sign for
      `I_A(F,F)` when `F ≥ 0`; by Theorem 14.1 that number is indefinite in
      sign on `Eff_A^{tr}`, and no clause of the specification asks otherwise
      (A.7 forbids using positivity of the Weil form to define the cone, and
      A.8 supplies no Hodge-index sign).
  (e) *Descent.*  Since `𝓝 = 0` (Theorem 9.8), the cone descends to `N_A`
      unchanged (Theorem 10.3a), and to `N_A^{dist}` as the proper closed cone
      `\mathcal{E}` (Theorem 10.3b).

*Proof.*  (a) `λ_∓(F) = ∫F|w|^{∓1/2}d^*w ≥ 0` for `F ≥ 0`, and `>0` unless `F`
vanishes a.e., hence everywhere by continuity.  (b) is Proposition 10.2b and
Theorem 9.1 (`λ_∓` multiplicative, `λ_∓(F^†) = \overline{λ_±(F)}`; on the
positive quadrant, real entries, so `♯` is the flip).  (c) `β_T(MF,MG) =
λ_−(F)\overline{λ_+(G)} + λ_+(F)\overline{λ_−(G)}`, a sum of products of
nonnegative reals by (a); apply (8A.10″) and Definition 10.6a below.  (d) is
Theorem 14.1 and the clause texts.  (e) as stated.  ∎

**Remark 10.3d (two different cones, and which one clause A.7 asks for).**
**[REBUILT, phase 130.]**  P123.010 builds the `C*`-cone
`Eff_S = \overline{\{Σ_i X_i^†X_i\}}` and its §5 lists, as a remaining item,
"effective descent in the stronger sense of lifting local positive
factorizations".  That item is a property of **that** cone.  The cone of this
paper is Definition 10.1/10.2a — pointwise nonnegative coefficients — and
Theorem 10.2(e) proves that the two are **different**, neither containing the
other (the effective permutation kernel is not operator-positive; `(e−t)^†(e−t)`
is operator-positive and not effective).  Clause A.7 asks for "a functorial
cone or exact positive class constructed from the source geometry" which
"descends to the numerical theory to the extent needed by Riemann–Roch";
Definition 10.1 is such a cone — it is the operator-kernel analogue of an
effective cycle with nonnegative multiplicity, which is P123.010 §4's own
comparison principle — and Theorem 10.3c is exactly "to the extent needed by
Riemann–Roch".  **Factorization lifting in the `C*`-cone is therefore not a
requirement of clause A.7, and it is not proved here.**  It is a property of a
different object, and it is recorded as such rather than carried as an open
row-A obstacle.

**Obstacle (O-A7-2) is CLOSED**, in the form (a)–(e), with the scope statement
of Remark 10.3d attached.

**Clause A.7: `PROVED`.**  A source-defined, functorial, proper cone
(Def. 10.1/10.2a, Thms. 10.2, 10.3), proper after the numerical quotient
(Thm. 10.3a) and after the completion (Thm. 10.3b), with the Riemann–Roch
compatibility Theorem 10.3c.  Positivity of the Weil form is used nowhere in
its definition, and no sign is asserted.


### 10.2 Round 4's assessment of A.8, kept as the record

**[REBUILT, phase 130: §10.2 is the round-4 text, unchanged.  Its verdict
"`A.8` is `OPEN`" is **superseded** by §10.3 below, and its displayed
requirement "(A.8-I)" is **withdrawn** as unsatisfiable (Theorem 10.6a,
§13.8).  Theorem 10.5's list of what is proved, and Proposition 10.6, stand.]**

**Definition 10.4.**  `λ_{RR} := Det_S^{rel}` of (8A.10), i.e. `exp T` as a
based line.

**Theorem 10.5 (A.8): what is proved, and A.8 is `OPEN`.**  **[REBUILT, phase
130: the verdict "`OPEN`" is superseded by §10.3.  Parts (ii)–(vi) below stand;
**part (i) does not**: it cites (8A.13), which is superseded by (8A.13′) and
carries the undetermined constant `c_{cut}`.  Existence and convergence of the
determinant are instead Theorem 8A.7b, which is import-free.  (Round 2 repair,
finding F13 of `R130.002`.)]**
**[REBUILT, phase 129; this replaces an assertion with no proof.]**

Proved, for `λ_{RR}` on `Corr_A^{tr}`:

  (i) *existence and convergence*: (8A.13), a limit of renormalized Fredholm
      determinants of trace-class operators, nonzero (P123.012 Thm. 2.1,
      `IMPORTED + PROVED APPLICATION`);
  (ii) *additivity*: `λ_{RR}(F+G) ≅ λ_{RR}(F)⊗λ_{RR}(G)` (P123.012 (3.2));
  (iii) *the biextension*: `L_S(F,G)=λ_{RR}(F⋆G^†)` is additive,
      conjugate-additive, Hermitian, with logarithmic character
      `I_A(F,G)`, descending to a nondegenerate determinant biextension on
      the numerical quotient (P123.012 §4, Theorem 8A.7);
  (iv) *principal/gauge invariance*: P123.012 §5, **under its own hypothesis**
      "the commutators remain in the trace domain" — vacuously satisfied here
      because `D_A` is commutative, hence proved but empty (Thm. 8A.7);
  (v) *restriction*: the comparison formula (8A.12) of Theorem 8A.5 — **not**
      invariance, which (8A.12) shows is false; clause A.8 asks for
      "restriction" among its requirements, and this supplies the comparison
      and not the invariance (O-A5-2);
  (vi) *an equality formula in `T_A`*: (8A.10) together with the local
      decomposition (8.4)–(8.5), i.e. `log λ_{RR}(F) = Σ_v W_v(F) + c·F(1)`
      with `W_v` the explicit local fixed-point distributions and `c` the
      open constant of Remark 8.6b.

**Not proved, and therefore A.8 is `OPEN`.  [REBUILT, phase 129, round 4,
finding F14: the round-3 obstacle list is replaced by ONE theorem in three
numbered parts, since (O-A8-1) and (O-A8-2) are not independent — they are the
source functor and its metric.]**  Clause A.8 asks that `λ_{RR}` **extend the
intrinsic periodic ruling determinant to the admissible mixed sector**, with
principal invariance, additivity/biextension, restriction, convergence and an
equality formula in `T_A`.  The analytic half is built.  The geometric half is
not, and the notes say so in their own status lines:

  * P123.108: "It is **not** identified with the geometric biextension or
    arithmetic RR correction."
  * P123.109: "Its geometric RR identification remains open."
  * P123.012 §7, listing why "the full A.8 clause is still not green":
    "1. the determinant is not yet proved continuous on the complete rapid
    infinite-label space; 2. comparison with the pre-existing intrinsic
    periodic cohomology determinant is proved only on its hyperbolic
    Tate/ruling realization, not as an equivalence of pro-perfect cohomology
    objects; 3. effective compatibility after the final numerical completion
    remains open."

**The analytic target already exists.**  `A_MIXED_METRIC_WORK` constructs, by
an all-amplitude quadratic determinant limit from the source determinant with
its fixed bulk subtraction,

    L_tan(F) = (C·1, ‖1‖² = exp(−I(F,F))),   I(F,G) = W_q(G^*⋆F),   (10.1)
    P(F,G) = L_tan(F+G) ⊗ L_tan(F)^{−1} ⊗ L_tan(G)^{−1},
    ‖1‖_{P(F,G)} = exp(−Re I(F,G)),                                (10.2)

"a based Hermitian biextension of this additive test space, with its exact
interchange and associativity identities", respecting "every prescribed
semilocal pullback preserving `I`".  The note then states exactly what is
missing, and it is the same thing this paper is missing: (10.1)–(10.2)
"construct the analytic determinant/biextension target for the comparison,
**not its missing geometric source functor** or the full A.8 assertion".

**Theorem (A.8), in the form that would close the clause.**  A.8 is closed
for `Corr_A^{tr}` if and only if the following three statements are proved.

  **(A.8-I)  The source functor.**  A monoidal functor
      `Φ_det : Corr_A^{tr} → (periodic Deligne ruling category, λ_{per})`
      carrying `⋆` to its tensor, `†` to its duality, `Δ` to its unit,
      `Tr_A = Det_S^{rel}` to `λ_{per}`, and the Tate boundary map `M` of
      Theorem 9.12 to the two ruling directions.  This is the arrow P123.007
      §8 draws and calls open, and it is P123.012 §7 item 2.
  **(A.8-II)  The metric comparison.**  An isometry of `Φ_det`'s determinant
      metric with `L_tan` of (10.1)–(10.2), with the correct normalization —
      `A_MIXED_METRIC_WORK` flags "including the distinction between a norm
      and its square" — under which the proved endpoint cocycle
      `A(F,G)A(F⋄G,K)=A(G,K)A(F,G⋄K)` (P123.108 (5), P123.110 (7)) becomes
      the biextension cocycle of `Φ_det`.
  **(A.8-III)  Continuity.**  = (O-A5-1), in its two parts (O-A5-1a) and
      (O-A5-1b); this is P123.012 §7 item 1.

**A final attempt at (A.8-I), and why it failed.**  **[REBUILT, phase 129,
round 4.]**  I re-read P123.007 §8, P123.009, P123.012 §§4–7 and
`A_MIXED_METRIC_WORK` §§6–7 looking for a construction.  The natural candidate
is to build `Φ_det` from the **derived diagonal contact**: P123.009 is
`PROVED-IN-REPOSITORY` "on the algebraic Bruhat–Schwartz kernel core for every
nonidentity rational dilation", and supplies, at one place `v` and one
`q ≠ 1`, a perfect diagonal-contact complex, its based determinant line
`H_{v,q}`, the half-density correction, a local Lefschetz number, dagger
symmetry and cyclicity.  One would set `Φ_det(E_F) := ` the determinant of the
contact complex of `E_F`, assembled over places and integrated over `supp F`.
**This cannot work, and the reason is a theorem of this paper.**

**Proposition 10.6 (the contact determinant is blind on the graph sector).**
Any functor `Φ` defined on `G` by taking the determinant line of the derived
diagonal contact of its objects is **constant** on the finite-place data of
the graph sector, and therefore cannot carry `Tr_A`.

*Proof.*  By Theorem 7.1(c) and Remark 7.3, at a finite place the contact
complex of a twist `E_w` with `v_p(w_p) ≠ 0` is the single rank-one class
`C[0]` of the fixed point `x_p=0`, **with no differential**, independent of
`w_p`; a rank-one complex concentrated in degree zero has determinant line
canonically `C`, with no torsion.  For a unit twist it is
`C[0] ⊕ ⊕̂_j C^{N_k}[0]`, again concentrated in degree zero, of infinite rank,
so its determinant line is not even defined.  Corollary 7.5 is exactly this
statement, and §13.3 records that the boundary determinant target built on
this idea "was proved trivial on every graph class: no isomorphism-class
invariant of bundles separates a graph from the diagonal".  Meanwhile
`Tr_A` separates classes (Theorem 9.5).  ∎

So `(A.8-I)`, if it exists, **cannot be of the "determinant of the contact
complex" type.**  The arithmetic content of `Tr_A` lives, as §1.3 says and
Remark 7.3 proves, in the **half-density normalization of the point class**,
not in any complex-theoretic invariant of it — the number `τ_p(σ)` is the
torsion of the *smooth model* `[R[z] →^{(σ−1)z} R[z]]` (P123.060 (1.1),
§4), which the totally disconnected coefficient algebra does not carry.  Any
`Φ_det` must therefore transport data that is invisible to K-theory of the
contact complex, and I have no construction of such a functor and no candidate
for one.  P123.009 §7 says the same from its side: the full A.8 gate is open
"for exactly two reasons — `q=1`: nonproper fixed locus → generic-orbit
renormalization; infinite `F`: determinant tensor product →
summability/completion", and P123.009 §5 warns that "a trace on the quotient
by `S`-units is a **sum** of local-stratum Lefschetz numbers, not the
fixed-point determinant of the Cartesian product of all transverse fields".

**Exact missing statement.**  (A.8-I) as displayed above, together with
(A.8-II).  I found no route, and Proposition 10.6 closes off the one route the
corpus suggests.  **A.8 is the binding constraint on row A**, and I report no
shortcut.

`λ_{RR}` therefore exists, is a biextension of the analytic pairing, and has an
equality formula in `T_A`; it is **not** shown to be a Riemann–Roch
determinant.  No Hodge sign is asserted, as the clause requires.

**[REBUILT, phase 130: the last sentence of the preceding paragraph is
superseded.  §10.3 shows `λ_{RR}` **is** a Riemann–Roch determinant, once its
target is the one the mathematics forces; the obstruction round 4 met was a
consequence of insisting on a scalar target, and Theorem 10.8 is the theorem
that says so.]**

### 10.3 Clause A.8, proved: the vector-valued Riemann–Roch biextension

**[REBUILT, phase 130.  §10.2 above is kept, unaltered, as the round-4 record:
its Theorem 10.5 lists what was proved, its displayed "(A.8-I)" states what was
then thought to be needed, and Proposition 10.6 refutes the one construction
the corpus suggested for it.  This subsection does three things.  (1) It
**withdraws the formulation** "(A.8-I): a monoidal functor carrying `Tr_A` to
`λ_{per}`" — Theorem 10.6a proves that requirement unsatisfiable, so it was
never the content of clause A.8, and §13.8 records the withdrawal.  (2) It
proves clause A.8 as the clause is actually written, with a determinant target
`T_A = Pic_b(B_T)` (Definition 8A.2′) that the mathematics forces.  (3) It
proves, beside it, the negative theorem that makes the vector-valuedness
necessary.]**

#### 10.3.1 The formulation of round 4 is withdrawn

**Theorem 10.6a (no functor through the Tate fibre can carry `Tr_A`).**  Let
`Φ` be any functor on `Corr_A^{tr}` whose value on a presented complex `K`
depends only on `M(s(K)) ∈ B_T`.  Then `Φ` does not determine `Tr_A`; that is,
there exist `K, K' ∈ Corr_A^{tr}` with `M(s(K)) = M(s(K'))` and
`Tr_A(K) ≠ Tr_A(K')`.

*Proof.*  Let `F, F'` be the two sections of Theorem 9.5 and put `K, K'` for
the one-term presented complexes with presentations `F, F'`.  By the proof of
Theorem 9.12 (T3)(i), `λ_−(F) = λ_−(F')` and `λ_+(F) = λ_+(F')`, so
`M(s(K)) = M(s(K'))`.  By Theorem 9.5, `T(F) − T(F') = ν(\log p)(p^j − p^{j'})
 ≠ 0`, so `Tr_A(K) = (C,e^{T(F)}) ≠ (C,e^{T(F')}) = Tr_A(K')` in `Pic_b(C)`.  ∎

*Consequence, stated with its hypothesis.*  **[REBUILT, phase 130, round 2,
finding F5 of `R130.002`.  Round 1 wrote "there is none, for any construction
whatsoever"; that is a non sequitur, because round 4's "(A.8-I)" does not
stipulate that `Φ_{det}` factors through `M(s(K))`, and a functor into a
Picard category of **metrized** lines can in principle carry more than the
bidegree.  The hypothesis is now stated.]**  Theorem 10.6a kills exactly the
functors that **factor through the Tate fibre**, i.e. those whose value on `K`
depends only on `M(s(K))`.  The round-4 display "(A.8-I) … carrying
`Tr_A = Det_S^{rel}` to `λ_{per}`" asks for a functor `Φ_{det}` into the ruling
category `(B_T,λ_{per})`, whose objects and morphisms are determined by
`B_T`-data; read as a functor through the Tate fibre — the only reading under
which "carries `M` to the two ruling directions" has a meaning — it is
unsatisfiable by Theorem 10.6a.  **Read otherwise, it is not addressed here,
and I do not claim it is impossible.**  Under either reading it is not what
clause A.8 asks, and it is withdrawn as a *formulation*:  The same is
true of Weil's theory, which is what the requirement was modelled on: the trace
`\mathrm{Tr}(D) = D·Δ` of a correspondence is not a function of its bidegree
`(d_1,d_2)`, and no functor to the ruling plane carries it.  What clause A.8
asks is that the Riemann–Roch determinant on the mixed sector **extend** the
intrinsic ruling determinant on the ruling plane — and with the ruling plane a
canonical quotient of `N_A` (Theorem 9.12, clause A.6 as amended) and a
canonical subobject of `N_A^{dist}` (Theorem 9.14a,b), that requirement has an
exact meaning, which §10.3.3 proves.

#### 10.3.2 The two determinants

**Definition 10.6a (the intrinsic periodic ruling determinant).**  On
`B_T = C_−⊕C_+` with the hyperbolic form `β_T` of P123.008 (2.2) put

    λ_{per}(b,b') := ( C , s = \exp β_T(b,b') ) ∈ Pic_b(C),
    so  ‖1‖²_{λ_{per}(b,b')} = \exp(−\mathrm{Re}\,β_T(b,b'))         (10.6)

in the convention (8A.10″).  For real `b = (d_1,d_2)` this is
`‖1‖² = \exp(−2d_1d_2)`, i.e. `‖1‖ = \exp(−d_1d_2)`: **the fixed ruling line of
Paper 42** (`eq:intrinsicRRform`; `A_MIXED_METRIC_WORK`, "the paper's fixed
ruling line has norm `exp(-d_1(x)d_2(x))`; its polarized form is
`d_1(x)d_2(y)+d_2(x)d_1(y)`"), whose polarized form is `\mathrm{Re}\,β_T`.  The
word *periodic* is justified by Theorem 9.7b(d)–(e): the Tate functionals
enter the local traces as residues, at `∞` as `λ_+` itself and at `p` as its
`2π/\log p`-periodization (9.7b.5).  `λ_{per}` is a based Hermitian biextension
of `B_T`: biadditivity of `β_T` gives canonical isometries
`λ_{per}(b+b'',b') ≅ λ_{per}(b,b')⊗λ_{per}(b'',b')` (both sides based, exponents
add), and the same in the second slot; the two orders agree because both send
`1 ↦ 1⊗1`; Hermitian symmetry is `β_T(b',b) = \overline{β_T(b,b')}`; and
`λ_{per}(b^♯,b'^♯) = \overline{λ_{per}(b,b')}` because `β_T(b^♯,b'^♯) =
\overline{β_T(b,b')}` (direct computation from P123.008 (2.1)–(2.2)).

**Definition 10.6b (the analytic determinant biextension).**  For
`F,G ∈ 𝓐_tr` put

    L_S(F,G) := Det_S^{rel}(F⋆G^†) = ( C , s = e^{I_A(F,G)} ) ∈ Pic_b(C),
    so  ‖1‖²_{L_S(F,G)} = \exp(−\mathrm{Re}\,I_A(F,G)) ,             (10.7)

with `I_A(F,G) = T(F⋆G^†)` and `c = 0`.

**Definition 10.6c (the vector-valued Riemann–Roch biextension).**  For
`x = (ℓ,m) ∈ N_A^{dist}` and `G ∈ D_A` put

    λ_{RR}(x,G) := \big( (C, e^{ℓ(G)}) ,\; λ_{per}(m, M G) \big) ∈ T_A ,
                                                                    (10.8)

and for `F,G ∈ 𝓐_tr`, `λ_{RR}(F,G) := (L_S(F,G), λ_{per}(MF,MG))`; on the
ruling plane, `λ_{RR}(σ(b),σ(b')) := (\text{unit}, λ_{per}(b,b'))`.  For
`F ∈ D_A`, `λ_{RR}(J(F),G) = λ_{RR}(F,G)`, the two definitions agreeing because
`J(F) = (I_A(F,·), MF)`.  Its logarithm is P123.017's vector-valued numerical
pairing:

    \log λ_{RR}(F,G) = ( I_A(F,G),\, β_T(MF,MG) ) = I_{vec}(F,G).    (10.9)

#### 10.3.3 Theorem 10.7 — clause A.8

**[phase 133: the review panel (PANEL_3 §3) sides with the dissent; the author's
decision on an amendment is decided: amendment accepted.]**  **[phase 133,
amendment accepted : the repository author (D. Trejo Pizzo) accepted
on  the amendment of A.8 proposed in PANEL_SYNTHESIS §4.2 — "`λ_{RR}`
is a based Hermitian biextension into `Pic_b(B_T)` whose Tate component is the
ruling determinant (Def. 10.6c), consistent with the test sector by Thms 9.14a,
9.14c′; the scalar determinant `L_S` is trivial on the rulings (Thm 10.8), so
the extension of the *scalar* determinant demanded by the original clause does
not exist."  New grading: `REFUTED AS STATED (scalar extension) + PROVED AS
AMENDED`.  The the earlier ruling of `R130.005` is superseded by the author's
decision; the auditor's dissent below is kept verbatim as history; the row-A
label stands unconditionally.  No theorem and no proof of this paper changes.]**

**Theorem 10.7 (A.8).**  With the target `T_A = Pic_b(B_T)` of
Definition 8A.2′ and the metric convention (8A.10″):

  (a) *Biextension laws.*  `λ_{RR}` is a based Hermitian biextension: additive
      in the first slot, conjugate-additive in the second, Hermitian under
      exchange, with the interchange and associativity identities; and
      `†`-compatible, `λ_{RR}(F^†,G^†) = \overline{λ_{RR}(F,G)}` with `♯` acting
      on the second component.
  (b) *Convergence and continuity.*  **[REBUILT, phase 130, round 2,
      finding F1.]**  **Both** components of `λ_{RR}` are defined and
      continuous on `𝓐_tr × 𝓐_tr` for `‖·‖_{tr}`: the scalar component by
      `|I_A(F,G)| ≤ ‖F⋆G^†‖_{tr} ≤ ‖F‖_{tr}‖G‖_{tr}` and the Tate component by
      Theorem 8A.7b(a′), `|λ_∓(F)| ≤ ‖F‖_{tr}`.  On `N_A^{dist} × D_A` it is
      separately continuous for the topologies of Theorem 9.14.  On the ruling
      plane its value is the **definition** of Definition 10.6c, and that
      definition is consistent with the dense test sector along every
      admissible approximation, by Theorem 9.14c′ — see Remark 9.14c″ and
      Theorem 10.8; no continuous extension of `I_A` to
      `N_A^{dist}×N_A^{dist}` is claimed.  Its determinant germ
      `z ↦ Δ_{F}(z)` is holomorphic on `|z| < 1/‖F‖_{L^1}` and exactly
      multiplicative (Theorem 8A.7b).
  (c) *Principal invariance.*  `λ_{RR}(a⋆F⋆a^{−1},G) ≅ λ_{RR}(F,G)` for every
      invertible multiplier `a` whose commutators remain in the trace domain
      (P123.012 §5).  On `D_A` the hypothesis is vacuous, because `D_A` is
      commutative, so the statement is proved and empty; it must not be
      transported to a noncommutative enlargement without re-checking.
  (d) *Restriction.*  Along the chart maps `J_{ST}`: the second component is
      **strictly invariant**, `λ_{per}(M J_{ST}F, M J_{ST}G) = λ_{per}(MF,MG)`,
      by Theorem 8A.7c(d); the first component transforms by the exact
      comparison (8A.12); and on the colimit `D_A^∞` with `T_∞`, both
      components are chart-invariant, `λ_{RR}^∞ ∘ (J_{ST}×J_{ST}) = λ_{RR}^∞`.
  (e) *Extension of the intrinsic ruling determinant, and the equality
      formula.*  In the canonical splitting
      `N_A^{dist} = N_A^{dist,prim} ⊕ σ(B_T)` of Theorem 9.14b:

        λ_{RR}|_{σ(B_T)×σ(B_T)} = (\text{unit},\, λ_{per}) ,
        λ_{RR}|_{N_A^{dist,prim}×D_A^{prim}} = (L_S,\, \text{unit}) ,

      and for every `F ∈ D_A` and `G ∈ D_A`, writing `J(F) = (I_A(F,·),0) +
      σ(MF)`,

        λ_{RR}(F,G) = λ_{RR}\big((I_A(F,·),0),\,G\big) ⊗ λ_{per}(MF,\,MG)
                                                                   (10.10)

      in `T_A`; equivalently, in logarithms,
      `I_{vec}(F,G) = (I_A(F,G), 0) + (0, β_T(MF,MG))`.  So the Riemann–Roch
      determinant **restricts on the ruling plane to the intrinsic periodic
      ruling determinant `λ_{per}`**, and the rulings enter a mixed
      intersection only through their Deligne pairing — Weil's
      `D·D' = P·P' + d_1d_2' + d_2d_1'`.  **[REBUILT, phase 130, round 2,
      finding F3 of `R130.002` and F3 of `R130.003`: the second slot of this
      display is **definitional** — `λ_{RR}`'s second component is
      `λ_{per}∘(M×M)` by Definition 10.6c, and (10.10) in logarithms is the
      decomposition of an ordered pair.  Its mathematical content is entirely
      Theorems 9.14a, 9.14b, 9.14c′ and 10.8; the paragraph "What A.8's
      closure actually consists of", below, says this in those words, and the
      clause verdict is stated accordingly.]**
  (f) *Metric comparison (the old (A.8-II)).*  Fix `q ∈ S_f`.  The local factor
      of `L_S` at `q`, `L_S^{(q)}(F,G) := (C, e^{W_q(F⋆G^†)})`, is **equal** to
      `L_{tan}` of `A_MIXED_METRIC_WORK` (6)–(7), including the norm-versus-
      squared-norm factor, under the convention (8A.10″) and the normalization
      dictionary of §8.2; the polarized line `P(F,G)` of (10.2) is the
      cross-effect of `L_S^{(q)}`, with `‖1‖ = \exp(−\mathrm{Re}\,W_q(F⋆G^†))`;
      and the all-amplitude limit holds at the symbol level: for
      `H := F⋆F^†` and `H_N := H/N`,

        N·Σ_{r≥1}(−1)^{r+1}T(H_N^{⋆r})/r = T(F⋆F^†) + O(1/N) = I_A(F,F)+O(1/N),
                                                                   (10.11)

      with the `O(1/N)` explicit:
      `≤ ‖H‖_{L^1}‖H‖_{tr}/(2N(1−‖H‖_{L^1}/N))`.
  (g) *No sign.*  Nothing in (a)–(f) asserts a Hodge-index sign.  The scalar
      component `I_A` is indefinite with infinite index on both sides
      (Theorem 14.1), and (10.10) is an identity of based lines, not an
      inequality.

*Proof.*  **(a)**  *Additivity.*  `I_A` is additive in `F` and
conjugate-additive in `G` (Theorem 8A.7, from `T` linear and
`(F⋆G^†)` bi-additive-conjugate-additive), and `β_T(M·,M·)` is likewise,
`M` being linear.  Based lines with exponents adding give canonical
isometries `λ_{RR}(F+F',G) ≅ λ_{RR}(F,G)⊗λ_{RR}(F',G)` in each component
(both sides are `(C,1)` with the distinguished generator matching), and the
same in the second slot.  *Interchange and associativity.*  The two composites
`λ_{RR}(F+F',G+G') ≅ ⊗` of the four pieces, built in the two possible orders,
both send the distinguished generator `1` to `1⊗1⊗1⊗1` and have equal
exponents, since the exponent is bi-additive; so they agree.  This is the
argument of `A_MIXED_METRIC_WORK` (7) and of P123.012 §4, carried out on both
components.  *Hermitian.*  `I_A(G,F) = T(G⋆F^†) = \overline{T(F⋆G^†)}` by
Theorem 8A.4's `†`-reality and commutativity, and
`β_T(m',m) = \overline{β_T(m,m')}`.  *`†`-compatibility.*  **[REBUILT, phase 130, round 2,
finding F17: the round-1 chain mislabelled its middle terms; the conclusion was
right and here is the correct one-liner.]**  `(F^†)^∧ = \overline{F̂}` and
`T̂+c` is real (Theorem 9.7(c)), so
`I_A(F^†,G^†) = \overline{I_A(F,G)}`; and `β_T(MF^†,MG^†) = β_T((MF)^♯,(MG)^♯) =
\overline{β_T(MF,MG)}` by Definition 10.6a.

**(b)**  **[REBUILT, phase 130, round 2.]**  First component:
`|I_A(F,G)| = |T(F⋆G^†)| ≤ N(F⋆G^†) ≤ ‖F⋆G^†‖_{tr} ≤ ‖F‖_{tr}‖G‖_{tr}` by
Theorem 8A.7b(a),(b), and `\exp` is continuous.  Second component: by
Theorem 8A.7b(a′), `λ_∓` extend to `𝓐_tr` with `|λ_∓(H)| ≤ ‖H‖_{tr}`, so
`M : 𝓐_tr → B_T` is continuous and `β_T` is a continuous bilinear form on
`B_T`; hence `(F,G) ↦ λ_{per}(MF,MG)` is continuous on `𝓐_tr × 𝓐_tr`.  On
`N_A^{dist}×D_A` the pairing `((ℓ,m),G) ↦ (ℓ(G),β_T(m,MG))` is separately
continuous by Theorem 9.14.  On the ruling plane the value is Definition
10.6c's, and its consistency with the test sector along every admissible
approximation is Theorem 9.14c′; Remark 9.14c″ states what is **not** claimed.  Holomorphy and multiplicativity of the germ
are Theorem 8A.7b(d)–(e).

**(c)**  `T(a⋆F⋆a^{−1}) = T(F)` by cyclicity (Theorem 8A.4), under the stated
hypothesis; on `D_A` conjugation is trivial.  The second component is
unchanged because `M(a⋆F⋆a^{−1}) = M(a)M(F)M(a)^{−1} = M(F)` in the
commutative `B_T`.  The honesty note is Theorem 8A.7's.

**(d)**  Second component: Theorem 8A.7c(d).  First component: (8A.12) applied
to `H = F⋆G^†`, noting `J_{ST}(F⋆G^†) = (J_{ST}F)⋆(J_{ST}G)^†` since `J_{ST}`
is a `†`-algebra map.  Colimit: Theorem 8A.7c(c).

**(e)**  By Theorem 9.14b, `J(F) = (I_A(F,·),0) + σ(MF)`, a decomposition
inside `N_A^{dist}`.  Evaluate (10.8) on each summand:
`λ_{RR}((I_A(F,·),0),G) = ((C,e^{I_A(F,G)}), λ_{per}(0,MG)) = (L_S(F,G),
\text{unit})`, since `β_T(0,·) = 0`; and
`λ_{RR}(σ(MF),G) = ((C,e^{0}), λ_{per}(MF,MG)) = (\text{unit},
λ_{per}(MF,MG))`, since the first component of `σ(m)` is `0`.  The tensor
product of these two elements of `T_A = Pic_b(C)²` multiplies the
distinguished generators componentwise, giving
`(L_S(F,G), λ_{per}(MF,MG)) = λ_{RR}(F,G)`, which is (10.10).  The two
restrictions in the display are the same two computations, with the second
read on `σ(B_T)×σ(B_T)` (where `λ_{RR}` is defined by Definition 10.6c and the
first component is the unit) and on `N_A^{dist,prim}×D_A^{prim}` (where
`MG = 0`, so the second component is the unit).  Taking logarithms gives the
additive form.

**(f)**  `A_MIXED_METRIC_WORK` (6) defines
`L_{tan}(F) = (C·1, ‖1‖² = \exp(−I(F,F)))` with `I(F,G) = W_q(G^*⋆F)`, and
fixes `‖1‖ = \exp(−I(F,F)/2)`.  Our `L_S^{(q)}(F,G) = (C, e^{W_q(F⋆G^†)})` has,
by (8A.10″), `‖1‖² = \exp(−\mathrm{Re}\,W_q(F⋆G^†))`.  The two pairings agree:
`D_A` is commutative, so `F⋆G^† = G^†⋆F`, and the normalization dictionary of
§8.2 identifies the unnormalized involution `*` of P123.007 (1.2) with the
half-density `†` of (8.2) under `F ↦ |·|_S^{1/2}F`.  Hence
`L_S^{(q)} = L_{tan}` with the same norm convention, and the polarized line
(10.2) is the cross-effect of `L_S^{(q)}` by the computation in (a).  For
(10.11): by (8A.17), `|T(H_N^{⋆r})| ≤ ‖H_N‖_{L^1}^{r−1}‖H_N‖_{tr}` with
`‖H_N‖_{L^1} = ‖H‖_{L^1}/N` and `‖H_N‖_{tr} = ‖H‖_{tr}/N`.  The `r=1` term is
`T(H_N) = T(H)/N`, so `N·T(H_N) = T(H) = I_A(F,F)` exactly; and

    N·|Σ_{r≥2}(−1)^{r+1}T(H_N^{⋆r})/r|
      ≤ N Σ_{r≥2} (‖H‖_{L^1}/N)^{r−1}(‖H‖_{tr}/N)/r
      ≤ ‖H‖_{tr}‖H‖_{L^1}/(2N(1−‖H‖_{L^1}/N)) = O(1/N),

valid for `N > ‖H‖_{L^1}`.  *Honesty note.*  `L_S` itself is **not** equal to
`L_{tan}`: `S` always contains `∞`, so `L_S = L_S^{(∞)} ⊗ ⊗_{p∈S_f}L_S^{(p)}`
carries an archimedean factor that `A_MIXED_METRIC_WORK`'s one-place
construction does not.  What is proved is the equality of the local factors,
which is what the comparison needs.  For general `S`, `L_S` is the
`\exp`-type relative determinant of P123.012 Theorem 2.1, whose Banach
extension is Theorem 8A.7b; the operator-cutoff determinant and its possible
anomaly (P123.107 §5) are a different object and are not used, see
Remark 8A.7b1.

**(g)**  By inspection of (a)–(f); and Theorem 14.1 for the indefiniteness.  ∎

**What A.8's closure actually consists of.**  **[REBUILT, phase 130, round 2,
findings F3 of `R130.002` and F3 of `R130.003`.  Said plainly, once, so that
no reader takes Theorem 10.7(e) for more than it is.]**  `λ_{RR}` is
**defined** as the pair `(L_S, λ_{per}∘(M×M))` (Definition 10.6c).  Part (e)
of Theorem 10.7 is therefore, in its second slot, the observation that the
second component of a pair is its second component; and (10.10), in
logarithms, is bi-additivity.  The mathematical content of "clause A.8 is
closed" is exactly these five statements, and nothing else:

  1. **Theorem 9.14a** — the two rulings are objects of the theory: they are
     limits, in `N_A^{dist}`, of explicit test classes (spread Tate
     characters), with `D_A` acting on them by the characters `λ_±`.
  2. **Theorem 9.14b** — the completion splits canonically as a `D_A`-module,
     `N_A^{dist} = N_A^{dist,prim} ⊕ σ(B_T)`, so the ruling plane is a
     **subobject** and not only a quotient.
  3. **Theorem 10.8** — the analytic determinant `L_S` is **trivial** on that
     subobject: along every admissible approximation of the rulings the scalar
     pairing tends to `0` while `β_T` tends to `1` (Theorem 9.14c′).  This is
     a theorem, and it is the one that forces the target.
  4. **Definition 10.6c is consistent** with the dense test sector, by 3; it
     is a definition, and Remark 9.14c″ records that no continuous extension
     of `I_A` to `N_A^{dist}×N_A^{dist}` is claimed.
  5. **The laws** (a)–(d) and (f) of Theorem 10.7: the biextension identities,
     the convergence on `𝓐_tr` (which needed the corrected norm, Definition
     8A.7b0), principal invariance, restriction, and the local metric
     comparison with `A_MIXED_METRIC_WORK`.

**Clause A.8: `PROVED WITH TARGET `Pic_b(B_T)`.**  **[REBUILT, phase 130,
round 2.]**  Clause A.8 asks: "`λ_{RR}` extends the intrinsic periodic ruling
determinant to the admissible mixed sector.  Principal invariance,
additivity/biextension, restriction, convergence and an equality formula in
`T_A` are proved.  This clause supplies no Hodge-index sign."  Principal
invariance: 10.7(c).  Additivity/biextension: 10.7(a).  Restriction: 10.7(d).
Convergence: 10.7(b), on the Banach `†`-algebra `𝓐_tr` with the corrected norm
(8A.15), where **both** components are now defined and continuous.  Equality
formula in `T_A`: (10.10).  No sign: 10.7(g).

*Extension — my honest judgement, stated as a judgement.*  What is proved is:
the analytic determinant extends to a completion in which the ruling plane is
a canonical subobject; on that subobject its **scalar** factor is trivial
(Theorem 10.8); and the intrinsic ruling determinant is therefore carried by
the **Tate** component of the vector-valued determinant, where it sits
consistently with every admissible approximation by test classes.  I judge the
clause's words "`λ_{RR}` extends the intrinsic periodic ruling determinant"
**satisfied by `λ_{RR}^{vec}` with the target `Pic_b(B_T)`, and not satisfied
by any scalar determinant** — the latter is Theorem 10.8, a theorem, not a
preference.  Accordingly the verdict is recorded as `PROVED WITH TARGET
Pic_b(B_T)` and **not** as an unqualified `PROVED`.

*The auditor's dissent, recorded verbatim* (`R130.002` §4 and §3):

> "A.8 | **NOT EARNED as written; EARNED-WITH-REPAIRS available** | F1 (the
> vector determinant is not defined on the declared completion — convergence,
> one of the clause's five named requirements, fails for the Tate component),
> F2 (the extension to the ruling plane is a fiat, not a proved continuous
> extension), F3 (part (e) and (10.10) are definitional), F5 (the withdrawal
> of (A.8-I) is argued under a hypothesis it does not state)."

> "**A.8: FAIL as currently written** — not because the underlying mathematics
> is a renaming (9.14a–c are not), but because the clause is discharged by a
> definition (Def. 10.6c) plus bi-additivity, so the only thing A.8 adds
> beyond A.6 is Thms 9.14a–c, which are already what closes A.6.  A.8 restated
> honestly — 'the ruling determinant is the Tate component of `λ_{RR}`, and
> the consistency of that definition with the test sector is Thms 9.14a,c' —
> would be a true and modest theorem, and would *then* pass the test as a
> non-renaming, because 9.14a–c are not restatements of the explicit formula."

*My response, for the record.*  F1 and F5 are repaired (Definition 8A.7b0,
Theorem 8A.7b(a′); Theorem 10.6a's hypothesis is now stated).  F2 is repaired
in the second of the two ways the auditor offers: Definition 9.14c0 names the
class, Theorem 9.14c′ proves the limit statement for the whole class, and
Remark 9.14c″ says the value on `σ(B_T)` is a definition.  F3 I **accept in
full**: the restatement the auditor asks for is the paragraph "What A.8's
