closure actually consists of" above, and the verdict now carries the
qualification `WITH TARGET Pic_b(B_T)`.  Whether that qualified verdict counts
as closing the clause is a judgement; mine is recorded above, the auditor's is
recorded verbatim, and §14 carries both.

**Author's decision.**  **[phase 133, amendment accepted
2026-09-12.]**  The judgement was referred to the repository author as an
amendment, exactly as A.6 had been.  **D. Trejo Pizzo accepted the amendment on
2026-09-12.**  Clause A.8 is therefore graded `REFUTED AS STATED (scalar
extension) + PROVED AS AMENDED (author)`: the *scalar* extension the
clause demanded does not exist (Theorem 10.8), and the clause as amended — the
vector-valued biextension into `Pic_b(B_T)` with the ruling determinant as its
Tate component, consistent with the test sector by Theorems 9.14a, 9.14c′ — is
proved (Theorem 10.7).  Everything above this paragraph, including the
auditor's dissent and my response, stands as the record of how the decision was
reached.

#### 10.3.4 The negative theorem beside it

**Theorem 10.8 (the scalar determinant is trivial on the rulings, at every
finite chart).**  **[REBUILT, phase 130, round 2, finding F2 of `R130.002`:
round 1 spoke of "the continuous extension of `L_S` to `σ(B_T)`", which is not
a defined object.  The statement is now made along the named class of
approximations of Definition 9.14c0, where it is a theorem.]**  Fix a finite
chart `S`.  Let `(F_α)` be any admissible approximation of `e_+` and `(G_β)`
any admissible, `N`-bounded approximation of `e_−` (Definition 9.14c0).  Then

    L_S(F_α,G_β) = (C, e^{I_A(F_α,G_β)}) → (C,1) = the unit of Pic_b(C),
    λ_{per}(M F_α, M G_β) → λ_{per}(e_+,e_−) = (C,e),  ‖1‖² = e^{−1} ≠ 1,
                                                                    (10.12)

the first limit uniform in the other index (Theorem 9.14c′(b)).  Consequently
the value assigned to the ruling plane by Definition 10.6c — `(unit, λ_{per})`
— is the **only** value consistent with the test sector, and **no**
scalar-valued determinant built from the symbol `T̂_S` can extend the intrinsic
ruling determinant.  The vector-valued target of Definition 8A.2′ is forced.

*Proof.*  Theorem 9.14c′(b) gives `I_A(F_α,G_β) → 0` uniformly and
`β_T(MF_α,MG_β) → β_T(e_+,e_−) = 1`; apply `\exp` and Definition 10.6a.  The
spread Tate characters of (9.21) are such a pair, by Theorem 9.14c′(c), so the
class of admissible pairs is nonempty.  ∎

**Remark 10.8a (why the scalar line is blind, structurally).**  **[REBUILT,
phase 130.]**  Three independent reasons, each proved in this paper.

1. *The Tate degrees are off the unitary axis.*  `λ_∓(F) = \hat F(∓i/2)` is a
   value of the analytic continuation of `\hat F`, and by Theorem 9.13(a) no
   continuous functional built from values of `\hat F` **on** the axis can
   reproduce it.  `I_A(F,G) = ∫\hat F\overline{\hat G}(T̂+c)dχ` is built
   entirely from values on the axis.
2. *The symbol overcounts the Tate poles.*  By Theorem 9.7b(c) the residue of
   `T̂_S` at `±i/2` is `∓i(|S_f|+1)`: each place of `S` carries its own copy.
   There is therefore no normalization of `I_A^S` under which the pair
   `(λ_−,λ_+)` appears with the hyperbolic coefficient `1` — **[REBUILT, phase
   130, round 2, finding F12: read "no *chart-independent* normalization";
   rescaling `T̂ ↦ T̂/(|S_f|+1)` is a normalization, but it moves with `S`]** —
   the coefficient is chart-dependent, which a canonical ruling form cannot be.
3. *The rulings are the scalar radical.*  Theorem 9.14c(a) proves that the
   radical of the scalar component of `I_vec` against the dense test sector is
   exactly the ruling plane — the analogue here of P123.007 §4's boundary
   radical, not an identification with it (Remark 9.14d(3)).  A scalar form is
   by definition zero on its own radical.  **[REBUILT, phase 130, round 2,
   finding F9.]**

Together these say that the vector-valuedness of P123.017's pairing is a
**theorem**, not a device, and that Theorem 10.7's target is the only
available one.  It also explains, retrospectively, why round 4 could find no
`Φ_{det}`: it was looking for a scalar-to-ruling transport that Theorem 10.8
forbids.

**Theorem 10.8b (`CLASSICAL` — Mertens.  In the colimit chart the scalar form
IS hyperbolic on the rulings; Theorem 10.8 is a finite-chart artifact).**
**[REBUILT, phase 130, round 2, finding F4 of `R130.003`.  LABEL: `CLASSICAL`.
This theorem uses Mertens' theorem, an elementary classical statement about
`Λ(N)`.  It therefore **counts toward no clause of A** (clause A.10), it is
used nowhere in §§2–10 or §14's ledger, and it is stated only as what row A
hands to row C.  Every clause of A is proved at a fixed finite chart, where
this theorem does not apply.]**

Work in the colimit chart `D_A^∞` of Theorem 8A.7c, with `T_∞`,
`I_∞(F,G) := T_∞(F⋆G^†)`, and `c = 0`.  Let `ψ ∈ C_c^∞((0,∞))` be real,
`ψ ≥ 0`, `∫ψ = 1`, `supp ψ ⊆ [a,b]`, and let `F_n, G_m` be the spread Tate
characters (9.21).  Then

    lim_{n,m→∞} I_∞(F_n, G_m) = 1 = β_T(e_+,e_−),
    lim_{n→∞}  I_∞(F_n, F_n) = 0 = β_T(e_+,e_+),                    (10.14)

the first limit **uniform in the order of the two limits** (indeed
`I_∞(F_n,G_m) = 1 + O(\max(n,m)^{−1})`), and both limits **independent of the
choice of `ψ`** in the stated class.  Hence in the colimit chart the scalar
line `L_∞(F,G) := (C, e^{I_∞(F,G)})` reproduces `λ_{per}` on the rulings —
Weil's `e_+·e_− = 1`, `e_±·e_± = 0` — and the vector-valuedness of `λ_{RR}` at
a finite chart (Theorem 10.8) is the **finite-chart shadow** of one scalar
hyperbolic global pairing.

*Proof.*  **Step 0: the profiles.**  `ψ` real gives
`G_m^†(w) = \overline{G_m(w^{−1})} = F_m(w)`, since the profile of `G_m^†` at
`t` is `\overline{g_m(−t)} = m^{−1}e^{−t/2}ψ(t/m) = f_m(t)`.  So
`I_∞(F_n,G_m) = T_∞(F_n⋆F_m)` and `I_∞(F_n,F_n) = T_∞(F_n⋆F_n^†)`.  All four
sections are supported on the principal component `𝔠_0` and are constant `=1`
on the compact part; since the compact part is a group of total mass one, the
convolution of the two compact factors is again its indicator, and on the
`t = \log|w|_S` coordinate the convolution is ordinary additive convolution of
profiles.  Writing `ψ_n(u) := n^{−1}ψ(u/n)` (a probability density on
`[na,nb]`),

    profile of F_n⋆F_m :  h_{n,m}(t) = e^{−t/2}φ_{n,m}(t),
    φ_{n,m} := ψ_n * ψ_m , a smooth probability density on [(n+m)a,(n+m)b],
    ∫|φ'_{n,m}| = ∫|ψ'_n * ψ_m| ≤ ‖ψ'_n‖_{L^1} = ‖ψ'‖_{L^1}/n ,     (10.15)

and by symmetry `∫|φ'_{n,m}| ≤ ‖ψ'‖_{L^1}/\max(n,m)`.

**Step 1: the finite places sum to a Mertens sum.**  For a section `H`
supported on `𝔠_0` with profile `h`, (8.5) gives — as in the proof of
Theorem 9.7(a), where `a_{k,1} = h(−k\log p)` because `j_p(p^ku)` has
archimedean coordinate `p^{−k}` and all compact coordinates units, on which `H`
is constant —

    W_p(H) = (\log p) Σ_{k≠0} p^{−|k|/2} h(−k\log p) .               (10.16)

Apply this to `H = F_n⋆F_m`, `h = h_{n,m}`.  Since `φ_{n,m}` is supported in
`(0,∞)`, only `k = −j` with `j ≥ 1` contributes, and there
`h(j\log p) = p^{−j/2}φ_{n,m}(j\log p)`, while `p^{−|k|/2} = p^{−j/2}`; so

    W_p(F_n⋆F_m) = (\log p) Σ_{j≥1} p^{−j} φ_{n,m}(j\log p) ,

and summing over **all** primes, with `N = p^j`, `Λ(N) = \log p`,

    Σ_{p} W_p(F_n⋆F_m) = Σ_{N≥2} \frac{Λ(N)}{N} φ_{n,m}(\log N) .    (10.17)

The sum is **finite**: `φ_{n,m}(\log N) = 0` unless
`\log N ∈ [(n+m)a,(n+m)b]`.  In particular `T_∞(F_n⋆F_m)` is the finite sum of
Theorem 8A.7c(b), and (10.17) is exactly its finite-place part.

**Step 2: partial summation against Mertens.**  Let
`A(x) := Σ_{N≤x}Λ(N)/N`.  Mertens' theorem (elementary; no zero of any
`L`-function, no explicit formula) gives `A(x) = \log x + E(x)` with
`|E(x)| ≤ C_0` for `x ≥ 1`.  Since `t ↦ φ_{n,m}(t)` is `C^∞` with compact
support in `(0,∞)`, Riemann–Stieltjes integration by parts on `[1,∞)` — with
vanishing boundary terms, `φ_{n,m}(\log x)` being `0` near `x=1` and for large
`x` — gives

    Σ_{N} \frac{Λ(N)}{N}φ_{n,m}(\log N) = ∫_1^∞ φ_{n,m}(\log x)\,dA(x)
       = −∫_1^∞ A(x)\,φ'_{n,m}(\log x)\frac{dx}{x}
       = −∫_0^∞ (t+E(e^t))\,φ'_{n,m}(t)\,dt
       = ∫_0^∞ φ_{n,m}(t)\,dt − ∫_0^∞ E(e^t)φ'_{n,m}(t)\,dt ,

using `−∫tφ' = ∫φ` (integration by parts, compact support).  The first term is
`1`; the second is bounded by `C_0∫|φ'_{n,m}| ≤ C_0‖ψ'‖_{L^1}/\max(n,m)` by
(10.15).  Hence

    Σ_p W_p(F_n⋆F_m) = 1 + O(\max(n,m)^{−1}) ,                       (10.18)

with an implied constant depending only on `ψ` — uniform in the other index,
so the double limit is `1` in either order.

**Step 3: the archimedean place is negligible.**  `h_{n,m}` vanishes on a
neighbourhood of `t = 0` (its support starts at `(n+m)a > 0`) and for `t<0`,
so (8.5∞) reduces to `W_∞(F_n⋆F_m) = ∫_0^∞ K(t)h_{n,m}(t)dt` with
`K(t) = e^{−t/2}/(1−e^{−2t})`.  On `t ≥ (n+m)a` one has
`K(t) ≤ (1−e^{−2a})^{−1}e^{−t/2}`, so

    |W_∞(F_n⋆F_m)| ≤ (1−e^{−2a})^{−1}∫ e^{−t}φ_{n,m}(t)dt
                   ≤ (1−e^{−2a})^{−1} e^{−(n+m)a} → 0

exponentially.  Adding to (10.18) gives the first limit of (10.14).

**Step 4: the self-pairing.**  `H' := F_n⋆F_n^†` has profile
`h'(t) = n^{−1}e^{t/2}∫_R e^{−ns}ψ(s)ψ(s−t/n)\,ds`, supported in
`|t| ≤ n(b−a)`; indeed `(f_n * f_n^†)(t) = ∫f_n(u)\overline{f_n(u−t)}du` with
`f_n(u) = n^{−1}e^{−u/2}ψ(u/n)`, and the substitution `u = ns` gives the
display.  Two bounds, both from `s ≥ a` and `s − t/n ≥ a` on the support:

    |h'(t)| ≤ C_ψ n^{−1}e^{−na}e^{−|t|/2},
    |h'(t)|+|(h')'(t)| ≤ C'_ψ n^{−1}e^{−na}e^{−|t|/2}  (n ≥ 1),      (10.19)

where for `t>0` one uses `e^{−ns} ≤ e^{−na−t}` (from `s ≥ a+t/n`) together
with the prefactor `e^{t/2}`, and for `t<0` simply `e^{−ns} ≤ e^{−na}` with
the prefactor `e^{t/2} = e^{−|t|/2}`; the derivative bound adds only
`½h' + n^{−1}(\dots)ψ'`.  Now:

*Finite places.*  By (10.16), `|W_p(H')| ≤ (\log p)Σ_{k≠0}p^{−|k|/2}|h'(−k\log
p)|`.  For `k = −j < 0`, `t = j\log p > 0`, and the first bound of (10.19) in
the sharper form used above gives
`p^{−j/2}|h'(j\log p)| ≤ C_ψ n^{−1}e^{−na}p^{−j}` — because
`h'(t) = e^{t/2}·[\,n^{−1}∫e^{−ns}ψψ\,]` and the bracket is
`≤ C_ψ n^{−1}e^{−na}e^{−t}`, so `p^{−j/2}h'(t) = p^{−j/2}p^{j/2}·[\;] ≤
C_ψn^{−1}e^{−na}p^{−j}`.  For `k = j > 0`, `t = −j\log p < 0`,
`p^{−j/2}|h'(−j\log p)| = p^{−j/2}p^{−j/2}|[\;]| ≤ C_ψ n^{−1}e^{−na}p^{−j}`.
Summing,

    Σ_p |W_p(H')| ≤ 2C_ψ n^{−1}e^{−na} Σ_{N ≤ e^{n(b−a)}} \frac{Λ(N)}{N}
       ≤ 2C_ψ n^{−1}e^{−na}·(n(b−a)+C_0) → 0

exponentially, by Mertens again.  *The real place.*  Split (8.5∞) at `t=1`:
near `0`, `K(t) ≤ 1/t` and the bracket
`h'(t)+h'(−t)−2h'(0)e^{−t/2}` is `O(t·\sup|(h')'|)`, so
`∫_0^1 K|{\cdots}| ≤ C\sup|(h')'|`; for `t ≥ 1`, `K(t) ≤ 1.2e^{−t/2}` and
(10.19) gives an integrable product.  With `|h'(0)| ≤ C_ψn^{−1}e^{−na}`, all
three contributions are `O(n^{−1}e^{−na})`.  Hence `I_∞(F_n,F_n) → 0`, which
is the second limit of (10.14).

**Independence of `ψ`.**  The limits `1` and `0` do not involve `ψ`; the error
terms are `O_ψ(\max(n,m)^{−1})` and `O_ψ(e^{−na})`.  ∎

**Remark 10.8c (what this is, and what it is not).**  **[REBUILT, phase 130,
round 2.]**

1. *It is the prime number theorem, in Mertens' form.*  The value `1` in
   (10.14) is `Σ_{N≤x}Λ(N)/N ∼ \log x`: the logarithmic density of prime
   powers.  Weil's `e_+·e_− = 1` is, in the colimit chart of row A, that
   density.  That is the precise sense in which the hyperbolic ruling pairing
   is arithmetic here.
2. *It is `CLASSICAL` input and counts toward nothing.*  Mertens' theorem is
   an import (§12, import `M`), and clause A.10 forbids using `Λ(N)` in any
   clause of A.  Theorem 10.8b is therefore quarantined: it is proved, it is
   labelled, and **no clause of A.1–A.10 uses it**.  Every clause is
   discharged at a fixed finite chart `S`, where the value is `0`
   (Theorem 10.8) and the target is vector-valued.  Removing Theorem 10.8b
   from the paper would change no verdict.
3. *It does not repair the finite chart.*  At finite `S` the primes carrying
   the value `1` are outside `S`, so `I_A^S` genuinely vanishes on the
   rulings; the chart dependence is the residue overcount of Theorem 9.7b(c)
   seen from the other side.  Nothing here makes a scalar finite-chart
   determinant extend `λ_{per}`.
4. *It is what row A hands to row C.*  Row C (Paper 45) may cite (10.14) with
   its `CLASSICAL` label; it may not cite it inside row A.  It also may not
   read (10.14) as a statement about `𝓝_∞`: no claim about the colimit
   numerical radical is made anywhere (§13.8).

#### 10.3.5 The source functor, correctly typed

**Theorem 10.9 (base change along the Tate boundary).**  Let `D_A^+` be the
unitization of `D_A` and `M : D_A^+ → B_T` the unital extension of
`(λ_−,λ_+)`, `1 ↦ (1,1)`.  Then:

  (a) `M` is a **surjective unital `†`-algebra homomorphism** (`†` to `♯`).
  (b) *Base change.*  `Φ_{det} := −⊗_{D_A^+}B_T`, from bounded complexes of
      **based free** `D_A^+`-modules with differentials in `D_A^+` (the
      presented complexes of Definition 8A.1, written in matrix form) to
      bounded complexes of based free `B_T`-modules, is exact, **strictly
      monoidal on free complexes**, carries `†` to `♯`, carries `Δ` to the
      unit, and has Euler class `M∘s`:
      `Φ_{det}(K)`'s alternating sum of based generators is `M(s(K))`.
  (c) *`M` is not a homological epimorphism.*  With `I := \ker M`,

        \mathrm{Tor}_1^{D_A^+}(B_T,B_T) ≅ I/I² ,                    (10.13)

      and `I/I²` **surjects onto `C²`**; hence `\mathrm{Tor}_1 ≠ 0` and is at
      least two-dimensional, so P123.008 §7's predicted relative Tor object
      `C_{T,S} = \mathrm{Cone}(B_T⊗^L_{D_A^+}B_T → B_T)` is nonzero.
  (d) *Where the Riemann–Roch content lives.*  It is carried by
      Theorem 10.7(e), not by `C_{T,S}`; and Proposition 10.6 stands — the
      **contact** determinant is blind, and `Φ_{det}` is not of that type: it
      is base change along the Tate boundary, a different construction.

*Proof.*  **(a)**  Multiplicativity and surjectivity of `(λ_−,λ_+)` on `D_A`
are Theorem 9.1; the unital extension to `D_A^+` is multiplicative because
`M(1)=(1,1)` is the unit of `B_T`.  `M(F^†) = (λ_−(F^†),λ_+(F^†)) =
(\overline{λ_+(F)},\overline{λ_−(F)}) = M(F)^♯` by Theorem 9.1 and
P123.008 (2.1).

**(b)**  A based free module `(D_A^+)^n` has `(D_A^+)^n⊗_{D_A^+}B_T = B_T^n`,
with the basis carried to the basis; a differential given by a matrix over
`D_A^+` is carried to the matrix of its `M`-images, and `d²=0` is preserved
because `M` is a ring homomorphism.  Exactness on the category of bounded
complexes of free modules: `−⊗_{D_A^+}B_T` is right exact always and is exact
on free modules, so a degreewise-split short exact sequence of free complexes
goes to a degreewise-split short exact sequence.  *Strict monoidality:*
`(D_A^+)^m⊗_{D_A^+}(D_A^+)^n = (D_A^+)^{mn}` maps to `B_T^{mn} =
B_T^m⊗_{B_T}B_T^n` on the nose, with bases matching; the associativity and
unit constraints are identities on both sides.  `Δ = D_A^+` (rank one) goes to
`B_T` (rank one), the unit.  `†` to `♯` by (a).  *Euler class:* `s` of (8A.0)
is the alternating sum of the presentations, and `M` is linear, so
`Σ_n(−1)^nM(F_n) = M(s(K))`.

**(c)**  For any ring `R` and any surjection `R ↠ R/I`, the standard
computation from the short exact sequence `0→I→R→R/I→0` gives
`\mathrm{Tor}_1^R(R/I,R/I) ≅ I/I²` (tensor the sequence with `R/I`:
`\mathrm{Tor}_1(R,R/I)=0`, so `\mathrm{Tor}_1(R/I,R/I) = \ker(I⊗_R R/I → R/I)
= I⊗_R R/I = I/I²`, the last map being zero since `I·(R/I)=0`).  Apply it with
`R = D_A^+`, `R/I = B_T`.  For the surjection onto `C²`: define
`∂_± : I → C` by `∂_±(F) := ∂_τ\hat F(±i/2)`, using that `\hat F` extends to an
entire function of `τ` on each component (the `R^×`-profile is compactly
supported, (9.8)) and evaluating on the principal component `𝔠_0`.  These are
linear.  **[REBUILT, phase 130, round 2, finding F14 of `R130.002` and F2 of
`R130.003`: the round-1 `I²` step was written for `F,G ∈ D_A` only, whereas
`I ⊆ D_A^+` contains elements `a·1+F`.  Here it is with the unitization
carried.]**  Extend the symbol to `D_A^+` by `(a·1+F)^∧ := a + \hat F`, the
unit having symbol `1`; then `M(a1+F) = ((a+\hat F)(−i/2),(a+\hat F)(i/2))`,
and `∂_±(a1+F) = ∂_±F` because `∂_τ a = 0`.  They **kill `I²`**: if
`X = a1+F` and `Y = b1+G` lie in `I` then `\hat X(±i/2) = \hat Y(±i/2) = 0`
(that is what `M(X)=M(Y)=0` says, by (9.8)), so `(X⋆Y)^∧ = \hat X\hat Y` has a
double zero at `±i/2` and
`∂_±(X⋆Y) = \hat X(±i/2)∂_±Y + \hat Y(±i/2)∂_±X = 0`; by bilinearity `∂_±`
kills all of `I²`.  They are **jointly surjective** on `I`: restrict to the sections
supported on `𝔠_0` with profile `f ∈ C_c^∞(R)`, for which
`\hat F(τ) = ∫f(t)e^{−iτt}dt`, so

    \hat F(∓i/2) = ∫ f(t) e^{∓t/2}dt ,   ∂_τ\hat F(τ) = ∫ (−it)f(t)e^{−iτt}dt ,

so `∂_+(F) = −i∫ t f(t)e^{t/2}dt` and `∂_−(F) = −i∫ t f(t)e^{−t/2}dt`.  The four
functionals `f ↦ ∫fe^{t/2}, ∫fe^{−t/2}, ∫tfe^{t/2}, ∫tfe^{−t/2}` on
`C_c^∞(R)` are linearly independent, because the four locally integrable
functions `e^{t/2}, e^{−t/2}, te^{t/2}, te^{−t/2}` are linearly independent on
`R` (a nontrivial relation would make a nonzero function of the form
`(α+βt)e^{t/2}+(γ+δt)e^{−t/2}` vanish identically, impossible by looking at
`t→±∞`).  Hence the joint map `C_c^∞(R) → C^4` given by the four functionals is
surjective; the preimage of `\{0\}×\{0\}×C²` therefore maps **onto** `C²` under
the last two coordinates, and that preimage consists precisely of the profiles
with `\hat F(±i/2)=0`, i.e. of elements of `I`.  So `(∂_+,∂_−) : I → C²` is
surjective and kills `I²`, whence `I/I² ↠ C²`.

**(d)**  Theorem 10.7(e) is the Riemann–Roch identity, and it uses only `M`
and `I_A`.  Proposition 10.6 is about determinants of derived **diagonal
contact** complexes; `Φ_{det}` is base change along a ring map, which is a
different functor, so Proposition 10.6 does not apply to it, and conversely
`Φ_{det}` does not transport `Tr_A` — which by Theorem 10.6a nothing can.  ∎

**Remark 10.9a (what P123.008 §7 asked for, and what is now known).**
P123.008 §7 conjectured that the relative Tor `C_{T,S}` is nonzero, "since the
common fixed point has nontrivial isotropy", and asked for four things.
Theorem 10.9(c) settles the first half of the question — `\mathrm{Tor}_1` is
nonzero, of dimension at least two, with the explicit cocycles `∂_±` — so
`M` is **not** a homological epimorphism and derived base change does **not**
commute with composition without correction.  Its §7 items 1–3, which asked
that the determinant of `C_{T,S}` compute the correction between the source
trace intersection and the hyperbolic ruling determinant, are **not needed
for A.8**, and that is the substantive finding: by Theorem 10.7(e) the
correction is not a Tor determinant at all, it is the **direct sum
decomposition** of the completion (Theorem 9.14b), in which the primitive and
ruling contributions simply add.  Item 4 ("no selected arithmetic weight is
inserted") holds throughout, by §11.



---

## 11. Independence (clause A.10)

No definition or proof in Sections 2–10 uses `Λ(n)`, a distinguished `Γ_n`
beyond its realization as a twist, Meyer's formula, a zero of zeta, RH or
positivity of `B_nuc`.  The half-density is fixed by the product formula
(Theorem 9.1); the fixed-point computations of §7 are Hochschild homology; the
local factors (8.1) are Fourier orbital integrals, and the archimedean term is
P123.035 (3.1), computed there without Meyer's coefficient (P123.035 §1 shows
the literal Meyer archimedean coefficient fails an explicit Gaussian check).
The trace (8.4) is defined by the two proved local formulas P123.042 (3.2) and
P123.035 (3.1) plus a declared constant; where the analytic regularization is
compared with it (Theorem 8.10), P123.012 §1 states "Only the geometric/local
side of this theorem is used.  No spectral sum over zeros is invoked."  P123.007 §7 performs the same independence audit for the same
objects.

**[REBUILT, phase 129, round 4, finding F12.]**  One point must be made
explicitly, because the specification's closure rule (§4) asks that A.2–A.8
not be "merely a renaming of the scalar explicit formula".  Theorem 9.7
computes the symbol `T̂`, and Remark 9.7a observes that it is **verbatim the
prime-power-plus-Gamma side of the Weil explicit formula**:
`Ŵ_p = Σ_{k≥1}2Λ(p^k)p^{−k/2}\cos(kτ\log p + kθ_p)` and
`Ŵ_∞(0,τ) = \log π − Re ψ(1/4+iτ/2)`.  Three statements, in order.

1. **`Λ` is derived, not used.**  It appears as the Fourier expansion of a
   geometric series whose weights are the proved orbital integrals
   `τ_p(p^k)=p^{−|k|/2}` (P123.042) with shell volume `\log p` (P123.042 §1).
   No arithmetic family is selected; A.10's prohibition is on *using* `Λ(n)`
   or a distinguished `Γ_n`, and neither occurs.
2. **No zero of any `L`-function occurs**, anywhere in §§8–10.  The explicit
   formula is an *identity* between this symbol and a sum over zeros;
   that identity is never invoked.
3. **`𝓝 = 0` is not a renaming of it.**  Theorem 9.8 uses three facts only:
   `T̂` is real-analytic in `τ`; the finite-place terms are **bounded**; the
   archimedean term **diverges to `−∞`**.  It is a statement about the *size*
   of a symbol, and it would hold verbatim for any symbol with a dominant
   logarithmic archimedean factor and a bounded finite part.  Nothing about
   the location of zeros is used, asserted or implied.

`PROVED`.

---

## 11bis. The sector is the invertible one

**[REBUILT, phase 129; new, in answer to phase-128 finding `A12`.]**

Every generator of `G` is `E_a`, the graph bimodule of an **invertible**
external class `a ∈ C_S` (§3.1, P123.014 §§2–3).  In Weil's proof the content
is carried by `Γ_{F^k}`, of bidegree `(q^k,1)`, which is not invertible.  So:

1. **Stated plainly:** row A is constructed on the automorphism-graph sector.
   No non-invertible correspondence is an object of `G`, and none is
   constructed here.
2. **The previous dismissal of phase 123's prescription is a non sequitur and
   is withdrawn.**  §13.1 argued from Corollary 7.5 that P123.082's
   prescription ("add non-invertible objects") was not the remedy.  Corollary
   7.5 is a statement about **additive invariants of the shadow**; it says
   nothing about which objects the sector should contain.  The inference is
   invalid and the sentence is corrected in §13.1.
3. **What is true about "equality of structural burden" in the adelic
   setting.**  The specification (§1) defines Weil-strength as equality of
   structural burden, not as literal isomorphism with the finite-field
   picture, and it explicitly allows an infinite-rank topological numerical
   theory.  In the adelic setting the group that acts is the idele class
   group `C_S`, and it acts by **invertible** dilations: P123.013 §4 fixes the
   two-level carrier `(Γ_S ↷ A_S) ⋊ C_{Q,S}` in which the external arrows are
   exactly the invertible ones, and the local weights that appear in the trace,
   `τ_v(x)=|x|_v^{1/2}/|1−x|_v`, are the fixed-point weights of those
   invertible dilations (P123.042 (2.1), (3.2)).  The arithmetic family of row
   B sits inside this sector: `Γ_n = E_{a_S(n)}` with `a_S(n)=(1,n,\dots,n)`,
   `Γ_m⋆Γ_n≅Γ_{mn}`, Tate modulus `1/n` (P123.038 §1, status `CONSTRUCTED`).
   So the sector is not empty of arithmetic content.
4. **What is nevertheless not established.**  Weil's argument uses the
   *degree* of `Γ_{F^k}` and the Hodge index theorem on a numerical group
   containing it.  Nothing here supplies a non-invertible object, a bidegree,
   or an index theorem.  P123.021 §2 additionally proves that for a map of
   degree `d>1` the transpose is not the inverse — at cycle level
   `Γ_F⋆Γ_F^t = Q·Δ`, so the inverse, if rational coefficients are allowed, is
   `Q^{−1}Γ_F^t`, not `Γ_F^t` — so the dagger of §5 cannot be extended to
   non-invertible graphs without a normalization that is not constructed.
   Therefore: **"equality of structural burden" with Weil's proof is not
   established.**  Row A is constructed on the invertible sector, and that is
   the claim.

5. **Re-assessment of item 4.**  **[REBUILT, phase 130.  Item 4 is kept above
   as written in round 4.  Three of its four ingredients are now supplied, and
   the fourth is excluded from row A by the specification itself; what remains
   unsupplied is narrower than item 4 says, and the narrowing is recorded
   here rather than by editing the earlier text.]**

   (i) *The bidegree is supplied.*  Theorem 9.1 gives the two external Tate
   characters, so every generator has a **bidegree**

       M(E_a) = ( |a|_S^{−1/2} ,\; |a|_S^{+1/2} ) ∈ B_T ,           (11.1)

   the half-density normalization of the classical bidegree `(1,q)` of
   `Γ_F` (P123.008 (2.3): `ρ_T(δ_q) = (q^{−1/2},q^{1/2})`).  Weil's proof uses
   the bidegree, and this is it.

   (ii) *The normalized Frobenius is invertible, and that is a theorem, not a
   restriction.*  P123.021 §2's cycle-level identity is
   `Γ_F⋆Γ_F^t = q·Δ`; the half-density of §5.2 divides by `q^{1/2}` at each
   slot, and in this normalization `E_a⋆E_a^† = E_a⋆E_{a^{−1}} = E_1 = Δ`
   exactly, by (3.2) and Definition 5.2, with
   `β_T(M E_a, M E_a) = 2` independent of `a` — the normalized self-pairing of
   a graph, matching `d_1d_2 = 1`.  The normalization P123.021 says is "not
   constructed" for the dagger of non-invertible graphs **is** constructed
   here, for the invertible realization, and it is the half-density.  Item 4's
   inference that the dagger "cannot be extended … without a normalization
   that is not constructed" is therefore withdrawn as stated: the correct
   statement is that this paper realizes the normalized graphs, and does not
   realize the unnormalized non-invertible ones as objects.

   (iii) *The span Weil's proof uses is present.*  Weil's argument runs on
   `aΔ + bΓ_{F^k} + (\text{rulings})`.  Here `Δ = E_1`, the normalized
   Frobenius powers are `E_{a}` (in particular `Γ_n = E_{a_S(n)}` with Tate
   modulus `1/n`, P123.038 §1), they are effective (Def. 10.1) and pairwise
   non-isomorphic (Prop. 3.2(iv)), and the **rulings** are objects of the
   theory too, as the canonical plane `σ(B_T) ⊆ N_A^{dist}` of
   Theorems 9.14a–b.  **[REBUILT, phase 130, round 2, finding F15 of
   `R130.002`: the three ingredients live in three different objects, and
   round 1's "the span is available" blurred that.  Exactly: `Δ = E_1` and the
   `E_a` are objects of `G`, and by Remark 8.11 `Δ` is **not** trace class, so
   they are not objects of `Corr_A^{tr}`; the smeared objects `E_F`, `F ∈ D_A`
— and `E_H`, `H ∈ 𝓐_tr`, by Theorem 8A.12 — are where `Tr_A`, `I_A`, `M`
   and `λ_{RR}` live; and the rulings are in the numerical completion
   `N_A^{dist}`.  What is true is that each of Weil's three ingredients has a
   realization somewhere in this theory, joined by the comparison maps
   `F ↦ E_F` and `J`.  What is **not** true, and is not claimed, is that all
   three are objects of one and the same of the three.]**

   (iv) *The index theorem is a sign, and the specification excludes it from
   row A.*  Clause A.8 ends "This clause supplies no Hodge-index sign", and
   clause A.10 forbids using positivity.  So the missing Hodge index is not a
   missing piece of row A; it is row D's, and Theorem 14.1 is this paper's
   honest report on what shape row D will *not* find here.

   (v) *What is still not in row A.*  A **non-invertible** correspondence as
   an object.  Everything above is on the automorphism-graph sector, and the
   bidegree (11.1) is the bidegree of a normalized graph.  Whether an
   unnormalized `Γ_{F}` of bidegree `(1,q)` can be an object, with a dagger
   and a trace, is not addressed by this paper.  **Conclusion:** every clause
   of the specification is discharged on the declared sector (§14), and the
   remaining difference from Weil's *proof* — as opposed to the
   specification's clauses — is the index sign and the non-invertible objects.
   That is a smaller and more precise statement than item 4's, and it replaces
   it.

## 13. What was tried and withdrawn

### 13.1 The Euler-characteristic pairing
The first numerical theory used the boundary corner shadow and
`χ(Tr^∂(K⋆L^†))`.  It satisfies the clauses and is worthless: its value on
the arithmetic family is zero, and Corollary 7.5 shows the full shadow does
not help.  This is P123.082's judgment made precise.  **[REBUILT, phase 129, finding
A12:]** the previous sentence added "its prescription (add non-invertible
objects) was not the remedy", inferred from Corollary 7.5.  That inference is
a non sequitur — Corollary 7.5 is about additive invariants of the shadow, not
about which objects the sector contains — and it is withdrawn.  See §11bis.

### 13.2 The de Rham dualizing object
The first dagger, aimed at the localized ideals, used `B⊗̂(S→S dt)` as
dualizing object with a conditional comparison.  Theorem 7.1(d) shows the
complex is HKR; on `G` the dagger is the Hilbert adjoint directly.  The
construction remains valid for the ideal sector, whose duals are boundary
profile objects `[I_J,U]`.

### 13.3 The boundary determinant target
A proposed target of graded line bundles on `B_q` was proved trivial on every
graph class: no isomorphism-class invariant of bundles separates a graph from
the diagonal.  An instance of Corollary 7.5.

### 13.4 A false intermediate theorem, and its correction
The first version of this paper stated that the full shadow of every
nontrivial class is **acyclic** over `C`, derived the invariant as the
Hochschild **torsion** of that acyclic complex, and gave (8.1) as the torsion
of a Koszul complex inside `E_p`.  This is wrong: the origin `x_p=0` is fixed
by every external dilation and contributes a rank-one class independent of
the twist (Theorem 7.1(c), P123.084), so the shadow is not acyclic and has no
Koszul differential at finite places (Remark 7.3).  The corrected statement
is Theorem 7.4: distinct classes with **isomorphic** shadows, which yields the
same Corollary 7.5 with a cleaner proof, and the invariant is the analytic
relative trace of the corpus with local factors (8.1), whose identification
with the smooth-model torsion is P123.060 §4.  The exact separating computation survives — **[REBUILT, phase 129:]** as
Theorem 9.5, restated for sections of the declared trace-class domain, since
`E_u` itself is not trace class (Remark 8.11).  Phase 125 records the correction
as A125.016.

### 13.5 A second false statement, and its correction
The corrected version of §7.1(c) asserted that every nontrivial finite-place
twist contributes only the origin class, and §7.2 concluded that two unit
classes have isomorphic shadows.  Appendix D.3 shows that a unit twist `u`
with `k=v_p(1−u)` leaves the `N_k=(p−1)p^{k−1}` characters trivial on
`1+p^kZ_p` in twisted degree zero, one copy per shell.  The shadows are not
isomorphic; they are infinite-dimensional.  The conclusion that no finite
additive invariant exists is unchanged and its proof is now correct
(Corollary 7.5); the analytic factor (8.1) is confirmed as the
Haar-regularized orbit count at a unit and the half-density at a non-unit.
**[REBUILT, phase 129:]** the former Theorem 9.3 is replaced by
Theorem 9.5.  The misuse of P123.096 for shell translations is
withdrawn; that note is used only for `C(B_q)`.  Phase 125 records this as
A125.017.

---

### 13.6 The germ definition of `T`, and its correction

**[REBUILT, phase 129.]**  The previous Definition 8.1 read: "`T(E_w)` is the
logarithm of the coupled induced relative determinant germ of P123.109–110
evaluated on `β_w`", and the previous (8.2) wrote `T(E_w)=Σ_{γ∈Γ_S}Σ_v log
τ_v(γw)`, said to be "not absolutely convergent", and Theorem 9.3 computed
with (8.2).  Three things were wrong.

1. **Domain.**  P123.109–110's germs are constructed for *small external
   Bruhat tests* (operator norms `<1/4`) in the induced single-zero normal
   representation at *one* normal place `q`.  `β_w` is an automorphism, not
   such a test.  Evaluating the germ on it is outside the germ's domain.
2. **Two different objects.**  The germ and (8.2) were never proved equal, and
   the paper defined `T` by the first and computed with the second.
3. **Wrong shape.**  Even formally, `Σ_v log τ_v` is not the shape of the
   relative trace: (8.4) shows the trace is `Σ_v W_v + c·F(1)`, a **sum of local
   distributions whose densities are the `τ_v` themselves**, not a sum of
   their logarithms.  Appendix A.2's withdrawn sentence made the same error in
   the opposite direction, reading `(log p)p^{−k/2}` as `log` of a product.

The replacement is Definition 8.6: a declared cutoff regularization whose
existence is the imported theorem `C`, whose local decomposition is proved
(Definition 8.6), whose ambiguity is exactly a distribution at the identity class
(Theorem 8.9), and which is **regularization-free** on `D_A^{off}` (Theorem 8.8),
where the separating computation of Theorem 9.5 lives.  The former "Lemma 8.2"
is withdrawn: the ambiguity is `c·F(1)`, not a ruling term, and no proof that
`δ_1` is a ruling term is known to me.  The former `T(Δ)` is withdrawn:
`Δ` is not in the trace-class domain (Remark 8.11).  P123.107–110 are now
cited only for what they prove — convergence of a germ, and a composition
cocycle — and never for a geometric identification, which P123.108 and
P123.109 both disclaim in their own status lines.

---

### 13.7 A false impossibility claim, and its correction

**[REBUILT, phase 129, round 3.]**  The round-2 version of §9.3 argued that
clause A.6 was structurally blocked: that `T` is carried by Haar-null slices
`j_v(Q_v^×)` while `λ_±` are Haar integrals, so that `λ_±` could not be
reached from `T`; and that the only route in the corpus — the Weil explicit
formula, through P123.035 §5's identification of the polar packet with
`λ_−+λ_+` — is barred by clause A.10.  It concluded that A.6 and A.10 are in
tension as the specification is written.

**That is wrong, and it is withdrawn entirely.**  The error is a confusion of
supports.  The numerical radical is not a condition on where a distribution
lives in the *space* variable; it is a condition on **Fourier** support:
`𝓝 = {F : F̂ = 0 on supp(T̂+c)}` — **[REBUILT, phase 129, round 4, finding F7:
the constant must be carried, `δ_1` having symbol identically `1`]**.  A
distribution can be carried by a single
point and still have full Fourier support — `c·δ_1` is the example — so
"carried by a null set" is not an obstruction to anything.  Once the question
is posed on the dual group it is a computation with the local distributions
that were already proved, and it comes out: Theorem 9.8.  No zeta zero, no
`Λ(n)`, no explicit formula and no positivity enter; A.10 is untouched.

Two lessons are recorded rather than deleted.  First, an impossibility claim
needs a proof exactly as much as a possibility claim does, and round 2 offered
none — the "argument" was an observation about supports with no implication
drawn.  Second, the reflex to protect a negative verdict is the mirror image
of the failure mode the corpus's method note names ("each correction preserved
the conclusion by construction"), and it is the same failure.

### 13.8 The phase-130 withdrawals

**[REBUILT, phase 130.]**  Four formulations are withdrawn here, in the manner
of §§13.1–13.7: the withdrawn text is left in place where it stands, and this
entry records what was wrong with it.

1. **"`Φ_det` carries `Tr_A` to `λ_per`" (round 4's display "(A.8-I)",
   §10.2).**  *Withdrawn as unsatisfiable.*  Theorem 10.6a: any functor whose
   value depends only on `M(s(K))` cannot determine `Tr_A`, because
   Theorem 9.5's two sections have equal `M` and different `T`.  The
   requirement was modelled on Weil's theory, where it is also false —
   `Tr(D) = D·Δ` is not a function of the bidegree.  The clause's actual
   content is *extension* of `λ_per` by `λ_RR`, Theorem 10.7(e).  Lesson: a
   requirement stated for a clause must be checked against the classical model
   it is imitating before being declared the binding constraint.

2. **"A.8 is `OPEN`, and is the binding constraint on row A" (Theorem 10.5's
   verdict, §14's round-4 ledger).**  *Superseded.*  The obstruction was the
   insistence on the scalar target `T_A = Pic_b(C)`; Theorem 10.8 proves that
   with a scalar target the clause is **unachievable**, and Definition 8A.2′
   replaces it by `Pic_b(B_T)`, which P123.008 §6 already identified as the
   determinant target of the Tate realization.  The mathematics that was
   missing was not a functor but the two theorems 9.14a–b, which put the
   rulings into the theory as objects of the completion.

3. **"(O-A5-1b) is missing mathematics" (§8A.3, round 4).**  *Withdrawn.*  The
   assessment conflated two determinants: the **operator-cutoff** germ
   `det(1+zR_Λ(F))`, which P123.105 §4 and P123.107 §5 disclaim and which may
   carry a Szegő-type anomaly, and the **`\exp`-type** relative determinant
   `\exp T` of P123.012, which is the determinant of this theory and whose
   cutoff limit Theorem 9.7(d) has already performed.  Theorem 8A.7b
   constructs the germ of the latter for all shells and all places at once.
   Lesson: an obstacle inherited from a note must be re-typed against the
   object the paper actually uses.

4. **(8A.13) as an unconditional identity.**  *Corrected to (8A.13′).*  With
   `c := 0` declared, the imported cutoff limit equals `e^{T(F)}` only up to
   `e^{c_{cut}F(1)}`, `c_{cut}` undetermined (Remark 8A.7e).  Nothing in the
   paper uses (8A.13); the correction is recorded so that no reader takes the
   Fredholm realization to be constant-free.

5. **"`E_F ≅ E_G ⟹ F = G` is needed" ((O-A5-4)(a)).**  *Withdrawn as a
   requirement* (Theorem 8A.11), on the ground that the classical trace is a
   function of a cycle with multiplicities and not of the underlying object.
   What is proved instead, and is what was really wanted, is that `F ↦ E_F` is
   **injective** (Theorem 8A.10).

6. **"The continuous extension of `L_S` to `σ(B_T)`" (round-1 Theorem 10.8).**
   *Withdrawn as an object.*  `I_A` does not extend to pairs of elements of
   `N_A^{dist}` (P123.017 §6), so there is no such extension to speak of.  What
   replaces it: Definition 9.14c0 names the class of **admissible
   approximations**, Theorem 9.14c′ proves the limit statement for the whole
   class, Definition 10.6c's value on the ruling plane is a **definition**, and
   Remark 9.14c″ records that no net-independent extension is claimed.

7. **"There is none, for any construction whatsoever" (round-1 consequence of
   Theorem 10.6a).**  *Withdrawn.*  Theorem 10.6a kills the functors that
   factor through the Tate fibre; round 4's "(A.8-I)" did not stipulate that.
   The hypothesis is now stated, and the impossibility is **not** claimed for
   functors that do not factor.

8. **Reason (iii) for `c := 0` (round-1 Definition 8A.7d).**  *Withdrawn.*  The
   "intrinsic Mittag–Leffler constant" of a local symbol is not an invariant —
   the expansion is canonical only up to the grouping of convergence factors —
   so the claimed uniqueness has no content.  `c := 0` is a declaration, and
   the paper says only that.

9. **"`Tr_A = \exp` of the Haar-regularized Euler characteristic of `Sh`"
   (round-1 Theorem 8A.9(c)).**  *Corrected.*  At the non-unit shells the
   shadow has rank `1` and the arithmetic is entirely in the declared
   half-density–Lefschetz weight; (8A.24)–(8A.24′) separate the two regimes,
   and Remark 8A.9a states which half is a theorem.

10. **"The positive index at finite `S` is a chart artifact" (round-1 §14.1
    item 4).**  *Withdrawn as a backwards inference.*  The correction terms are
    non-negative on the cone, so adding places makes the form **more**
    positive.  Only the window statement survives.

11. **"`σ(B_T)` is P123.007 §4's `R_∂`" (round-1 Theorem 9.14c, Remark 9.14d).**
    *Downgraded to an analogy.*  P123.007 (4.2) is a radical against a
    different completion, on which `I_A` is not defined here.  (9.24) is a
    definition internal to this paper, and `R_∂ = σ(B_T)` is proved for it.

12. **"The completions of the test algebra close the category" (round-1
    §3.3bis, clause A.2).**  *Replaced by a construction:* Theorem 8A.12 builds
    the objects `E_H = λ(H)` over `𝓐_tr`; Remark 8A.12a records that
    `N_A^{dist}` is not a category completion.

**A statement that is deliberately NOT made.**  **[REBUILT, phase 130.]**  One
might hope to prove `𝓝_∞ = 0` for the colimit `D_A^∞` of Theorem 8A.7c —
the analogue of Theorem 9.8 with all places present, whose symbol is
`T̂_∞ = Ŵ_∞ + Σ_{\text{all }p}Ŵ_p`.  **No such statement is claimed here.**
Theorem 9.8 is proved **per chart**, for each finite `S`, and that is all that
clause A.6 (which is a statement about `N_A`, per chart) requires.  The colimit
statement is a uniqueness assertion for a convolution equation with an
exponentially growing arithmetic kernel, and every route I know to it passes
through the analytic continuation of `−ζ'/ζ` and a comparison of the
Riemann–von Mangoldt zero count with a Cartwright-type zero bound for `\hat f`
— **classical input about the zeros of zeta, which clause A.10 forbids in row
A**.  It is therefore recorded here as `CLASSICAL`, **declined by row C**
(Paper 45 §0.2, §14.3) **and claimed by no row**, and it is used nowhere in
this paper.  **[phase 133, PANEL_2 MINOR-2: this read "**belonging to row C**".
The phase-132 edit corrected the §14.0 obstacle row to "declined by row C …
not claimed by any row" but did not reach here; Paper 45 declines it
explicitly.  No clause depends on it.]**

## 14. Verdict and clause ledger

**[REBUILT, phase 130, round 2.  The round-1 block is replaced after the
adversarial audit `R130.002` (20 findings) and the final audit `R130.003` (F1–F6).
Every finding is disposed of in `R130_004_ROUND2.md`; the changes that
move a verdict are: A.2 (objects over the completion now constructed,
Thm. 8A.12), A.8 (verdict qualified, `WITH TARGET Pic_b(B_T)`, and the
auditor's dissent recorded verbatim in §10.3.3), and O-A5-5 (disposal
downgraded to exactly what (8A.24)–(8A.24′) prove).]**

### 14.0a The carrier, named once

**[REBUILT, phase 130, round 2, finding F7 of `R130.002`.  Specification §1
requires that the structure "belong to one theory on one carrier"; the paper
must therefore say which carrier each clause is discharged on, and not let
each clause pick the one that suits it.]**

```text
THE CARRIER IS A FIXED FINITE CHART S (infinity in S, S_f nonempty),
with the chart system {C_S, pi_TS} and its colimit D_A^infty as
ADDITIONAL STRUCTURE on that carrier, not as a replacement for it.

  discharged ON THE CHART SYSTEM AND ITS COLIMIT:
      A.1 (restriction, comparison maps, descent-as-chart-system: 3.3bis)
      A.5's restriction requirement (O-A5-2): T_infty is chart-invariant
          on D_A^infty (Thm 8A.7c)
  discharged AT A FIXED FINITE CHART S:
      A.2, A.3, A.4, A.5 (all other requirements), A.6, A.7, A.8, A.9
  NOT CLAIMED on the colimit:
      N_infty = 0 or N^W_infty = 0 (section 13.8); consequently (3.10)
      is stated but used by NO clause.  The per-chart sequence (9.17),
      with middle term N_A = D_A(S), is where A.6-A.8 are proved.
  WHY the chart dependence is real and not formal: by Thm 9.7b(c) the
      Tate residue of T-hat_S is -/+ i(|S_f|+1); the numerical theory
      genuinely moves with S, and Thm 8A.7c(e) says exactly on which
      tests (radius < log l_next(S)) the chart form equals the colimit
      form.
```

### 14.1 What row A hands to rows B, C and D — including a negative

**[REBUILT, phase 129, round 4, finding F6: the shape of the numerical form is
a result, and it is a negative one for any downstream positivity argument.
It is stated here as a theorem so that rows B/C/D cannot inherit a false
expectation.]**

**Theorem 14.1 (the numerical form has no Hodge-index shape).**  For
`F ∈ D_A`,

    I_A(F,F) = T(F⋆F^†) = ∫_{Ĉ_S} |F̂(χ)|² ( T̂(χ) + c ) dχ ,        (14.1)

a **real** number.  Let `m := sup_{Ĉ_S} T̂ < ∞` (Theorem 9.7(c)).  Then:

  (a) `I_A` has **infinite negative index**, for every `c`: `T̂+c → −∞` on
      every component, so `{T̂+c < −K}` contains a nonempty open set for every
      `K`, and tests whose symbols **concentrate** in a sequence of such sets
      span an infinite-dimensional negative-definite subspace.
      **[phase 133: "concentrate", not "are supported in".  See the proof.]**
  (b) `I_A` has **infinite positive index if and only if `c > −m`**, and then
      `I_A` is **indefinite**, by the same concentration argument applied to
      an open set on which `T̂+c ≥ κ > 0`.
  (c) For the natural normalization `c = 0` — the one the imported semilocal
      theorem would supply if (O-A5-3) were settled with `c_{an}=0` — the
      hypothesis of (b) holds, since

        m ≥ T̂(ε=0, τ=0; all κ_q = 1)
          = log π − ψ(1/4) + Σ_{p∈S_f} (log p)·\frac{2p^{−1/2}}{1−p^{−1/2}}
          ≥ log π + γ + 3\log 2 + π/2 ≈ 5.372 > 0 .                (14.2)

  (d) `(D_A^{prim})^{⊥I_A} = 0` and there is no `D_A`-module splitting of the
      Tate boundary map (Theorem 9.13).

**Remark 14.1a (`[phase 135]`: part (d) and the Weil completion).**  Part (d),
`(D_A^{prim})^{⊥I_A} = 0`, is a statement **inside `D_A`**, and Paper 46
Theorem 11.12 proves its colimit analogue for `I_∞` on the radial sector, by
zero counting.  **[CORRECTED, phase 135 round 2, finding M3 of `R135_002`: the
zero-counting argument there compares the zeros of `Φ_N` with the **distinct**
zeros of `ξ`, not with the Riemann–von Mangoldt count, which is taken with
multiplicity.  Paper 46 Theorem 11.12 now imports a positive proportion of
simple zeros ((X18), Conrey 1989) to supply the distinct count.  The statement
of Theorem 11.12, and everything in this remark, is unchanged.]**  Both remain true after the completion of Paper 46 §11, and
there is no conflict with the canonical class `K` of Paper 46 Theorem 11.7,
which is a **nonzero** element of the perp of the primitive sector: `K` is not
compactly supported, and **every element of `D_A` and of the colimit `D_A^∞`
of Theorem 8A.7c is compactly supported** — the colimit enlarges the chart, not
the support, and (8A.21) is a finite sum precisely because `R(F) < ∞`.  So the
perp becomes nonzero exactly in the passage to the Weil class `D^W` of Paper 46
Definition 11.1, which is **not** the colimit and on which `T_∞`'s own formula
(8A.21) does not converge.  *The Hodge-index shape this theorem denies at every
chart is a phenomenon of that completion, and of no smaller one.*  Nothing in
this paper changes.

*Proof.*  (14.1) is (9.13) applied to `H = F⋆F^†`, whose symbol is `|F̂|²`,
together with reality of `T̂` (Theorem 9.7(c)); the value is real because
`|F̂|²` and `T̂+c` are.  (a)–(b).  **[phase 133: the previous
argument was withdrawn.  It chose `F̂` **supported** in disjoint open sets of
`{T̂+c<0}` and invoked "`D_A`'s Fourier images contain all Schwartz functions
in `τ`".  That is **false**: `D_A = C_c^∞(C_S)` is compactly supported in the
`R^×` coordinate, so by Paley–Wiener each profile transform `F̂(𝔠,·)` is entire
of exponential type and is never compactly supported in `τ`; the Fourier image
is a proper subspace of `S(R)` and the disjoint-support construction does not
exist.  The conclusions (a),(b) are true, and are proved here by
concentration instead of support.]**

Fix a component `𝔠` and work with profiles: for `φ ∈ C_c^∞(R)` let `F[φ]` be
the test with profile `φ` on `𝔠` and `0` on the other components, so
`F̂[φ](𝔠,τ) = φ̂(τ)` and `I_A(F[φ],F[φ]) = ∫_R |φ̂(τ)|²(T̂(𝔠,τ)+c)\frac{dτ}{2π}`.
Two operations stay inside `C_c^∞(R)` and act on `φ̂` by the two motions we
need: `φ(t) ↦ e^{iat}φ(t)` translates `φ̂` by `a`, and `φ(t) ↦ φ(t/δ)`
dilates it, `φ̂ ↦ δ·φ̂(δ·)`.  Fix once and for all `φ_0 ∈ C_c^∞(R)` with
`‖φ̂_0‖_{L²} = 1`; `φ̂_0` is a Schwartz function on `R` (integration by parts).
Write `φ_{a,δ}(t) := δ^{−1/2}e^{iat}φ_0(t/δ)`, so that
`φ̂_{a,δ}(τ) = δ^{1/2}φ̂_0(δ(τ−a))` has `L²`-norm `1` and, for any `ρ > 0`,

    ∫_{|τ−a| > ρ} |φ̂_{a,δ}|²dτ = ∫_{|σ| > δρ}|φ̂_0(σ)|²dσ  →  0   (δ → ∞),

and, using the growth bound `|T̂(𝔠,τ)| ≤ A_S + B_S\log(2+|τ|)` established in
the proof of Lemma 9.14a0 from Theorem 9.7,

    ∫_{|τ−a| > ρ} (|T̂(𝔠,τ)|+|c|)|φ̂_{a,δ}(τ)|²dτ
      ≤ ∫_{|σ|>δρ}\bigl(A_S+|c|+B_S\log(2+|a|+|σ|/δ)\bigr)|φ̂_0(σ)|²dσ
      →  0   (δ → ∞),                                              (14.1a)

because `φ̂_0` is Schwartz and the weight grows only logarithmically.  So the
**weighted** tail, not merely the `L²` tail, can be made arbitrarily small on
any window `(a−ρ,a+ρ)` of any fixed width.

*(a).*  By Theorem 9.7(c), `T̂(𝔠,·)+c → −∞`.  Choose `K_j ↑ ∞`, and centres
`a_j` and radii `ρ_j > 0` with `T̂(𝔠,τ)+c ≤ −K_j` on `I_j := (a_j−ρ_j,a_j+ρ_j)`,
the `I_j` pairwise disjoint.  By (14.1a) choose `δ_j` so large that the
weighted tail of `ψ_j := φ_{a_j,δ_j}` outside `I_j` is at most `ε_j`, with
`ε_j > 0` to be fixed, and so large that `∫_{I_j}|ψ̂_j|² ≥ 1/2`.  Then

    I_A(F[ψ_j],F[ψ_j]) ≤ −K_j·\tfrac12 + ε_j ,

negative once `K_j ≥ 4ε_j`.  For the **subspace**, put
`u_j := F[ψ_j]/\sqrt{K_j}` and let `G_{jk} := I_A(u_j,u_k)`.  Diagonally
`G_{jj} ≤ −1/2`.  Off the diagonal, by Cauchy–Schwarz against the measure
`(|T̂|+|c|)dτ/2π` and because `ψ̂_j` and `ψ̂_k` have their weighted mass in the
disjoint windows `I_j`, `I_k`,

    |G_{jk}| ≤ (K_jK_k)^{−1/2}\Bigl(∫(|T̂|+|c|)|ψ̂_j|²\Bigr)^{1/2}
                                  \Bigl(∫(|T̂|+|c|)|ψ̂_k|²\Bigr)^{1/2}

with each factor split over `I_j`, `I_k` and their complements; every one of
the four resulting products contains a tail factor, so choosing the `ε_j`
inductively small (each `δ_j` after `δ_1,…,δ_{j−1}` are fixed) gives
`|G_{jk}| ≤ 2^{−j−k−4}` for `j ≠ k`.  The real symmetric form `G` is then
strictly diagonally dominant with negative diagonal, hence negative definite
on `span{u_j}`, which is infinite-dimensional (the `u_j` are independent, `G`
being nondegenerate on every finite sub-block).  This is the infinite negative
index, for every `c`, since the construction used only `T̂+c → −∞`.

*(b).*  If `c ≤ −m` then `T̂+c ≤ 0` everywhere and `I_A ≤ 0`, so the positive
index is `0`.  If `c > −m`, pick `𝔠` and a window `I = (a−ρ,a+ρ)` with
`T̂(𝔠,·)+c ≥ κ > 0` on `I` (possible: `T̂` is continuous and `\sup T̂ = m`).
Split `I` into countably many disjoint subwindows `I_1,I_2,…` and repeat the
construction inside them: with `ψ_j` concentrated in `I_j` and weighted tail
`≤ ε_j`,

    I_A(F[ψ_j],F[ψ_j]) ≥ κ·\tfrac12 − ε_j > 0   for ε_j < κ/4 ,

and the same diagonal-dominance argument (now with positive diagonal bounded
below by `κ/2` after normalizing `‖ψ̂_j‖_{L²}=1`, and off-diagonal entries
`≤ 2^{−j−k−4}κ`) gives an infinite-dimensional positive-definite subspace.
With (a) this makes `I_A` indefinite with infinite index on both sides.  Note
that no `F̂` is compactly supported anywhere in this argument.  (c):
at `τ=0` with all `κ_q=1`, every finite-place term of (9.11) is
`(log p)2Re(ζ/(1−ζ))` with `ζ = p^{−1/2}` real and positive, hence **positive**,
and the archimedean term is (9.12b).  `ψ(1/4) = −γ − 3\log 2 − π/2`.  (d) is
Theorem 9.13.  ∎

**What rows B, C and D must take from this.**

> **ROW D'S TARGET (boxed identically in Paper 43 §14.1, Paper 44 §8bis.4(vi)
> and §10.4, and Paper 45 §14.4).**  **[phase 133, PANEL_2 §5 / PANEL_3
> recommendation.]**
>
> **ROW D'S TARGET: I_∂ ≤ 0 on every primitive radial class Γ_g, g ∈ C_c^∞(R), on the colimit D_A^∞ with T_∞ and c = 0 (Paper 45 Cor 9.6a); this implies RH by Paper 45 Thm 9.6 (9.10) and Thm 10.4. Standing negatives: Paper 43 Thm 14.1, Paper 44 Thm 8bis.4a; Paper 44 Thm 8bis.7 is conditional under (H_L), OPEN.**


1. **There is no Hodge-index statement to be had on `N_A` as it stands.**  The
   form is indefinite with infinite index on both sides, and the primitive
   sector has no orthogonal complement; so the classical shape "positive on
   the primitive part of a hyperbolic-plus-definite decomposition" is not
   available.  Any downstream positivity argument must either change the form
   (e.g. compose with a positive operator, as row C's `Π = −∂_t²+¼` does), or
   restrict to a sector on which `T̂+c` has a sign — and by (a) such a sector
   is never all of `D_A`.
2. **The sign character depends on the constant `c`, which is now declared.**
   **[REBUILT, phase 130.]**  By (b), if `c ≤ −m` the form would be negative
   **definite**; with `c := 0` (Definition 8A.7d) and `m ≥ 5.372 > 0` by
   (14.2), the form is **indefinite with infinite index on both sides**,
   unconditionally.  The signature is therefore settled — and settled the
   wrong way for any naive Hodge-index hope.  Note that `c` was *declared*,
   not derived; a reader who prefers another normalization gets the same
   conclusion for every `c > −m`, and for `c ≤ −m` gets a negative definite
   form, which is equally useless for a Hodge index (it would make *every*
   class negative, rulings included).

3. **The ruling plane is a quotient of `N_A` and a subobject of
   `N_A^{dist}`.**  **[REBUILT, phase 130; round 4 said only the first half.]**
   Inside the dense test sector it is a quotient and nothing more
   (Theorem 9.13(a)), so a statement of the form "the ruling component of a
   test class" must be phrased through `M = (λ_−,λ_+)`.  Inside the completion
   it is the canonical plane `σ(B_T)` (Theorems 9.14a–b), it is exactly the
   boundary radical of the scalar form (Theorem 9.14c), and the completion
   splits canonically as `primitive ⊕ rulings`.  Rows B and C may use both
   statements; they may not conflate them.

4. **The finite-chart positive index is a chart artifact outside a window.**
   **[REBUILT, phase 130.]**  By Theorem 8A.7c(a), `W_ℓ(J_{S,T}F) = 0` whenever
   `\log ℓ > R(F)`, the radius of `F`.  Hence, by Theorem 8A.7c(e), on tests
   of radius `< \log ℓ_{next}(S)` — `ℓ_{next}(S)` the least prime not in
   `S_f` — the chart form `I_A^S` **equals** the colimit form `I_∞` exactly,
   and inside that window any statement about `I_A^S` is a statement about the
   global object.  Outside it the two differ by the finite sum (8A.21).
   **[REBUILT, phase 130, round 2, finding F6 of `R130.002`: round 1 inferred
   from the positivity of those terms that the finite-`S` positive index is a
   "chart artifact".  That inference is **backwards** and is deleted: for
   `F ≥ 0` the correction terms are non-negative, so `I_∞(F,F) ≥ I_A^S(F,F)` —
   adding places makes the form **more** positive, not less.]**  What is
   proved, and all that is proved: inside the window the two forms agree
   exactly; outside it the comparison is known only on the positive cone,
   where the correction is non-negative.  The sign is a purely global
   statement and belongs to rows B and D.

5. **What row A hands to row C about the rulings: a `CLASSICAL` statement,
   quarantined.**  **[REBUILT, phase 130, round 2, finding F4 of `R130.003`.]**
   Theorem 10.8b proves that in the **colimit** chart the scalar form is
   hyperbolic on the rulings, `\lim I_∞(F_n,G_m) = 1 = β_T(e_+,e_−)` and
   `\lim I_∞(F_n,F_n) = 0`, and that the value `1` is Mertens' theorem — the
   logarithmic density of prime powers.  Three things must travel with it.
   (i) It is `CLASSICAL`: it uses `Λ(N)`, so by clause A.10 it **counts toward
   no clause of A**, it is used by no theorem of this paper, and deleting it
   would change no verdict (Remark 10.8c(2)).  (ii) It does **not** repair the
   finite chart: at finite `S` the primes carrying the value `1` are outside
   `S`, and Theorem 10.8 stands — which is why `λ_{RR}` is vector-valued.
   The vector-valuedness at finite `S` is the finite-chart **shadow** of one
   scalar hyperbolic global pairing.  (iii) It says nothing about `𝓝_∞^W`;
   no claim about the colimit numerical radical is made anywhere (§13.8).
   Row C (Paper 45) may cite it with its label; row A may not.

### 14.1a Concretely, what rows B and C may use

Rows B and C have been decoupled from the withdrawn Definition 8.1 and rest
only on the **proved** local factors of P123.042 — that is, on Lemma 8.2 and
formula (8.5) above, not on any germ.  Concretely:

* the local density `τ_v(x)=|x|_v^{1/2}/|1−x|_v`, with `τ_p(p^k u)=p^{−|k|/2}`
  for `k≠0` and `τ_p = 1/|1−x|_p` at a unit, exact and eventually constant in
  the cutoff (Lemma 8.2);
* the local decomposition `T = Σ_v W_v + c·F(1)` with the explicit `W_p` of
  (8.5), the explicit `W_∞` of P123.035 (3.1), and `c` the open constant of
  Remark 8.6b **[REBUILT, phase 129, round 3, finding G6: round 2 restated
  this without the `c` term]**; and the symbol `T̂` of Theorem 9.7, which is
  new and fully explicit;
* the graph sector `G`, its composition, its half-density dagger and its
  excision (§§3–5);
* the realization `Γ_n = E_{a_S(n)}` inside `G` (P123.038 §1).

**[phase 132 — what row C actually used, replacing the stale round-129 text
below.]**  Paper 45 (phase 132) uses from this paper: Lemma 3.3 (the carrier);
Def. 8.6 and (8.5), (8.5∞), Rem. 8.6a, (8.5b); Thm. 8.8; **Def. 8A.7d, whose
constant `c := 0` is now *derived* — Paper 45 Cor. 9.6a**; Thm. 8A.7c as its
standing chart convention (C1), including the window (e); Thm. 9.1; Def. 9.2;
Thm. 9.7, 9.7b; Thm. 9.7(d)'s Plancherel form **on all of `D_A`**, which is
what lets Paper 45 Lem. 5.1a drop every support hypothesis; Thm. 9.8;
Thms. 9.13(a), 9.14, 9.14a–c′ and Def. 9.14c0 for the rulings; **Thm. 10.8b
only as the structure constant of `β_T`** (Paper 45 Thm. 9bis.4(ii),
`CLASSICAL`, quarantined, with a deletion test — it is used for nothing else);
and **Thm. 14.1 as Paper 45's Warning 8.5**, which deletes the Hodge-index
reading the old Paper 45 offered.  Paper 45 **declines** Thm. 8A.7b (no
determinant of the theory is used), Thm. 8.10 / Rem. 8A.7e's `c_{cut}` (it
works with `c := 0` throughout), and `𝓝_∞^W = 0` (§13.8).  *The three items
named in the round-129 paragraph below are superseded: `c` is no longer "the
open constant of Rem. 8.6b", `Γ_n = E_{a_S(n)}` belongs to the withdrawn
carrier, and Paper 45 §12's `G_rad` is withdrawn (Paper 45 §13 W45.8) in
favour of `D_A^{rad}`.*

Row B is [Paper 44](../44-row-b-arithmetic-family/main.md); row C is
[Paper 45](../45-row-c-explicit-formula/main.md).  Paper 45 §12 records that
its intersection form `I_A` lives on the **radial** sector — sections whose
coefficient depends only on the modulus — while `T` above is a functional on
all of `D_A` and separates sections of equal radial push-forward
(Theorem 9.5).  Those statements are unaffected by this rebuild.
**[REBUILT, phase 129, round 3:]** round 2 told Paper 45 that it "may not cite
Theorem 9.1's ruling plane in `N_A`".  That restriction is **lifted**:
Theorem 9.8 makes `N_A = D_A`, and Theorem 9.12 attaches the hyperbolic `B_T`
to it canonically, as a boundary quotient.  Paper 45 may cite Theorem 9.1's
external Tate characters, Theorem 9.8, and the exact sequence (9.17) — with
the one caveat that `B_T` is a quotient, not a subobject (Theorem 9.13), so a
statement of the form "the ruling classes lie in `N_A`" must be phrased
through `M = (λ_−,λ_+)`.  Rows B and C are not otherwise touched here, and
this paper does not use anything from them.  **[phase 132]** (The phase-132
records above — the `[phase 132]` block, the obstacle rows for `𝓝_∞^W` and
`c_{cut}`, and Def. 8A.7d reason (iv) — cite Paper 45, but **no clause of A
rests on any of them**: they are records of what row C used, declined or
characterized.)

### 14.2 Proposed specification amendment, for the author's decision

**[REBUILT, phase 129, round 4; DECIDED 2026-09-12.  The amendment below
was accepted by the repository author and is now the text of clause A.6 in
`P123_A_ACCEPTANCE_SPECIFICATION.md`.  Under the amended clause, A.6 is
PROVED by Theorems 9.8, 9.11, 9.12, 9.13 and 9.14 of this paper.  The
historical discussion is kept as written.]**

Clause A.6 currently reads, in part:

> its Hausdorff quotient `N_A` contains the ruling plane and a nonzero
> completed mixed sector.

Theorem 9.13(a) proves that on the carrier of this paper the word "contains",
read as "has a subobject isomorphic to", **cannot** be satisfied: a submodule
mapping isomorphically to `B_T` would contain `Φ_∓ ≠ 0` with
`a⋆Φ_∓ = λ_∓(a)Φ_∓` for every `a`, i.e. `â(χ)=λ_∓(a)=â(∓i/2)` on the support
of `Φ̂_∓` — a value of `â` off the unitary axis forced to equal one on it,
which is impossible.  Theorem 9.12 proves that `B_T` **is** canonically
attached to `N_A`, as the quotient by the primitive sector, with the
hyperbolic form and the `†`-exchange.

Proposed replacement text for that clause fragment:

> its Hausdorff quotient `N_A` carries a canonical exact sequence
> `0 → N_A^{prim} → N_A → B_T → 0` of topological modules, in which `B_T` is
> the two-dimensional Tate boundary with the hyperbolic form `[[0,1],[1,0]]`
> and with `†` exchanging its two directions, and in which `N_A^{prim}` is
> nonzero and carries a nondegenerate induced pairing.  No splitting of this
> sequence is required.

Rationale, for the record: this is P123.017 §4's own formulation, that note's
status line is `PROVED-IN-REPOSITORY` for "the exact Tate-boundary/primitive
sequence", and its §8 already claims A.6 closed "in the appropriate
correspondence-module sense".  The amendment would make the clause say what
the repository proves rather than what a finite-dimensional analogy suggests.
**If the amendment is rejected, A.6 stands `REFUTED` on this carrier and a
different carrier would be needed; that is a legitimate outcome and I do not
prejudge it.**

> **Status,  (phase 130).**  **[REBUILT, phase 130.]**  The
> phase-129 loop closed A.6 and left A.5, A.7 and A.8 open, with A.8 called
> the binding constraint.  Phase 130 closes all of them, and the closure rests
> on three theorems that did not exist before.  (i) **Theorem 9.14a–c:** the
> two rulings *do* live in the theory — not in the dense test sector, where
> Theorem 9.13(a) forbids them, but in its completion, as explicit limits of
> spread Tate characters; the completion then splits canonically
> (Theorem 9.14b), and P123.007 §4's unspecified boundary radical is computed:
> it *is* the ruling plane (Theorem 9.14c).  (ii) **Theorem 9.7b:** every local
> symbol is a Mittag-Leffler sum over the Tate points with the universal
> residue `∓i`, periodized with period `2π/\log p` at a finite place; the
> scalar symbol of a finite chart therefore carries `|S_f|+1` copies of the
> Tate pole, which is why no scalar form can be the ruling form
> (Theorem 10.8).  (iii) **Theorem 8A.7b:** the determinant of the theory lives
> on a Banach `†`-algebra `𝓐_tr` built from the symbol, on which the germ is
> holomorphic and exactly multiplicative for all shells and all places at
> once; the obstacle (O-A5-1b) was an obstacle for a *different* determinant.
> Consequently `λ_{RR}` is vector-valued, into `T_A = Pic_b(B_T)`, and it
> extends the intrinsic periodic ruling determinant exactly (Theorem 10.7(e)),
> with Weil's `D·D' = P·P' + d_1d_2' + d_2d_1'` as the equality formula
> (10.10).  Round 4's requirement "(A.8-I)" is withdrawn as unsatisfiable
> (Theorem 10.6a): no functor through the Tate fibre can carry `Tr_A`, and
> none does in Weil's theory either.  Two things are **not** claimed:
> `𝓝_∞ = 0` for the colimit (§13.8, `CLASSICAL`, **declined by row C and
> claimed by no row** — **[phase 133, PANEL_2 MINOR-2: was "row C"]**), and any
> Hodge-index sign (Theorem 14.1).
>
> **Status,  (phase 129, round 4).**  The round-3 audit
> (`R129.006`) verified Theorem 9.8 step by step and confirmed (T1)–(T6).
> Round 4 makes the paper exact rather than adding results.  Four changes are
> substantive.  (i) `T` is now **defined** by (8.4) from the two proved local
> formulas P123.042 (3.2) and P123.035 (3.1) plus a declared constant, so
> **`𝓝 = 0` is import-free**; the semilocal cutoff theorem becomes the
> comparison Theorem 8.10 and is used nowhere else except Theorem 8A.7.
> (ii) The archimedean measure is corrected by the factor `½` that P123.035
> §3's folding carries (Remark 8.6a), and `Ŵ_∞` is re-derived with every
> constant in place.  (iii) Theorem 9.13 is strengthened: **no `D_A`-module
> splitting of the Tate boundary exists at all**, which settles the A.6
> reading question against the literal wording and turns it into the
> specification decision of §14.2.  (iv) Theorem 14.1 records the shape of the
> numerical form — indefinite, infinite index on both sides, no orthogonal
> complement, signature hostage to the unpinned constant — so that rows B, C
> and D cannot inherit a false expectation of a Hodge-index statement.
>
> A.8 remains open and is the binding constraint.  Proposition 10.6 is the
> one new negative: the "determinant of the contact complex" route to
> (A.8-I) is closed off by this paper's own Corollary 7.5.
